# OdyTTY Devlog - October 2026

[Devlog index](../DEVLOG.md) | [Current work](../TODO.md) | [Specification](../SPEC.md)

Entries are listed in reverse-chronological order.

---

## 2026-10-13 -- Emoji layout names stay distinct, profile values keep their spaces, and layout saves report a closed workspace

Saved-layout names that contain non-ASCII visible text compare by their exact
characters, so an emoji ZWJ family and its separate member emoji are two
layouts. ASCII-only names still ignore ZWJ, ZWNJ, VS15 and VS16 when checking
for a clash. A save whose workspace closes while the replace dialog or name
prompt is open reports the refused save, and Delete Layout refuses a row whose
file identity was never captured, with a notice, instead of reporting it gone.

The profile editor keeps leading and trailing spaces in environment values
through save. Theme suggestions keep the built-in themes and examine at most
1,024 directory entries; a typed theme name still resolves beyond that list.
The quick-terminal edge and animation rows offer exactly their valid choices.
The docs state that a layouts folder over 1,024 entries refuses every save.

The full test suite ran once on this group of five commits.

## 2026-10-13 -- SVG colour glyphs are refused only for real filter, mask or marker declarations, and combining marks follow Unicode 17.0.0

SVG colour glyphs whose style text only mentioned `filter`, `mask` or
`marker` (in a class name, a font name or a comment) were refused and drawn
in monochrome. Style attributes and `style` elements are now read with
`simplecss`, the CSS parser `usvg` applies to the same text, pinned to the
version already in the build. A declaration whose property starts with
`filter`, `mask` or `marker`, in any letter case, is still refused, as is any
`url(` in style text; element, attribute, size, node, nesting, reference and
raster limits are unchanged. `simplecss` (Apache-2.0 OR MIT) becomes a direct
dependency; it was already linked through `usvg`.

The glyph atlas's combining-mark table is generated from the Unicode 17.0.0
General_Category data (Mn and Me, less variation selectors) by
`scripts/unicode-mark-data.py`. It drops fourteen spacing marks and 37
unassigned code points the hand-written table listed and adds 34 newer marks,
so those marks land over their base and spacing marks may use fallback fonts.

An automation reply whose object count is within the limit but larger than
the bytes that follow is refused as truncated rather than as oversized. The
memory report log tracks how much of each write reached the file, so a torn
line is ended before the next sample and a complete header is not repeated.
On Unix a failed parent-directory sync after an atomic write is logged at
debug level; Windows cannot sync a directory and stays silent.

The full test suite ran once on this group of five commits.

## 2026-10-13 -- Bash keeps $_ with shell integration, forced PTY end-of-file keeps readable output, and rail autohide warnings name their variable

With bash shell integration loaded, `echo foo; echo $_` printed the name of
the integration's DEBUG hook instead of `foo`, and a user DEBUG hook saw the
wrong last argument. The integration now preserves the preceding command's
last argument (`$_`) and status, with or without an existing DEBUG hook,
including with `functrace` and `extdebug`. Zsh, fish and PowerShell use
separate hooks and are unchanged.

When a PTY reader is forced to end of file (closing a pane whose descendant
keeps the terminal open, or the Unix `--interactive` frontend after the child
exits), it first reads up to 256 KiB of output that is already readable, so
bytes still in the kernel buffer are kept within a fixed bound. Wake requests
are nonblocking and the interactive frontend retries them; an unexpected
reader disconnect presents the final screen. Windows uses ConPTY and does not
offer the interactive frontend.

The workspace rail autohide warning names the canonical or legacy variable
that supplied the invalid value.

The full test suite ran once on this group of five commits.

## 2026-10-13 -- ambiguous_width = wide applies from the first window, and a combining mark after DEL joins its base

`ambiguous_width = wide` (and `ODYTTY_AMBIGUOUS_WIDTH=wide`) was ignored for
the launch session and for new tabs, split panes, new workspaces, attached
sessions and SSH connections until a configuration reload or a workspace
restore re-measured every session. The policy is now set when each session is
created: the global value, or the profile-merged value on a profile launch.
Restored workspaces and opened layouts were already re-measured. Greek and
Cyrillic letters stay one cell with `wide` on; docs/features.md states it.

DEL is dropped in the ground state without ending the cluster in progress, so
a combining mark that follows it joins the preceding base, as in xterm.

The session navigator's `Authorization` preview redaction continues through a
quoted value that contains a space and past a comma that stands apart, so a
Digest or AWS4 nonce and response after a quoted username are hidden. Two GPU
errors that alternate every frame are throttled like a single repeated error
(the eight most recent distinct messages are tracked). The
THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES header states the objc2 holder-file year accurately.

PTY tests that join an output pump after killing the child are bounded and
fail naming the stage that stalled instead of running until the CI attempt
timeout. The application's own close path was already bounded.

The full test suite ran once on this group of five commits: formatting,
pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite single-threaded and
at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- The Unix interactive frontend shows a program's final output after it exits

The `--interactive` diagnostic frontend on Linux and macOS could end as soon
as the child exited while captured output was still queued, so the last part
of a program's output could be missing from the final screen. Child exit now
completes only after the queued output has drained in order. After a 250 ms
grace the reader is asked for end-of-file once, so a background process that
keeps the terminal open cannot hold the frontend forever, and output already
captured is still shown. Input and resize keep being serviced while it
drains. Windows does not offer the interactive frontend.

Tests: the profile catalog tests keep their parse counter and one-entry cache
per test thread, so concurrent catalog loads in other tests can no longer
change an exact-count assertion; the production cache is unchanged. The
fixture that supplies a guaranteed-absent program no longer falls back to a
fixed path in the filesystem root: it uses the first whitespace-free folder
it can create and own, or a uniquely named folder verified missing, so a test
that expects a failed open can never start a real program.

The full test suite ran once on this change together with the previous
entry: formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite
single-threaded and at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- Comment and test accuracy: shared gamut conversion, shared handle duplication and self-cleaning integration scratch folders

Source comments describe current font discovery, colour failure caching,
allocated memory, theme floors, background composition, platform behaviour
and settings state without implementation history. Named tests assert what
they claim: separate queue and rate limits, profile precedence, unique theme
names, unity contrast validation, OSC bounds and parser recovery.
Complex-script owner fixtures also move within right-to-left isolates, and
Bengali's known shaping difference is documented at the glyph-position level.

Palette generation, colour-vision adaptation and theme authoring share one
gamut conversion with the same bisection and byte output. Windows PTY and
process handle duplication share one helper, Unix descriptor conversions
share their ownership rules, the Windows named-pipe prefix is one literal,
and integration tests share a self-cleaning scratch folder helper. Runtime
configuration and transport behaviour are unchanged.

The full test suite ran once on this change together with the next entry's
fixes: formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite
single-threaded and at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- DEL no longer prints a cell, the replace-tab prompt keeps its question mark, and a split tab has no phantom scroll thumb

A DEL byte (`0x7F`) arriving as ordinary text no longer writes a cell,
advances the cursor, triggers a pending wrap or becomes the character a later
`REP` repeats. xterm and the VT100 drop it in Ground; OdyTTY printed it as a
one-column cell. It now reaches the screen as an ignored control, which ends
any open grapheme cluster like every other ignored control. DEL inside an
escape or control sequence was already ignored.

The replace-tab confirmation measures the host alias in terminal cells. A
dialog body at least 45 cells wide keeps the shortening ellipsis and the
closing question mark even when no alias text fits, wide characters stay
whole, and a combining alias that fits is shown unshortened. Narrower bodies
keep a cell-fitted prefix.

The scroll thumb is drawn only on a single-pane tab, but its hit test
measured the window grid on every tab. In a split tab with the focused pane
scrolled back, the right edge reported a grabbable thumb that is not drawn,
and that phantom thumb took the edge from a right-hand workspace rail, so the
rail's resize seam could not be grabbed there. The hit test now returns
nothing on a split tab.

Every pixel offset in the glyph rasterizer, including the subpixel filter,
now widens to `usize` before multiplying, like the atlas byte length. The
192 MiB atlas budget keeps current atlases far below the 32-bit limit, so
rendering is unchanged.

Release and test tooling: every manifest version read (AUR publish, dev
build, CI and the AppImage script) goes through one helper that reads only
the `[package]` table. With `--release-version` at the published version, the
documentation guard also requires the badged release devlog heading and a
`Shipped` roadmap checkpoint. The memory baseline table refuses a field with
both a geometry-independent and a per-geometry ceiling, benchmark probe
records redact roots only on path boundaries, the vttest signature check is
tested through both verifier branches, and the release-notes tests cover a
symlinked notes file and the size limit. The test that keeps the inherited
home and profile folders untouched now checks after each child test and names
the test, variable and entries it finds. Six settings fixtures own their
scratch folders, and five more test files encode base64 with the production
encoder.

The full test suite ran once on this change together with the previous
entry: formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite
single-threaded and at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- Comment, test and helper accuracy: shared overlay helpers, one drag threshold and test-only diagnostics

A further pass over comments, tests and shared helpers. Behaviour is
unchanged except where noted:

- Top-tab and rail-workspace reorder gestures arm through one shared distance
  test, pinned at the exact threshold.
- The profile auto-switch recency rule (a profile applied in one pane never
  counts as recent in another pane, host or directory) is one function used by
  the application and driven directly by its test.
- The clipboard-write consent prompt builds its request line once for both the
  overlay signature and the painted cells.
- A background pane of a split tab is covered for its synchronized-output
  hold: its release deadline is part of the event-loop wake set.
- Scalar text truncation, snapshot read classification, settings preparation
  and dialog adapters each have one implementation; scratch socket folders,
  the per-platform configuration folder and the Wayland seat and device
  release each use one shared helper.
- SPEC.md and docs/features.md state how the window-merge picker behaves:
  focus leaving the whole window cancels it, its keys are consumed (repeats,
  releases, multi-character text and keys for closed windows), and a notice
  reports input that was not delivered.

Unused code is removed (three JSON accessors, an always-true profile check,
two unreachable profile aliases, a duplicate missing-socket check and unused
restore wrappers), the profile catalog parse counter is test-only, three
grid tests that could skip silently now require their atlas, and comments
state current contracts without tracking labels or change history.

The full test suite ran once on this change together with the next entry's
fixes: formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite
single-threaded and at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- The Unix interactive fallback stays responsive under floods, and the key-remap list holds still while a chord is captured

The diagnostic `--interactive` frontend on Linux and macOS now reads the
shell and the keyboard through bounded queues and handles at most a fixed
amount of shell output before it checks input and resizes again, so a
program that floods output no longer starves typing or window resizes. A
paste from the host terminal larger than 8 MiB is refused as a whole and
logged rather than buffered without limit, and a failed PTY resize keeps the
previous size and is retried instead of ending the session. Windows has no
interactive fallback.

In the key-remap overlay, the mouse wheel no longer moves the highlighted row
while a chord is being captured, while a conflict is being confirmed or while
the unsaved-changes prompt is open, so the next key always goes to the action
that is shown. Browsing the list with the wheel is unchanged.

The same full test run as the two previous entries covered this change.

## 2026-10-13 -- SSH sign-in checks read the whole error output, the tarball installer refuses merged-/usr aliases, layout names cannot collide invisibly, and the Browse chip takes the click it shows

The connection form's sign-in check now reads all of the SSH client's error
output instead of only the first 8 KiB. It keeps the host-key warnings, any
denial and the last list of offered sign-in methods as it reads, so a long
transcript whose final answer is a rejection is reported as a rejection
rather than as a password prompt.

The tarball installer checks every directory it writes, not only the prefix.
On systems where `/bin` links to `/usr/bin`, `PREFIX=/` is now refused, as is
any binary, data, icon or font licence folder that resolves under `/usr`
(except `/usr/local`), for both install and uninstall, before any file
changes.

Saving a layout named like an older layout whose file name is reserved on
Windows (`COM0`, `LPT0`) shows the normal replace prompt, and confirming
leaves one entry. Layout names that differ only by zero-width joiners or
emoji variation selectors count as the same name, so two identical-looking
layouts can no longer exist side by side; the saved name keeps its
characters.

Clicking the connection form's Browse chip for the key file uses the chip
as it was last drawn, so a click on its first cell opens the browser and the
cell just after it no longer does, even when the field was not focused.

On Windows, checking whether a shell has exited rereads the exit code once
the process has finished, so a shell that exits during the check reports its
real code. The Windows test for a genuine exit code of 259 now runs an
ordinary child process instead of a console shell.

Formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, the full test suite
single-threaded and at four threads, five more four-thread runs, the file
size and documentation guards, the dependency audit and the tooling
self-tests ran once on this change together with the previous entry.

## 2026-10-13 -- Comment, test and tooling accuracy across the codebase: quieter repeated GPU errors, safer header redaction and worktree-aware builds

A pass over comments, tests and tooling in every area of the codebase brings
a few small behaviour changes with it:

- A GPU validation error that repeats every frame is logged when it first
  occurs and then only at power-of-two repeat counts, with the count, instead
  of on every frame. The shared presentation device uses the same handler.
- The session navigator preview redacts an `Authorization:` header with a
  parameter list (`Digest`, `AWS4`) through its last parameter.
- Top-strip and workspace-rail labels drop bidi and zero-width format
  characters as overlay rows already do, keeping joiners.
- The inline image viewer captions a Windows path by its file name.
- A failed hovered-path recheck restores the normal pointer, focus loss
  forgets keys taken by local UI, and a refused workspace close is no longer
  recorded as closed.
- An upload staging name that already exists is reported as a collision.
- Building from a linked git worktree or a submodule watches that checkout's
  own `HEAD`, index and shared refs.
- Status and notice text from the SSH connect path uses ASCII punctuation.

Tooling: the benchmark child oracle compares against the canonical fixture
digests; DRM memory readings are `unsupported` when the descriptor bound or
an unknown unit makes them incomplete; non-finite or inexact benchmark numbers
and placeholder pins are refused; the vttest runner treats a mismatched
signature as fatal, exits 2 for a harness fault and redacts more profile path
forms; the memory capture folds unrecognised mapped files into one entry; the
demo script keeps its files in one private temporary folder; the
documentation guard checks the release-candidate marker, both lockfiles and the
newest AppStream entry against a published version; the CI waiter retries a
whitespace-only response; Miri runs are classified unsupported only after a
failing run; and version derivation reads only the `[package]` table.

Tests that could pass without checking anything now assert: Kitty deletes check
the surviving placement, smart Ctrl+C checks the clipboard and the exact PTY
bytes, file-drop tests use the production limits and exact notice, font-load
skips that could never fire are expectations, and a button-table test checks
that a newer entry keeps its key. Unused code is removed (font version and
asset constants, an unused font-family list, an unreachable font fallback,
an identity tab-bar placement helper, duplicated bloom bind-group helpers, a
no-op visual setter and an unreachable parser branch), seven copies of the
0.18 light-background threshold become one constant, and test-only helpers are
gated to tests. Comments throughout state current contracts, without tracking
labels, rebuild history or em-dashes.

The full test suite ran once on this change together with the next entry's
fixes: formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite
single-threaded and at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- Dead-key text reaches Windows input mode, config saves through 40 links, SSH key and config scans stay complete, and loading animations park on their last shown frame

On Windows, when a program has turned on Win32 input mode, a key press after
a dead key that cannot combine with it (an accent followed by a consonant, for
example) now sends both characters. The key records carry the text the system
produced rather than only the key's own character. Named keys, Ctrl chords and
key releases are encoded as before.

Saving the config through a chain of symbolic links now follows up to 40
links, as documented, and updates the final target. A longer chain, a loop or
a link that cannot be read is refused before anything is written, so no link
is replaced by a plain file. A link to a missing file still creates that file
on the first save.

The SSH key browser counts a `~/.ssh` folder entry it cannot read toward its
scan limit and shows the "key list is incomplete" notice, while still listing
the keys it found. Importing hosts from an SSH config that reaches the
1024-host limit still merges later `Host` blocks into hosts already listed;
only new names are refused, and the first value of each setting still wins.

A loading Kitty animation whose newest frames have no display time yet now
stops on the last frame it can show even when one late timer tick covers
several frames, matching what separate ticks do, so the not-yet-shown frames
never appear.

`THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` names the copyright holder of the objc2 crate family
from the upstream licence files, and a checked-in table records where each
such holder comes from. Tests that need a program that cannot exist now use a
path in a folder they own, so a program on the machine's `PATH` cannot make
them pass by accident. The CI checks also confirm that temporary `ci/**`
branches run the full matrix.

Formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, the full test suite
single-threaded and at four threads, five more four-thread runs, the file
size and documentation guards, the dependency audit and the tooling self-tests
ran once on this change together with the previous entry.

## 2026-10-13 -- Comment and test accuracy: numbered reconnect failures, shared scratch folders and shorter session notes

A reconnect that fails again now says which attempt failed: the first failure
still reads `reconnect failed`, and each later one reads
`reconnect failed (attempt N)`, so repeated Enter presses visibly produce new
attempts. The count resets after a successful reconnect.

Comments that a later read found too long or inaccurate are shortened or
corrected in the session lifecycle, hover-path memo, tab-activation scroll
reconciliation, GPU frame and pipeline notes, copy mode and selection. The SSH
config host entry documents that `HostName`, `User` and `Port` take the first
value across all concrete `Host` blocks naming the alias, and the copy-mode
notes say trailing ASCII spaces are trimmed whether or not a program wrote
them. Several test modules that built their own temporary folders now use the
shared scratch-folder helpers, which remove the folders afterwards.

The full test suite ran once on this change together with the next entry's
fixes: formatting, pinned Linux, Windows and macOS lints, and the test suite
single-threaded and at four threads all pass with no failures.

## 2026-10-13 -- Windows refuses batch-file editors, the tarball installer refuses /usr, older layouts stay usable, notification helpers time out, and a held X11 quick terminal shortcut summons once

On Windows, an editor or opener that is a batch file (`.cmd` or `.bat`) is no
longer started when it has a file-name argument. Windows runs a batch file
through `cmd.exe`, so the file name taken from terminal output reached
`cmd.exe`, whose metacharacter parsing no argument quoting can make safe. The
check is made on the resolved program, ignores letter case and the trailing dots
and spaces Windows drops, and the existing failure notice names the reason.
Direct executables and the default Explorer open are unchanged, and other
platforms are unaffected. The Store app execution alias test now selects
entries by their reparse tag and checks both program lookups, and the Windows
test stand-ins resolve their helpers to absolute files.

The Linux tarball installer now refuses, before installing or removing any
file, a prefix that resolves to `/usr`, including dot components, trailing
slashes and symlink aliases. That prefix belongs to the deb, rpm and AUR
packages, and there is no override; the tarball keeps a per-user prefix or
`/usr/local`. The check uses GNU `realpath`. Windows and macOS installs are
unchanged.

Saved layouts named `COM0` or `LPT0` before port names were mangled stay
listed, openable and deletable under their original names, while new saves
still get the underscore prefix. Layout names keep ZWJ, ZWNJ and the VS15 and
VS16 variation selectors after visible text. Layout preparation no longer
scans and repairs every sibling file, so one stray JSON directory or symlink
cannot disable the others; load, save and delete check their own file.

Desktop notification helpers get ten seconds, then are killed and reaped, and
their failures produce a debug diagnostic without terminal text. On Windows
each of the four notification kinds now has its own fixed wording and loads
the WinRT types it uses explicitly; terminal text still never enters the
script.

On X11, holding the quick terminal shortcut toggled the window again on every
auto-repeat. It now summons once per physical press, as on Windows and macOS,
and the grab thread waits on the X connection instead of waking every 30 ms.
On macOS the hot-key handler answers only OdyTTY's own hot key (signature
`odty`) and passes any other on. `quick_terminal_shortcut` can name the minus
key as `minus` or a final `-` (`ctrl+-`) and the equals key as `equal` or `=`;
`+` and `-` remain interchangeable separators.

Sessions, PTY pumps, attach pumps and background workers now wake the window
through a small handle that wraps winit's event-loop proxy. Test builds can
hold a discarding handle instead, so the 58 session-spawning tests that were
ignored on macOS, because winit cannot build an event loop off the main thread
there, now run on macOS. `ODYTTY_TEST_DISCARD_WAKER` selects the same handle on
Linux and Windows. The real-process detach and reattach test re-runs its own
executable as the session host, so it no longer needs a built `odytty` binary.

Validation for the group, run once on the combined tree: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, the touched test groups five more times
at four threads, the production and documentation guards, and the RustSec
audit passed. Each behaviour change has a test that fails before it.
Notification delivery, the macOS hot-key handler and a physical X11 key are
not exercised by these tests and are left to hands-on checks.

## 2026-10-13 -- Comment, documentation and test accuracy: dismissed dialogs keep nothing, clearer drop and replace prompts, and headless tests that cannot skip

Comments, documentation and tests that a later read found inaccurate or weak
are corrected across the terminal core, graphics, the native app, settings,
packaging notes and the tooling. A few small behaviour changes come with them.
Closing an overlay now drops every pending dialog payload, so a dismissed
confirmation keeps no session id, host, layout name or working directory. The
file-drop refusal names both limits: at most 128 files and 256 KiB of paths,
and at most 1024 KiB once quoted for the shell. The replace-tab confirmation
shortens a long host alias with an ellipsis so its question mark stays
visible. The GPU memory report no longer counts the viewport uniform as a
vertex buffer, a window-icon failure is logged instead of printed, and the
memory capture tool gives every unmeasured Linux field its own reason.

Headless App test builders that can never fail now return the App directly,
and more than 100 unreachable "no PTY, skipping" exits are removed, so those tests
cannot pass without running. The scrollbar and shell-integration tests assert
that their fixtures created scrollback instead of returning early. Module-wide
dead-code allowances in the persistence and window-merge code are replaced by
test-only items and narrow, stated allowances, an unused palette alias table
and an unused layout path helper are removed, and several tests are renamed to
what they assert or gain positive controls.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
the full suite with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the
touched test groups five more times at four threads, the production and
documentation guards, and the RustSec audit passed, with the full suite run
again on the combined tree.

## 2026-10-12 -- Reconnected panes get their pixel size, window moves never panic, quoted config paths keep their backslashes, and a zero SSH persist window means off

The entries below landed together. A reconnected remote pane gets its pixel
size at once and says when the reconnect fails. Window merges and tab or pane
moves refuse a broken source instead of panicking halfway. Theme names refuse
Windows device names. Quoted `odytty.conf` values keep backslashes such as
Windows paths, and a zero SSH `Persist` value turns connection sharing off
instead of keeping a master forever. On Windows, a shell that fails during its
own start shows its reason in the pane. Saved-layout versions are judged
before rounding, and the versioned user install creates its launcher
directory.

Validation for the group, run once on the combined tree: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, five more four-thread runs of the whole
suite, the production and documentation guards, the Python tooling self-tests,
the fuzz lockfile check and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- Reconnected panes get their pixel size, failed reconnects say so, window moves never panic mid-transfer, and theme names refuse Windows device names

A reconnected remote pane now sends its cell pixel size to the new PTY as soon
as it starts. The terminal model already held that size, so no later layout
pass saw a change, and the fresh shell read the PTY layer's 8x16 default until
an unrelated resize. A failed pixel-size send is retried like any failed
resize. When the connection command cannot be started again, the pane now
shows `reconnect failed` and keeps the prompt; before, Enter appeared to do
nothing.

Window merge and tab or pane moves take their sessions out of the source in
one step that puts every session back on a miss, before any tree changes. The
merge used to panic if a validated session was missing at commit, after
earlier sessions had already moved. A tab whose tree names one session twice
used to leave its workspace and then panic on the second removal; it is now
refused with the source unchanged.

Theme Builder save names are now limited to 64 characters, and Windows device
names such as `con`, `nul` or `lpt1` are refused on every platform, as saved
layout and profile names already were. Name entry stops at the limit, color
and seed entry stop at `#rrggbb`, and a suggested name from a long theme name
is shortened to fit.

The reconnect tests now also run on Windows, through `cmd.exe /C exit N` stub
children started after the window's first grid. Each test waits for the
child's output to end and then runs the exit decision once, as the app does:
`arm_reconnect_declines_a_clean_exit` no longer reaps the child first,
`a_local_shell_never_arms_reconnect` now uses a real local child that exits
255 instead of the headless startup pane, and the two restored remote upload
tests and `ssh_session_stores_reconnect_anchor_but_a_local_shell_does_not`
lose their Unix-only gate. `level1_enter_drills_and_level2_esc_backs_out`
compares the Fonts rows with the catalog's Font group instead of a fixed key
list.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
the session, Theme Builder and Settings panel tests one at a time and five
times four at a time, with and without a display, the production and
documentation guards and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- Quoted config values keep their backslashes, and a zero ControlPersist window means off

Inside double quotes an `odytty.conf` value decoded every backslash as an
escape, so a hand-written `"C:\Users\me\theme.conf"` read back as
`C:Usersmethemeconf` without a warning. Only `\\` and `\"` are escapes now, which
is exactly what the writer produces, so saved values still round-trip and every
other backslash is kept as written. A leading byte order mark was already
removed by the shared capped reader; a test now pins that the first setting of
such a file loads.

A per-host `Persist` value that totals zero (`0s`, `0m`, `00`) was passed to
OpenSSH as `ControlPersist=0`, which OpenSSH reads as "persist indefinitely",
leaving a master process running for good. A zero total is now the off token,
as a bare `0` already was. A window longer than 30 days is clamped to 30 days
rather than handed to OpenSSH, which rejects values beyond its integer range and
fails the connection.

Validation: tests cover a quoted Windows path, a path with `#`, trailing
backslashes and the writer's own escapes, and the zero and oversized durations;
each fails when its change is reverted. Platform-neutral; Windows has no
ControlPersist at all.

## 2026-10-12 -- A failed shell start reaches the pane before the session ends, and the ConPTY probes are bounded

When a Windows shell died during its own start, the child waiter closed the
pseudoconsole before it recorded the one-line reason, so the output pump could
reach end-of-file first and leave the pane blank, and a blocked standard-error
write held the waiter (which session teardown joins). The line is now stored
for the pump first, the pseudoconsole is closed second, and the standard-error
copy is written last on its own short-lived thread; if that thread cannot start
the copy is skipped. The order lives in a small portable function so it is
tested on every platform.

The two ConPTY button probes read without a limit and waited without a
deadline, so a regression in end-of-file handling or a flood of output could
hang or grow the test. They now read through a worker thread with a 60 second
deadline and a 256 KiB cap, kill the child on either, and report only the tail
of the raw stream. The button probe also checks the admitted buttons rather
than only their count: the labelled button must match, cell for cell, the one
the same bytes produce in a second terminal, and the point button must have the
same code, scope and state.

Validation: the order is tested with recorded steps and fails when the close
runs first. The probe changes are Windows-only tests that run on the Windows CI
leg; the comparison logic was exercised off Windows against a stream with an
altered code, which keeps the same entry count but a different hit map.

## 2026-10-12 -- The versioned user install creates the launcher directory

The documented versioned user install linked the launcher into `~/.local/bin`
without creating the directory, so it failed on a fresh account while the
versioned binary's own directories were created by `install -D`. The block now
creates `~/.local/bin` first, and a test checks that every documented launcher
link into that directory is preceded by its creation.

## 2026-10-12 -- Fractional layout versions are refused before rounding

Saved-layout restore refuses a non-whole version even when floating-point
rounding would turn it into a supported version. It shares decimal-literal
classification with profile validation while retaining the layout reader's
existing compatibility behavior. Profile form fixtures assert distinct
portable environment errors, inject shell and theme suggestions, exercise the
128/129 JSON depth boundary, and own temporary files through panic-safe guards.

Validation: fmt, Linux/Windows/macOS Clippy, full serial and four-thread test
suites, five four-thread repeats of touched groups, production-file and
documentation guards, staged whitespace and RustSec for both lockfiles passed.
Every retained gate step exited zero. The rounded-version regression failed
before the correction; equivalent whole-version spellings passed before and
after.

## 2026-10-12 -- Comment and documentation accuracy: vttest capture causes, connect retries and test harness notes

Comments and documentation that a later read found inaccurate are corrected.
The vttest runner now says whether an unfinished capture came from a failed
read or from a process that kept the output open, and the compatibility notes
describe exactly which processes a timeout kill reaches and that the Windows
cleanup path runs only in self-tests. The automation connect documentation and
the feature notes describe the retry rule as it is: the first attempt always
runs, no retry starts after the deadline, and a success in flight is kept. Test
harness comments name the variables and directories they actually pin, the
Windows one-shot argument comment matches the fixed and default commands, and
a misplaced doc block in the protocol fuzz test is moved to its function.
Smaller layout fixes in SPEC.md, TODO.md, docs/features.md and the runtime
knobs notes. The only behaviour change is the vttest skip and fail wording.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
the full suite with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the
touched test groups and the vttest self-tests five more times at four threads,
the production and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- The tarball uninstaller keeps other packages' licence files, and benchmark and memory tooling refuse or explain bad input

The entries below landed together. Uninstalling the Linux tarball removes
only the files it installed, so a deb, rpm or AUR install sharing the prefix
keeps its licence directory. The W6 benchmark runner rolls back only the
directories it created, refuses out-of-range numbers by name, reports
context switches only from complete readings and leaves a retryable private
directory after an early refusal. Memory records give a reason for every
unmeasured field, and the demo repository ignores the user's git
configuration and hooks.

Validation for the group, run once on the combined tree: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, five more four-thread runs of the whole
suite, the production and documentation guards, the Python tooling self-tests,
the fuzz lockfile check and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- The tarball uninstaller no longer deletes a shared licence directory

`install.sh --uninstall` removed `PREFIX/share/licenses/odytty` recursively.
With `PREFIX=/usr` that directory can belong to a deb, rpm or AUR package, so
uninstalling the tarball install deleted another package's licence files. It now
removes only the files the tarball installs: the `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and
`THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` notices and the font licence files in the tarball's own
`licenses/fonts` tree, then the directories left empty. A file or directory it
did not install stays, and the uninstaller is run from the extracted tarball as
the installer is.

Validation: an installed prefix that already holds foreign files in the licence
directory, in its `fonts` directory and in a foreign font set directory keeps
them through install and uninstall, while every installed file and the binary go;
the test fails against the previous script. Linux only (the tarball ships for
Linux only); no Windows or macOS surface.

## 2026-10-12 -- W6 benchmark refusals before measurement leave a retryable private directory

The measured W6 command created its private evidence directory before the
session's remaining pre-flight checks, so a refusal such as a time budget
shorter than the frozen schedule left the directory behind and a retry with
the same paths was refused because it already existed. The checks that need
only the preregistration (its numbers, the frozen schedule and the time
budget) now run before the directory is created. A later pre-flight refusal
before the results target exists removes the directory when it holds no
files; if an availability probe already wrote logs into it, it is kept and
the message asks for a new private directory.

Validation: the benchmark runner, preregistration and result-schema self-tests,
the published W6 result validation, and the file-size, documentation and
link guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- W6 benchmark numbers beyond the float range are refused by name

A preregistration number such as an integer of 1 followed by 400 zeros is a
valid JSON number, passed the preregistration check, and then stopped the W6
runner with an `OverflowError` when it was converted. The runner now admits
its time budget and ceilings through one check that refuses booleans,
non-numbers, NaN and values beyond the float range with the field's own
message, before any probe or directory. The preregistration check applies the
same rule to its budgets and ceilings, and now refuses an
instrumentation-overhead ceiling above 100 percent, which the runner already
refused.

Validation: the benchmark runner, preregistration and result-schema self-tests,
the published W6 result validation, and the file-size, documentation and
link guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- W6 context switches are reported only from complete, matched readings

The context-switch diagnostic skipped a process whose threads could not be
listed and a thread whose status could not be read, so two partial readings
could still produce a number. Threads were matched by process and thread id
only, so a reused id compared equal, and a counter that went down was clamped
to zero. A reading is now all or nothing, each thread is identified by its id
and start time, and a decreasing counter makes the metric unsupported for
that replicate. The collector description now states the limit of comparing
two endpoints: a thread that starts and exits between them is not seen.

Validation: the benchmark runner, preregistration and result-schema self-tests,
the published W6 result validation, and the file-size, documentation and
link guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- Benchmark private directories are rolled back only when this run created them

A failed private-storage reservation removed every directory it had found
missing before it started, so a directory another process created in the
meantime could be removed. Each directory is now recorded only after this
run's own `mkdir` creates it, and a rollback removes only those, newest
first, and only while each is still the same empty directory.

Validation: the benchmark runner, preregistration and result-schema self-tests,
the published W6 result validation, and the file-size, documentation and
link guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- Memory records explain every unmeasured field, and the demo repository ignores user git configuration

`scripts/memory-capture.py` recorded `anonymous_bytes` as unmeasured on macOS and
Windows without a reason, and the Windows failure paths gave a reason for the
resident size only, leaving the peak and commit counters unexplained. Every
unmeasured process field now has its own entry in `unavailable`, and the self-test
checks each platform record, including the failure paths, for fields that lack one.

The demo script's fixture repository still ran a global or template `post-checkout`
hook on checkout and applied the user's global settings. Each fixture git command now
runs with the global and system configuration ignored, and the repository is created
without templates, so the per-command signing and hooks overrides are no longer
needed. The isolation does not reach the showcase session started afterwards. A
regression runs the fixture against a global hooks path and a template directory whose
hooks fail, with a global line-ending rule set, and checks the script still succeeds
and the settings do not reach the repository.

Platform behavior: Python and Bash tooling run on Linux CI; the memory capture's
macOS and Windows branches are exercised through injected readers, not on those systems.

## 2026-10-12 -- Settings file pickers show hidden files and stay responsive, Windows reserved names stay out of saved layouts, and Store app aliases still launch

The entries below landed together. The Settings file pickers list hidden
files, follow links the user picks and never wait on a slow folder. Saved
layout names keep non-ASCII text but not invisible format characters or
Windows device names. On Windows, a program available only as a Microsoft
Store app alias is still found. Profile numbers that round away keep their
literal, schema versions are judged by their digits, interrupted
compatibility runs stop their subject, and the third-party licence file
names more copyright holders.

Validation for the group, run once on the combined tree: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, five more four-thread runs of the whole
suite, the production and documentation guards, the Python tooling self-tests,
the fuzz lockfile check and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- Settings file pickers list hidden files, follow chosen links and never stall; saved layout names refuse invisible characters and Windows device names

The Settings path pickers now list hidden matching files and follow a link the
user selects to its target. Labels drop control and invisible format
characters, a listing cut at its limit shows an inert notice saying so, and a
short window keeps the keyboard selection visible while the wheel scrolls
freely. Probes and directory listings share four worker slots, and a worker
that times out stays counted until it exits, so a slow or hung folder cannot
pile up threads. Pressing Backspace in an empty profile launch field no longer
clears the other launch choice.

Saved layout names keep visible non-ASCII text within the 96-byte limit but
replace Unicode default-ignorable characters and line and paragraph separators
with underscores, so a name cannot hide or reorder text in a file listing.
Layouts and profiles now share one Windows device-name check, which also
covers the superscript ports `COM¹`-`COM³` and `LPT¹`-`LPT³` and `COM0`/`LPT0`;
such a layout name gets an underscore prefix instead of opening a device.

Validation: each new regression fails against the previous behavior, including
the hidden-file listing through the real App input path.

## 2026-10-12 -- A Store app execution alias still counts as a program on Windows

Program lookup on Windows (the default PowerShell search, bare names from
profiles and `--exec`, and the helper programs OdyTTY starts) decided that a
candidate exists with `Path::is_file`, which follows reparse points. A Microsoft
Store app execution alias under `WindowsApps` is a reparse point that
`CreateProcessW` launches but that cannot be opened as an ordinary file, and
whether the standard library reports it as a file could not be confirmed, so a
program available only as an alias risked being refused now that bare names are
never left for `CreateProcessW` to search. Lookup now accepts a candidate when
following it finds a file or when the entry itself is neither a directory nor a
plain link; a directory, a dangling link and a link to a directory are still
refused.

Validation: file, directory, missing name, link to a file, dangling link and
link to a directory are tested where links can be made. The alias case itself
cannot be built off Windows; a Windows test checks every real alias entry in the
account's `WindowsApps` directory when there are any and reports a skip
otherwise. No locally failing-before test exists for the alias branch.

## 2026-10-12 -- Profile numbers that round away keep their literal, and schema versions are judged by their digits

A short unknown-key number whose nearest 64-bit float is a different number
lost its value on save: `1e-400` rounds to zero, was held as `0` and written
back as `0`, and a short subnormal such as `1.234567e-322` lost digits the
same way. The strict profile reader now keeps the validated literal whenever
the float, written back in its shortest form, is not the same decimal number,
in addition to literals with more than 15 significant digits. Ordinary short
numbers, zero in any spelling and the smallest subnormal `5e-324` read back
unchanged and are still written in their shortest form.

`schema_version` values such as `1.00000000000000000001` and
`0.99999999999999999999` were accepted as version 1 because the whole-number
check looked at the rounded float. A retained literal is now judged from its
own digits, so both are refused as not a whole number, while whole spellings
such as `1e0` and `10e-1` are still version 1. `docs/profiles.md` says so.

Validation: `cargo fmt --check`; clippy on Linux and pinned 1.96 for Windows and
macOS; the profile tests once serially and five times at four threads with
and without a display; RustSec, file-size, documentation and link guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- Interrupted compatibility runs stop their subject, and licence notices name every holder they can

The vttest runner starts each case in its own session, so a Ctrl-C reached only
the runner and left the subject terminal and its window running. The bounded run
now kills the case's process group on any exception, including an interrupt, and
re-raises it; a simulated interrupt and a real timer-driven one are covered by
self-tests.

The licence generator now reproduces COPYRIGHT and AUTHORS files beside the
licence texts. For rustix and linux-raw-sys, whose MIT texts carry no copyright
line, the COPYRIGHT file is the only holder statement. A crate with no declared
authors and no licence file in its published source now says so in its entry and
names the repository that keeps its copyright statement, instead of leaving the
holder unstated. `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` is regenerated: 168 licence texts for the
same 276 linked crates.

Platform behavior: the runner and generator are Python tooling run by Linux CI;
the interrupt tests need POSIX signals and are skipped elsewhere.

## 2026-10-12 -- macOS CI test attempts get room for the grown suite, and the demo script passes shellcheck

A healthy single-threaded run of the full suite on the macOS CI runner now
takes 5:40 to 6:00, against a 6-minute limit per attempt, so three
consecutive commits timed out in a different test on each attempt. The
per-attempt limit is now 9 minutes with the deadlock retry unchanged; two
attempts still fit inside the step's 30-minute backstop and the job's
40-minute ceiling, and a workflow policy test keeps those budgets
consistent. The demo script clears git's repository variables in a loop
instead of an unquoted expansion, which shellcheck flagged.

Validation: shellcheck over every tracked shell script, actionlint over all
workflows, the workflow policy tests, and the documentation and
production-file guards passed. The macOS timing itself is confirmed only by
the macOS CI leg.

## 2026-10-12 -- Plain text speed back to its earlier level, Windows helper programs only run from absolute paths, and profile numbers keep a short form

The entries below landed together. Plain ASCII output skips the extension
checks that only composite text needs, which brings full repaint and plain
text feed back to about their level before the Unicode width work. On
Windows, the helper programs OdyTTY starts itself and the PowerShell paths
derived from environment variables resolve only to absolute paths. Profile
numbers are saved in a short exponent form when that is shorter, the
benchmark validator reports malformed samples instead of stopping, and test
fixtures that run git no longer write to a repository selected by the
caller's environment.

Validation for the group, run once on the combined tree: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, five more four-thread runs of the whole
suite, the production and documentation guards, the Python tooling self-tests,
the fuzz lockfile check and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- Plain ASCII skips extension checks after a plain owner

A plain ASCII scalar that follows an owner with no retained extension scalars
now takes its one-cell footprint directly instead of testing whether it extends
that owner. Composite owners, non-ASCII text, DEC charset translation and
boundary projection keep their existing behavior. A unit test checks that every
range in the four Indic property tables starts above ASCII, and the table
generator refuses ASCII members, so the earlier ASCII shortcut cannot be broken
by a table regeneration.

On the default model benchmark, three alternating runs on one Linux computer
reduced full repaint from 42.90 to 34.24 ms and plain ASCII feed from 6.82 to
5.88 ms, against about 33.9 and 6.0 ms before the width work. `docs/memory.md`
records the workload and the small repaint difference that remains.

## 2026-10-12 -- Windows helper programs and derived shell paths no longer resolve against the launch directory

The default-shell lookup built its PowerShell candidates from `%ProgramFiles%`
and `%SystemRoot%` without checking those values. An empty or relative value
joined to the PowerShell path gave a relative candidate, which exists when a
matching file sits under the launch directory, and a program with a directory
part is deliberately passed on as given. Both bases must now be drive-absolute
paths; an unusable `%ProgramFiles%` yields no PowerShell 7 candidate, and an
unusable `%SystemRoot%` is replaced by `C:\Windows`, as `%ComSpec%` handling
already did. The directories searched for a bare program name use the same
checked Windows directory.

The helper programs OdyTTY starts itself were still found by `std`, which
searches non-empty relative `PATH` entries, so a helper planted under one could
run before a later genuine one. The SSH probe, the SSH image upload and its
cleanup, the WSL distribution listing, the desktop notification PowerShell and
the file opener (including a bare editor name) now resolve a bare name to an
absolute file in the executable's directory, `System32`, the Windows directory
or an absolute `PATH` entry, and a name found nowhere is not started (the
existing failure notice or log line reports it). A configured program given with
a directory part, such as an editor path, is used as given. Linux and macOS
lookup is unchanged. The other process spawns are exempt: the session host
starts the absolute path of the running executable, the Unix PTY builder is
Unix-only, and the font, MIME and fontconfig helpers run on Unix only.

Validation: the root and lookup rules are pure functions tested on every
platform with empty, relative, rooted and drive-relative values, relative `PATH`
entries before an absolute one, and the real working directory; the Windows leg
adds tests for the PowerShell candidates and for the helper command on a real
system. A source scan fails if a helper site goes back to building a command from
a bare name. Each rule fails when its code is reverted. The Windows-only tests
run on the Windows CI leg.

## 2026-10-12 -- Benchmark validator refuses malformed samples without stopping

The benchmark result validator could stop with an exception instead of
reporting errors: a sample whose `block` was a JSON array or whose `attempt`
was an object raised a `TypeError` when the duplicate-identity check hashed
it, and a pass value such as a 401-digit integer raised an `OverflowError`
when the W6 summaries were recomputed. Sample identity and reason fields of
the wrong type and pass values that are not finite 64-bit floats are now
named errors, such samples are left out of the recomputed statistics and
execution reasons, and a list-valued metric in the top-level unsupported list
is reported as a mismatch. Any other field whose type or range the checks
cannot handle refuses the whole document with one error instead of raising.

Validation: the benchmark self-tests and the published W6 result validation;
`cargo fmt --check`, clippy, RustSec, file-size, documentation and link
guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- Profile numbers keep a short exponent form on save

The profile writer printed every number without an exponent, so an
unknown-key `1e300` was saved as a 301-digit integer and `1e-300` as a
302-character decimal; a profile holding a few thousand such values grew past
the 1 MiB file cap and its save was refused. Numbers are now written in their
shortest round-tripping form, in exponent notation when that is shorter, so
`1e300` stays `1e300`. Integers below 1e15 and short decimals are written as
before; an integer from 1e15 up is now written in exponent form, so the
existing test `short_numbers_are_written_as_before` expects `1e15` where it
expected sixteen digits. Numbers with more than 15 significant digits still
keep their validated literal. The saved-layout writer is unchanged: it only
writes indices and split ratios that it generated itself.

Validation: `cargo fmt --check`; clippy on Linux and pinned 1.96 for Windows and
macOS; the profile tests once serially and five times at four threads with
and without a display; RustSec, file-size, documentation and link guards.

## 2026-10-12 -- Test fixtures that run git no longer follow an inherited repository

The demo-repository fixture in `scripts/make-demo.sh` and the repository fixtures
inside the production-file guard and benchmark runner self-tests ran `git init`,
`git add`, `git commit` and `git remote` with whatever repository variables the
caller exported. A run from a hook or a linked worktree, which export `GIT_DIR`,
`GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and related variables, therefore wrote to that
outer repository instead of the scratch one. The demo script now clears git's own
list of repository-local variables before it builds the fixture, and the two
self-tests hide the same set while they run. The workflow policy tests build their
child environments without those variables and gain three regressions that run each
fixture with a decoy outer repository selected and assert that the decoy index,
history and remotes are untouched. Read-only provenance lookups (`build.rs`, the
benchmark preregistration and runtime identity checks, the coverage report) keep
following the caller's repository on purpose, and the two Rust tests that spawn git
already cleared these variables.

The tarball installer test now stages and checks `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` with the
other notices, and the third-party licence test requires the installer loop to name
it.

Platform behavior: the demo script and policy tests are Bash and Python tooling
that Linux CI runs; there is no Windows or macOS surface, and the tarball installer
test runs on Linux only, where the tarball ships.

## 2026-10-12 -- Plain text draws faster again, stuck remote upload helpers stay counted, Kitty error replies name their image, and every package carries the Rust crate licences

The entries below landed together. Plain text drawing, which the Unicode width
work had made roughly twice as slow, recovers most of that cost. Remote upload
and cleanup helpers that cannot be stopped keep counting against their limits.
Kitty graphics error replies carry the image id of the request they answer.
Saved-layout names keep non-ASCII text. Every package now ships
`THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` with the licence notices of the linked Rust crates, and
the tarball installer installs it with the other licence texts. Settings save
on network homes without file locking, and recalled shell history keeps an
escaped trailing space.

Validation for the group, run once on the combined tree: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, five more four-thread runs of the whole
suite, the production and documentation guards, the Python tooling self-tests
including the licence generator's offline check, the fuzz lockfile check and
the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- Plain text draws and reflows faster again after the Unicode width work

The grapheme and width work for Indic, Southeast Asian and emoji sequences made
the model benchmark's full repaint about 2.3 times slower, plain ASCII about 1.9
times and deep reflow about 2.5 times. A bisect over that range found the cost
in checks that plain text never needs: a cell with no extension scalars searched
the script and emoji sequence tables, every ASCII scalar looked up its Indic
property, and an emoji modifier searched the base table before checking the
next scalar. A cell without extensions now keeps its scalar width directly,
ASCII skips the Indic lookup, and the modifier check tests the next scalar
first. Cell ownership, wide-cell footprints, cursor, selection, copy and reflow
are unchanged.

On the default model benchmark, three alternating runs on one Linux computer
reduced full repaint from 82.02 to 42.20 ms, plain ASCII from 11.00 to 6.90 ms
and deep reflow from 44.75 to 19.95 ms. These remain above the figures before
the width work (about 35.7, 6.0 and 18.1 ms); `docs/memory.md` records the
workload, spread and the remaining cost, which is still being investigated.

## 2026-10-12 -- Remote upload helpers that cannot be stopped keep counting, and tab close never waits for a cleanup helper

A helper `ssh` reported as stuck (it could not be killed, or did not exit within
the grace period after the kill) was dropped when its worker returned, and the
worker released its slot at the same time. Repeated stuck helpers therefore
outlived their slots and exceeded the stated limits of two concurrent uploads
and eight concurrent cleanups, although the worker count stayed capped. Each
limiter now keeps such a helper, with its process handle, counted against its
cap until a later admission sees it exit; every admission asks the parked
helpers to die again and frees the ones that are gone. The parked list can never
be longer than the cap, because only a helper that held a slot is parked. The
cleanup watcher also logs a helper it could not stop instead of discarding the
result. Image paste and the cleanup spawner share the limiter, so both are
covered; a paste refused for this reason shows the existing "earlier uploads are
still running" notice.

When the thread that watches a cleanup helper cannot start, the failure used to
be handled by killing and then waiting up to two seconds for the helper on the
caller, which is the window thread when a remote tab closes. The helper is now
sent the kill once and checked once; one not yet gone is parked and reaped by a
later admission, so closing a tab never waits. A parked helper that no later
request ever collects stays a zombie until the process exits, bounded by the
cap.

Validation: stand-in helpers that survive every kill show that a stuck upload
helper and a stuck cleanup helper keep counting after their workers return and
are freed only after a later admission sees the exit, and that a watcher that
cannot start kills once, polls once and parks. Each rule fails when its code is
reverted. No real unkillable process was created. The behavior is shared by
Linux, macOS and Windows; only the kill and exit probe underneath differ, and
Windows spawns still go through the no-console-window helper.

## 2026-10-12 -- Kitty graphics error replies name their image, the vttest runner bounds every capture, and packages carry the Rust crate licences

A Kitty graphics error reply omitted the request's `i=`, `I=` and `p=` keys,
which OK replies already carried, so a client sending several images could not
tell which one failed. Error replies now carry the same keys; a failing
continuation chunk answers with the first chunk's keys, a request that named
none keeps the bare reply, and `q=2` still silences errors.
`docs/graphics.md` states the reply format.

The vttest runner starts each child in its own session so a timeout kill
reaches its descendants, bounds every wait, and marks a capture whose reader
outlives the child as incomplete. An incomplete replay case fails; an upstream
case is skipped like a timeout. On Windows it uses a bounded `taskkill /F /T`,
which the Windows CI leg does not exercise, as `docs/compatibility/vttest.md`
says.

`THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES`, at the repository root, reproduces the licence notices
of the 276 Rust crates linked into the Linux x86_64, Windows x86_64 and macOS
arm64 binaries, generated from the locked dependency list by
`scripts/third-party-licenses.py`. A crate whose published source has no
licence file gets the standard text of its declared licence and its declared
authors, and the file says so. It ships in the deb, rpm, AUR package, AppImage,
binary tarball, macOS app and Windows zip, and `NOTICE` points to it. CI checks
the file against the lockfile, and a test checks that every package definition
ships it.

Validation: with the reply change reverted, 18 Kitty tests fail; the runner's
seven new self-tests and the licence generator's ten offline fixtures each
fail under deliberate breakage of the rule they cover.

## 2026-10-12 -- The tarball installer installs the licence texts

The binary tarball carries `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` and the bundled fonts' licences,
but its `install.sh`, which the one-line installer's tarball branch also runs,
installed only the binary and desktop files, so a tarball install left no
licence copy beside the binary. It now copies them to
`PREFIX/share/licenses/odytty`, and `--uninstall` removes that directory.
`docs/install.md` and `PACKAGING.md` say so.

Validation: a new packaging test lays out an extracted tarball with a stand-in
binary, installs into a scratch prefix, compares every licence file with its
source, and checks that uninstall removes them. It fails with the previous
installer. The test runs on Linux only, where the tarball ships; the macOS
app and Windows zip are unchanged.

## 2026-10-12 -- Settings save unlocked where locking is unavailable, and recalled history keeps an escaped trailing space

Settings saves refuse when the `odytty.conf.lock` lock fails, except where
file locking is unsupported. An NFS mount without a lock manager reports that
as `ENOLCK`, not as unsupported, so a home directory on such a mount could
never save settings. `ENOLCK` on Unix, and `ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` and
`ERROR_INVALID_FUNCTION`, which some Windows network redirectors return for
`LockFileEx`, now count as locking unavailable: the save goes ahead unlocked
with a warning, and `docs/settings-guide.md` names both cases. Every other lock
error still refuses the save.

Command-palette history recall trimmed all trailing whitespace from a command,
after the zsh importer had already removed zsh's one padding space. A command
ending in a backslash and a space (an escaped literal space) was recalled with
a dangling backslash, which the shell parses differently. Shell history
commands now keep the spaces and tabs after a final backslash, for zsh, plain
(bash) and fish history; other trailing whitespace is still trimmed, and
directory entries are unchanged.

Two comments that had gained a second opening sentence were merged, and the
search painters call the shared snapshot shape check directly, now that it
refuses a zero side itself.

Validation: the new escaped-space test fails with the old trimming, and the
new raw-code checks in the lock policy test fail with the old policy. The
Windows codes are checked on the Windows CI leg only; no NFS mount or network
share was exercised.

## 2026-10-12 -- Saved-layout names keep non-ASCII text

Saved-layout filename stems preserve non-ASCII text on Linux, macOS and Windows
instead of replacing it with underscores. The 96-byte bound respects complete
UTF-8 scalars. Existing ASCII sanitization, traversal refusal and Windows device
name handling remain; older underscore aliases stay usable without automatic
renaming. Filesystem case folding and normalization retain their native limits.

Validation: the new test fails with the previous name sanitizer.

## 2026-10-12 -- Comment and documentation accuracy: Unicode data notice, number precision and session docs

Comments and documentation that a later read found inaccurate are corrected.
`NOTICE`, `PACKAGING.md` and the notice test now say the embedded tables come
from Unicode Character Database and Unicode emoji data files (both under the
Unicode License v3). The profile writer's comments describe the 15-digit limit
as the digits that survive a trip through `f64` and back, rather than digits
`f64` holds exactly. The active-workspace comment says a stray index panics in
debug builds, matching the debug assertion. Two doubled doc summaries are
merged and the memory notes name the benchmark profile once. No behaviour
changes.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
the full suite with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the
touched test groups five more times at four threads, the production and
documentation guards, and the RustSec audit passed.

## 2026-10-12 -- Windows refuses unresolved program names, settings saves refuse an unusable lock, profile backups never overwrite, and the search bar keeps long queries readable

The entries below landed together. They fix defects found by a full read of
the codebase and by reads of the changes that followed it. On Windows, a bare
program name that is found in none of the safe directories is now refused
instead of being handed to `CreateProcessW`, which would have searched the
directory OdyTTY was started from; this corrects an earlier entry's claim.
Shell discovery skips empty and relative `PATH` entries on every platform.
A settings save refuses with an error when its lock cannot be used, rather than
risk losing another window's save. Profile saves refuse an incomplete
case-collision check, malformed-profile backups never overwrite an earlier
backup, and a profile keeps unknown `switch` keys and long number literals
through a save. The search bar shows the end of a long query and its match
counter. Session teardown still kills and reaps a child after a panic poisoned
its lock, and a stale divider drag no longer resizes a zoomed, stacked or
floating tab's hidden layout. `NOTICE` now carries the Unicode License text for
the embedded Unicode data.

Validation: the full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,733 tests with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, every test target five more
times at four threads, the Python tooling self-tests, the fuzz crate check,
production and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. Each fix
has a test that fails before it, except where an entry says otherwise.

## 2026-10-12 -- Unresolved program names are refused, and shell discovery skips relative PATH entries

A bare program name from a profile or `--exec` that is found in none of the
safe Windows directories (the executable's directory, `System32`, the Windows
directory and `PATH`) used to be passed on as given. `CreateProcessW` with a
null application name then searched the directory OdyTTY was started from, so
a file of that name planted there still ran. The previous entry's claim that
Windows launches no longer resolve a bare name from the launch directory held
only for names that were found, and is corrected in place. Such a name is now
refused with a "was not found" error before any pseudoconsole is created. A
program given with a directory part is the caller's own path and is used as
given.

Shell discovery had the same gap on every platform: it joined each `PATH`
entry to the program name, including an empty entry, `.` and relative ones,
which resolve against the working directory. Those entries are now skipped, as
the launch lookups already did, so a shell copied into the launch directory
cannot make a candidate appear.

Validation: the new fallthrough test and the relative-entry test fail without
the change. The Windows spawn refusal is compiled by the Windows CI leg and
exercised there only; the pure rule is tested on every platform.

## 2026-10-12 -- Settings saves refuse an unusable lock, and a cut hosts file with a byte order mark keeps only whole lines

A settings save, and the first-run file that stops the welcome card from
reshowing, both take the `odytty.conf.lock` lock first. When that lock could
not be opened or taken, they went ahead without it, which reopened the
lost-update race the lock exists to close. They now refuse with an error that
says nothing was saved. The one exception is a platform or file system that
reports file locking as unsupported: there settings still save unlocked, a
warning is logged, and `docs/settings-guide.md` states that two windows saving
at the same moment can then lose one change.

The saved-hosts reader reads one byte past its limit to tell a longer file from
one that ends at the limit. It removed a leading byte order mark before that
comparison, so an oversized file with a mark looked complete and a `HostName`
the limit cut was read as a shorter, wrong destination. The cut is now found in
file bytes, the mark included, on both the byte parser and the file reader, and
only whole lines of a cut file are kept. When the first byte past the limit is
a newline, the last line inside the limit is whole and is now kept too.

Validation: the new lock test fails when an unopenable lock still lets the
save and the stub write, and the two hosts tests fail with the old length
check. The unsupported-locking rule is checked as a pure function; no file
system that refuses locking was exercised. The lock and the reader are shared
by Linux, macOS and Windows; the Windows lock runs on the Windows CI leg only.

## 2026-10-12 -- Bound profile save admission and preserve quarantine backups

Profile saves now refuse failed or incomplete case-collision scans, including
entry errors and directories beyond the 1,024-entry budget. Malformed-file
recovery claims each backup with a non-replacing hard link before removing the
source. Competing destinations retry the bounded suffix sequence; uncertain
inspection or claim errors preserve the source. Filesystems without hard
links refuse recovery. Source removal errors leave both names intact, and
symbolic links are left untouched. The full `.profile.json.bad` backup suffix
is documented.

Strict profile JSON checks decoded duplicate keys with a per-object set and
admits at most 4,096 members per object, including unknown objects. Unknown
members inside that bound retain their save behavior; lenient tool-output and
palette readers retain their existing policy. No speed improvement is claimed.

Validation: ten regressions failed before and passed after. Formatting,
Rust 1.96 clippy on Linux and both Windows/macOS cross targets, the full serial
and four-thread suites, both guards and RustSec passed. The 157 touched tests
passed serially and in five four-thread runs. Full runs retained 34 ignored
cases and explicit zsh/font runtime skips. Native three-platform CI and
filesystem acceptance remain separate from cross-target lint.

## 2026-10-12 -- Profiles keep unknown switch keys and long number literals through a save

A profile whose `switch` object held only keys this build does not know failed
validation on save and export, because the writer dropped the object; the
profile guide told users to avoid editing such a profile. The object is now
written back, and an empty `switch` object is still omitted. An unknown number
whose literal has more than 15 significant digits, which a 64-bit float cannot
always hold exactly, now keeps its literal and is saved unchanged. Shorter
numbers are written in their shortest form as before, so `1E+2` saves as
`100`, and fields this build owns are still written from their typed value.
The lenient reader used for tool output and palette files is unchanged.

Validation: the switch and literal tests fail before the change; two control
tests (short numbers, a known 20-digit font size) pass before and after. No
existing profile is newly refused. All platforms share the code.

## 2026-10-12 -- NOTICE carries the Unicode License, and memory documentation states current cell sizes

The binary embeds tables generated from the Unicode Character Database
(bidirectional classes, emoji widths, Indic properties), but `NOTICE` did not
carry the Unicode License. It now names Unicode 17.0.0 and includes the
Unicode License v3 text in full, with a short note that the linked Rust crates
and their versions are listed in `Cargo.lock` and their licence expressions in
each crate manifest. A new packaging test keeps `NOTICE` equal to the licence
files the generated tables cite, checks the named Unicode version, and fails
when a new table citing the Unicode copyright is not listed. Every package
already ships `NOTICE`.

`docs/memory.md` no longer states the 44-byte cell as current. The old tables
stay as dated history, and the current figures are stated: a 92-byte live cell,
a 28-byte stored cell, 29.97 bytes per retained cell without marks, 80 more
bytes per marked cell, and a 77.5 percent break-even. A three-run same-machine
measurement of the commit that grew the live cell records whole-grid model
operations 1.3 to 2.0 times slower and vertex building 20 percent faster; that
commit also changed cluster logic, so the difference is not attributed to the
cell size alone. No optimization is adopted.

Validation: two of the three packaging tests fail on the previous `NOTICE`.

## 2026-10-12 -- Test oracles for background-pane resize, timers and a missing Kitty file are exact

Three tests passed against code that was wrong. The resize test now dirties
selection, hover, viewport, search, the restore viewport and row fades on a
visible and a background workspace, checks that a resize resets them all, and
checks that a second resize to the same grid resets nothing. The timer test
arms the synchronized-output hold, which the visible sibling pane keeps and a
background workspace loses. The Kitty missing-file test now requires the
`EIO:read-failed` class and exactly one error reply. No production code
changed.

Validation: seven deliberate faults in the production code each fail the new
tests; the old tests passed under the three that mattered.

## 2026-10-12 -- Control connects honor their deadline, and idle accepts end the transient streak

The automation client's connect retry checked its deadline only after a full
accept queue, retried an interrupted attempt without looking at the clock, slept
a full five milliseconds even with less time left, and could make one more
attempt, and succeed, after the deadline. The first attempt is still always
made; every later attempt now runs only while time remains, a sleep never
exceeds the time left, and an interrupted attempt stops at the deadline like the
rest. The listener's run of transient `accept` failures was documented as
consecutive but survived idle polls, so a hundred aborted peers spread over a
long session stopped the endpoint. An idle poll now ends the run.

Validation: the retry rules run against an injected clock with no sleeping, so
they are exact: interrupted attempts stop at the deadline, no attempt runs at
or after it, the last sleep is the time left, and success before the deadline
returns. The transport's not-delivered marks are now driven through the real
client for a missing endpoint, a wrongly permitted one and a connect timeout
against a full accept queue, and a failure after the request was written is
checked to stay unmarked; the peer-credential and stream-setup marks sit on
failures this process cannot provoke as its own user and stay untested. Each
rule fails when its code is reverted. Linux and macOS share the transport;
Windows uses a separate named-pipe client that this change does not touch.

## 2026-10-12 -- Session teardown survives a poisoned lock, and divider queries follow the visible layout

Closing a session, closing it after its shell exited, and whole-application
shutdown skipped the kill, the reap and the forced reader end-of-file whenever
