a panicking thread had poisoned the session's PTY or attach-client lock, which
left the child running and its threads and descriptors open. Teardown now
takes those locks through the same poison-recovering helper the rest of the
session code uses.

A divider drag index left over from the tiled view still resolved an axis, and
could still drag or snap the hidden tiled tree, after the tab was zoomed,
stacked or floated. Those three calls now share the gate the divider grab and
hover already used, so they do nothing while no divider is drawn.

Moving a tab or pane to another window resolves the source workspace and tab
once and removes exactly what it checked, instead of re-reading the indices.
The fallbacks for a focused pane with no session and an out-of-range active
workspace or tab stay in release builds and now assert in debug builds.

The three close and shutdown boundedness tests no longer compare elapsed time
with a wall-clock limit. Each parks its reader until the test releases it and
checks that the close or shutdown returned while the reader was still parked;
the parked readers are released when the test ends instead of outliving it.

Validation: the poisoned-lock tests and the divider test fail before the
change. The reworked boundedness tests pass on the current code and would
block, then fail, against a teardown that joined the reader on the caller. The
poisoned-lock tests run on Linux and macOS, because they need a child that
outlives the test; Windows teardown runs the same lock helper.

## 2026-10-12 -- The search bar keeps a long query's end and its match counter visible

A search query longer than the bar was cut at the right edge, which hid the
last typed characters and the `n/m` match counter. The bar now shows the label,
an ellipsis, as much of the end of the query as fits, and the counter. A wide
character that does not fit is left out whole. A bar too narrow for the label,
the counter and one character of query keeps the old cut at the edge. The
search highlight and the bar painters also return without drawing for a
snapshot with a zero side or missing cells, instead of subtracting one from a
zero dimension; the terminal never produces such a snapshot.

Concealed (SGR 8) text stays in a scrollback export, the same as copy and
command-output export return it. `docs/features.md` now says so, and a copy
test pins it beside the existing export test. Two search tests changed with
the new bar: the edge-cut test became
`a_long_query_keeps_its_tail_and_counter_without_touching_other_rows`, and the
wide-owner test became
`a_wide_owner_that_does_not_fit_the_query_tail_is_left_out_whole`.

Validation: the new bar tests and the zero-size test fail before the change.
The concealed-copy test passes before and after: it records existing
behaviour. The search bar is shared by Linux, macOS and Windows.

## 2026-10-12 -- Snapshot admission validates before it allocates and refuses empty dimensions

The allocating `build_vertices` wrapper reserved its vertex buffer from the
unchecked dimension product before the admission check ran, so a short
snapshot reserved memory for cells it did not hold and an overflowing claim
panicked, although the `_into` builders refused the same snapshot. The wrapper
now checks first and reserves from a checked figure. A snapshot claiming no
columns or no rows is refused too: `Dimensions::new` never produces one, but
the fields are public, and the bidi plan used to build per-row vectors for
however many rows such a snapshot claimed. Normal snapshots draw exactly as
before.

Validation: the shortened, overflowing, no-column and no-row snapshots now go
through the allocating wrapper and the bidi plan; the tests fail against the
previous sources (a short snapshot reserved capacity) and pass after. No
huge allocation is attempted. All platforms share the code.

## 2026-10-12 -- Windows file transport is tested against a planted directory link

The Windows file-transport checks covered a link as the final path component
but not a directory link planted inside the admitted root, which resolves
outside it once the path is canonicalized. A Windows test now plants one and
checks that the file behind it is not delivered. Creating a directory link
needs a privilege some accounts lack, so a refusal to create one skips the
check with a printed note instead of passing it. Only the Windows CI leg runs
this test; the junction form of a directory link is not created by it.

## 2026-10-12 -- Isolate tab-bar input persistence tests

Keyboard editing followed by Save, plus pointer drags, now persist tab-bar
height in owned scratch config files. The tests check the saved value and
preserved comment.
Three tab-bar seam geometry tests and the hidden-top-bar rail-width test also
use private paths, as does the background-tab input responsiveness fixture.
A subprocess
regression checks all seven real input routes against untouched inherited
platform config and state directories. Production settings paths are unchanged
on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation passes: formatting, Clippy on Linux plus Windows and macOS targets,
serial and four-thread local suites with the Git archive and pager fixtures
excluded, five four-thread repetitions of the touched groups, documentation
and production-file guards, and RustSec. The seven-case regression fails on the
original fixtures. The final inventory contains no inherited App config, lock
or shell state; GPU driver cache isolation remains separate.

## 2026-10-12 -- Two structural test checks cover their own inputs

The check that every settings reload test holds the render-globals lock scanned
a hand-written list of test files. It now also compares that list with the
directory's `mod` lines and with the files on disk, so a new test file cannot
be left out of the scan, and its comment states what the scan does not see:
calls through a helper, the application-level reload tests, and tests that
only read the globals.

The CI validation self-test read the required GPU, shell and composite test
names from `scripts/ci-validation.sh` but never checked them against the code.
It now requires each name to be defined exactly once as a `#[test]` function,
and each composite name to live in `tests/gpu_composite_smoke.rs`.

Validation: both checks pass on the current tree. Each fails when its input
drifts in a scratch copy (an undeclared test file, a test module missing from
the table, a misspelt required name); neither found drift today.

## 2026-10-12 -- Comment and documentation accuracy follow-up

Comments and documentation that a later read found inaccurate are corrected.
The colour-emoji routing comments now state that the media controls
U+23F8..U+23FA are text-default in Unicode and included deliberately, rather
than claiming the list matches Emoji_Presentation exactly. The rpm, tarball,
font-licence and one-line installer comments describe where licence texts go
and which package managers resolve the rpm's dependencies. The benchmark
results note that their context-switch counts came from the main thread only
and are not comparable with later counts, which sum the whole process tree.
Smaller test and helper doc comments now describe what the code does. No
behaviour changes.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
7,691 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the
touched test groups five more times at four threads, and the production and
documentation guards passed.

## 2026-10-11 -- Windows network working directories, emoji highlight colour edges, Kitty layout keys, packaging licences and test-tooling hardening

The entries below landed together. They fix defects found by a full read of
the codebase. On Windows, a profile or command-line working directory may name
a network share or a WSL distribution, and a profile variable whose name
differs only by non-ASCII case replaces the inherited one. A highlight no
longer hides or invents a colour change inside an emoji sequence, and Kitty
alternate-key reporting names the key of the active layout. zsh history recall
follows zsh's continuation rule, copy-mode word motions rest on wide glyph
leads off screen, profile-form command edits stay explicit, and the settings
path picker reports failed reads instead of loading forever. Remote upload
cleanup is bounded. Every package now carries the bundled fonts' licence texts,
and the version-pinned installer installs its own release. Test, conformance,
mutation, demo, memory and benchmark tooling no longer hides a wrong result,
and shell test children no longer write to the real home directory.

Validation: the full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting, pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,691 tests with none failing
both one at a time and four at a time, every test target five more times at
four threads, the Python tooling self-tests, the fuzz crate check, production
and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. Each fix has a
test that fails before it, except where an entry says otherwise.

## 2026-10-11 -- Windows environment names fold non-ASCII case

A profile variable whose name differs from an inherited or terminal variable
only in non-ASCII case (`Ä_X` and `ä_X`) reached a Windows shell twice, and
removing an inherited variable missed such spellings, because the name match
folded ASCII letters only. Windows itself treats those names as one variable:
it compares environment names ordinally through its uppercase table. The
environment block now uses that rule (`CompareStringOrdinal` with case folding)
for override deduplication, inherited shadowing, removal and the block's sort
order, keeping the last spelling and value. The merge moved into its own
module with a portable model of the rule, so its behaviour is tested on every
platform; the system call decides on Windows. Unix environment names stay
case-sensitive and unchanged. Two Windows tests changed with the comparator:
the ASCII-only equality test became `env_name_cmp_folds_ascii_and_non_ascii_case`,
and the sort test checks order with the new comparison.

Validation: the portable deduplication, shadowing and removal tests fail with
the previous ASCII-only comparison and pass with the Windows rule. The Windows
tests run on the Windows CI leg only.

## 2026-10-11 -- zsh history recall follows zsh's own continuation rule

The command-palette history import treated a zsh history line ending in an even
number of backslashes as a complete command. zsh 5.9 does not count them: its
writer puts one backslash before each newline inside a command without
escaping the backslashes already there, and its reader joins a line to the
next whenever the final byte is a backslash. A command containing a literal
backslash right before a newline was therefore split into two entries. The
importer now removes exactly the final backslash as the newline marker and
keeps every earlier backslash as command text, and it removes the single
padding space zsh writes after a command that itself ends in a backslash. Fish
and plain history decoding are unchanged. The shared importer behaves the same
on Linux, macOS and Windows when a zsh history path is configured. The existing
test `zsh_history_joins_continued_lines_and_unmetafies` changed its
end-of-command backslash sample to the padded form zsh writes.

Validation: a new test over a project-authored sample in zsh 5.9's layout,
with one, two and three trailing backslashes, a padded literal end backslash
and metafied text inside a continued entry, fails with the previous parity
rule and passes now.

## 2026-10-11 -- The Sixel pixel cap is stated and fuzzed at its shipped value

The Sixel decoder refuses images over 16,777,216 pixels, 64 MiB of RGBA, the
same bound the image store applies to decoded images. SPEC.md and
`docs/graphics.md` still gave the older 40 million pixel (about 152 MiB)
figure, and the coverage-guided Sixel fuzz target asserted that older value
along with its own copies of the axis and input limits, so a decoder cap
raised past the store bound would still have passed it. The decoder's width,
height and pixel limits are now public constants; the fuzz target imports
them and the raw graphics payload bound, and checks the pixel cap against the
image store's decoded-byte bound on every run. A unit test pins the same
lockstep in the ordinary test suite. The documents state 16,777,216 pixels.
Decoder behaviour is unchanged on every platform.

Validation: the new lockstep test passes and fails when the decoder cap is
raised in a scratch copy; the fuzz target compiles with `cargo check` on the
pinned toolchain.

## 2026-10-11 -- Benchmark harness: exact interval ranks, robust oracles, per-thread context switches and retry-safe targets

Corrections to the comparative benchmark harness under `scripts/bench-protocol/`.
None changes a published result: the published W6 document still validates
against its preregistration with the corrected statistics.

- Bootstrap intervals: the low endpoint used a percentile computed in floating
  point (2.500000000000002), which moved its nearest rank over 10,000
  resamples from 250 to 251. Percentiles are now exact rationals, so the
  endpoints are ranks 250 and 9750 as the protocol states.
- The result validator recomputed W6 summaries from raw samples before
  checking them, so a pass sample without a value or block aborted
  validation with an exception instead of listing the error. Malformed samples
  are now left out of the recomputation and reported by the per-sample checks.
- The software-endpoint child parsed the first `ESC [` in its input as the
  cursor report, so a focus report or arrow key arriving first voided a valid
  reply. It now takes the first complete `ESC [ row ; col R`. Restoring the
  terminal state after an injected CPR preparer no longer depends on an import
  made in another branch.
- Context switches were read from `/proc/<pid>/status`, which counts only the
  thread-group leader, and differenced across process sets taken at different
  times. They are now read per thread and summed over the threads alive for
  the whole measurement; when threads start or exit in between, the replicate
  reports the metric unsupported. The collector description says so.
- A W6 session refused by a pre-flight check no longer leaves its results
  directory behind, record values (time budget, background and
  instrumentation-overhead ceilings) are checked before the first probe launch,
  and the overhead ceiling must be a number from 0 to 100. A `--run` checks the
  public anchor and runtime identities before creating its private evidence
  directory, and a refused evidence package writes neither manifest, so each
  can be retried after a fix.
- An interrupted reference-readiness run removes its empty public reservation
  and keeps its private evidence, as the other preparation actions do. The four
  reservation helpers share one implementation, a geometry smoke reports its
  own target and option names, every directory a reservation creates is 0700,
  and a failed reservation removes the directories it created.
- The three copies of the common-grid selection rule (the retired search, its
  validator and the calibration diagnostic) now share one selector and differ
  only in which attempts they admit.
- Execution order is balanced exactly only over whole cycles of blocks. Five
  W6 replicates or 30 SE blocks across four implementations leave a residual
  imbalance; `docs/benchmark-protocol.md` now says so, and
  `ordering.py --balance` reports the position counts of a preregistered order.

The harness runs on Linux; its self-tests run in the Ubuntu CI leg.

Validation: `bench-protocol.py --self-test` passes. Each new self-test check
fails with its fix reverted, except the order-balance report, which is new
disclosure, and the shared selector, which keeps the existing selection tests
passing unchanged.

## 2026-10-11 -- Explicit working directories may name a Windows network share

Windows refused every working directory that began with two path separators
before any probe or ConPTY child creation, which also blocked a directory the
user had typed: a profile `working_directory` or `--working-directory` naming a
UNC share or a `\\wsl.localhost` distribution fell back to the home directory.
Launch directories now carry their origin. A profile or command-line directory
is explicit: on Windows it is kept as typed, the profile form and the
missing-directory fallback do not probe it (a probe is itself a network
access), and ConPTY child creation receives it unchanged, so an unreachable
share is reported when the tab starts. Directories reported by a terminal
program (OSC 7), restored from a saved layout or inherited from the focused pane
stay refused and fall back to the home directory, so terminal output and saved
state still cannot aim a probe or a child at a share. A fallback directory is
derived, never explicit. The profile form again reports a plain "does not exist"
for a missing local directory. Linux and macOS are unchanged: double-slash paths
are ordinary there. The Windows rule is tested on every platform with the
platform injected; the ConPTY call itself is exercised only by the Windows CI leg.

Validation: five mutations of the new source plumbing each fail the new tests.

## 2026-10-11 -- A control socket that cannot be identified after binding is left in place

The previous change removed the socket file when the bind could not identify
it. The bind does not report which file it created, so nothing proved that what
occupied the path was the socket this call had bound: if the path had been
replaced in between, the cleanup deleted the replacement. The file is now
retained and the bind returns the identification error. A later start at that
path reports that the address is in use until the leftover is removed by hand.
The guard that removes an identified socket still checks the socket and its
parent before unlinking. Unix-only; Windows uses a named pipe and has no
socket file.

Validation: the new test replaces the bound path with an ordinary file inside
a failing identification and requires the file to survive; it fails before the
change. The earlier test that expected removal is replaced.

## 2026-10-11 -- A highlight keeps a stored colour change inside a legacy emoji sequence

The selection, search and copy-mode passes mark the cells they recolour, and
the color-cluster join ignored colour and inverse on a marked cell, because the
pass had overwritten them. That also hid a genuine colour change the program
had stored: two adjacent flag owners with different foregrounds stayed separate
until one was highlighted, then merged into one glyph, and split again when the
highlight cleared. Before overwriting a cell, each pass now records whether the
stored foreground, background or inverse changes at the cell's two edges, and
the join reads those records, so a stored boundary still splits the sequence
under any highlight and a highlight never adds or removes one. The records are
render-only flags in free bits of the existing attribute word: they are never
parsed, serialized or compared, and the cell size is unchanged. Hover,
hint and overlay-panel paints do not mark cells and are unchanged.
Platform-neutral render code, so Linux, macOS and Windows behave the same.

Validation: the new selection, search and copy-mode tests fail before the
change (the pair merges under a highlight) and pass after, as does the edge
recording test over every painting order.

## 2026-10-11 -- The demo repository and the memory capture no longer hide a wrong result

`scripts/make-demo.sh` built its fixture repository with `git init` and then
switched to a branch named `master`. Under a default branch of `main` the
switch failed silently, the merge became a no-op and the README screenshot
showed the wrong graph. The repository is now created on `master` explicitly,
signing and hooks are turned off for its commits, and a failing step stops the
script before a screenshot is taken. `scripts/memory-capture.py` recorded
`heap_bytes` as `0` for a process with no `[heap]` mapping, which is a
measurement; it is now `unmeasured` with a reason, and the documentation says
the figure is the main heap only. Windows, macOS and unsupported-platform
records now carry `swap_bytes` as `unmeasured` with a reason instead of
omitting it.

Validation: two new workflow policy tests (a hostile git configuration and a
failing step) and six new self-test checks fail before the change and pass
after.

## 2026-10-11 -- The mutation classifier names code by its text, not its line number

The platform-exclusion table and the risk rules that mark a mutant equivalent
pinned source line numbers that had moved. Lines 429 to 533 of the Kitty
transport were excluded as macOS-only although Linux compiles most of them, so
a surviving mutant there would have been reported as unmeasured; the Windows
reparse check and the macOS copy were not excluded at all; and the two
equivalence rules pointed at lines that no longer held the fast-path arms or
the open-flag expression, so those mutants were classed as ordinary risks.
Exclusion rows now give a file, a build condition and a text anchor, and the
classifier resolves each to the attribute-to-end range of exactly one item in
the source tree it runs against; the equivalence rules test the text of the
mutated line, and the open-flag rule applies only to the exclusive-or
replacement. A row whose item is gone or ambiguous, or a rule that matches no
line, stops the run. `--verify-sources` checks both, runs with the campaign's
`verify` step, and is part of the classifier self-test in CI.

Validation: the new self-test cases (item extents, shifted items, missing,
ambiguous and wrong-condition rows, anchored risk rules, unreadable source)
and the live-tree check pass; against the previous table and rules the same
lines classify wrongly (retained comparison).

## 2026-10-11 -- The conformance runner no longer trims evidence or buffers unbounded output

The runner's own rule is that a capture which hits its cap fails the case, but
the case runners sliced the captured output to the cap and carried on, trimmed
the suite's log to its cap before reading verdicts, and buffered the whole
output in memory until the child exited. Child output is now read in chunks
with each stream capped as it arrives, a replay case whose output reaches the
cap fails, an upstream log larger than the artifact cap fails instead of being
read from its prefix, and the subject's own output in an upstream run, which is
only a diagnostic, is marked as truncated in its file. Archive extraction now
validates every member in a first pass before the second pass writes any file,
as its documentation already claimed. The identity redaction applied to
captures was quadratic in the length of a token and effectively hung on a
capture of one million characters without whitespace; it is now a single pass
over tokens with identical results.

Validation: new self-tests for the bounded reader, both caps, the validation
order and the long-token redaction; before the change the redaction tests hit
a watchdog, the cap tests reported `ignore` and `skip` instead of `fail`, and
the extraction test left the first member on disk.

## 2026-10-11 -- A shaped owner run is drawn from its own pixels, never another run's

A whole complex-script owner run is cached under a key that holds a 64-bit
digest of its face, style source and placed glyphs. The digest uses a fixed,
public hasher and the text is chosen by whatever writes to the terminal, so
two different runs could share one key and the second would draw the first
run's pixels. The atlas now keeps the run each key was drawn from and answers a
hit only when the run at hand is equal to it; a different run with the same
digest keeps the per-cell path. Selection, copy and search were never affected
because they read logical text. Platform-neutral atlas code, so Linux, macOS
and Windows behave the same.

Validation: the new tests force two different runs onto one digest and fail
before the change (the second run was handed the first run's key).

## 2026-10-11 -- Kitty character keys report the layout's key, not a US fold

With alternate-key reporting on, a character key was reported from the produced
character alone: shifted punctuation was folded back to the unshifted key of a
US layout, and the base-layout field was derived the same way. On a German
layout the key that makes `+` and, with Shift, `*` was reported as the key for
`8`, and the key that makes `Z` carried a base layout of `z` where a US layout
has `y` in that position. The native keyboard now passes the encoder what the
layout and the physical key say: the key code is what the key produces
unshifted on the active layout, the shifted field is the produced character,
and the base-layout field is what a standard US layout has under the same
physical key, left out when it equals the produced character or when the
position has no US key. Composed text with no single key behind it reports the
produced character and no alternates. Keys synthesized without layout data keep
the US derivation. Platform-neutral encoder code fed by the winit key and
physical key on Linux, macOS and Windows; Windows with Win32 input mode active
uses its own record and is unchanged.

Validation: new encoder tests and an App-level test through the real key path
with US and German-layout keys; the App-level test fails before the change and
the encoder tests exercise the new entry point.

## 2026-10-11 -- Keep profile launch edits explicit and pane failure attention stable

Profile-form shell edits and shell suggestions replace a stored command and its
arguments. Clearing the command program now selects the default shell instead
of restoring the old command. Untouched command data still round-trips.
Workspace snapshot versions reject fractional, negative and out-of-range
values before version classification; integral decimal and exponent forms keep
their existing behavior. Later pane monitor notices preserve an unseen
command-failure badge until the pane is viewed or attention is cleared.

Validation: six regressions fail before the changes and pass after them; the
integral-version control passes throughout. Formatting, pinned Rust 1.96
clippy on Linux and both cross targets, full serial and four-thread suites,
130 touched tests serially and in five four-thread runs, production and
documentation guards, RustSec and staged whitespace checks pass. The full
suites retain 34 ignored cases and runtime skips for unavailable zsh and
font-dependent cases.

## 2026-10-11 -- Shell test children isolate writable state

Direct shell helpers and Unix PTY smoke tests pass private home, config, data,
state, cache and history paths through the shared child-directory fixture.
Bash history, fish startup directories and htop settings stay within the owning
test. File-drop shell probes use the same fixture, and the CLI live-host test
re-executes in a private child environment. Existing startup tests retain
their explicit HOME and ZDOTDIR overrides. Cleanup follows child exit and also
runs when an assertion unwinds. Product spawning and Windows console policy
remain unchanged.

Validation: both writer regressions fail with the former child environment and
pass after isolation. Serial and four-thread suites pass with the two
Git-writing fixtures excluded. Five four-thread repetitions pass for 92 shell,
file-drop and directory tests, nine PTY tests and the CLI live-host test.
Inherited Bash history, fish and htop state stays absent. Formatting, Linux,
Windows and macOS Clippy, file guards and both-lockfile RustSec checks pass.

## 2026-10-11 -- Copy-mode word motions rest on wide glyph leads off screen

A word-end motion that walked past the viewport edge onto a wide-glyph word
could end on the glyph's trailing spacer, because the off-screen lookup
carried only the resolved character (a spacer resolves to its lead's
character) and could not tell a spacer from a lead. The lookup now also
reports whether an off-screen cell is a wide-glyph spacer, so the caret rests
on the lead below and above the viewport as it does on screen, and `e` from an
off-screen lead steps over the glyph's spacer to the next word. Shared App
code, so Linux, macOS and Windows behave the same.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the
full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,617 tests, none failing), the
copy-mode tests five more times at four threads, the production and documentation
guards and the RustSec audit all passed. Two model tests and one App-level test
through the real key path fail before the change (caret on the spacer, no
advance) and pass after it.

## 2026-10-11 -- Required GPU validation covers the composite tests; test fixtures are hermetic

The required Linux validation step proved execution of the library's headless
GPU tests only. The integration tests in `tests/gpu_composite_smoke.rs`
(passthrough composite, bloom, CRT, viewer scrim and the fixture lifetime
check) did not read `ODYTTY_REQUIRE_GPU_TESTS`, so a lost adapter or a missing
`Rgba16Float` render format turned all of them into silent passes on every
platform. They now select the software Vulkan adapter when the variable is set,
fail with the same `VALIDATION_REQUIRED` text the library helper uses when the
adapter or format is missing, and print a `VALIDATION_EXECUTED` line that
`scripts/ci-validation.sh` requires for each of them in a second `cargo test`
invocation. The two bloom tests now report their own names.

The protocol-fuzz ownership scan is two-sided: it takes the owner width from
the production rule instead of "first scalar is wide or any extension scalar",
so a stray spacer after a one-column cluster (a base with a combining mark) and
a two-column multi-scalar owner (a VS16 emoji, a keycap, a conjunct) without its
spacer or in the final column are both rejected. Four new tests break a real
snapshot in each way and require the scan to fail. The rule is exposed as the
documentation-hidden `odytty::core::owner_display_width`.

Python self-test suites embedded in tools are now covered by the workflow
policy test (`scripts/vttest-runner.py selftest` was run by no step and now runs
in the shell-script fixtures step), and the macOS/Windows GPU availability
smoke is checked against the required list. Test fixtures that run `git` or
`less` are hermetic: the pager smoke test builds its repository with a cleared
environment and pins the repository variables of the one inherited-environment
command, a failed `git init` there is a failure and no longer a skip, the `less`
tests use an empty home and clear every lesskey input, and the provenance
fixture check re-runs itself under an injected `GIT_CONFIG_*` environment and
shows the injection is effective against a plain `git`. The box-drawing off-path
check compares against an atlas that never had the feature touched, and no
longer prints atlases on failure.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,619 tests, none failing), the touched suites five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards and the RustSec audit all passed. The composite tests fail before the change when the adapter is withheld under the required variable (they passed as unavailable-hardware), the four ownership-scan tests fail against the old rule, the inherited-environment `less` test fails under a hostile lesskey, and the provenance check fails when the environment is no longer cleared. The box-drawing and embedded-suite changes strengthen assertions and have no failing-before run.

## 2026-10-11 -- Pinned installer, rpm upgrade path, font licences in every package, conformance fixture repairs

- The version-pinned `odytty-<version>-install.sh` release asset used to be a
  byte copy of `dist/install.sh`, which downloads from `releases/latest`, so a
  pinned installer installed whichever release was newest. The release workflow
  now stamps the download directory with `dist/pin-installer.sh`, so the asset
  installs the release it was published with and checks that release's signed
  manifest. The stamping fails unless the source has exactly one expected line.
  Installers published before this change keep installing the latest release.
- The installer's rpm path uses `rpm -Uvh --replacepkgs`, so a newer package
  upgrades an installed one, and a host with zypper and without dnf hands the
  package to zypper. `--help` works in the `curl ... | bash` form (the usage text
  is embedded; it used to read `$0`), an empty sudo array no longer aborts old
  bash, and downloads are held to HTTPS including redirects. The tarball
  installer documents `sudo env PREFIX=/usr/local ./install.sh`; a `PREFIX`
  assigned before `sudo` never reached the script.
- The bundled fonts are embedded in the binary, and their licences require the
  notices to travel with every copy. The deb, rpm, AUR package, binary tarball,
  Windows zip, AppImage and macOS app now carry the licence texts listed in
  `dist/font-licenses.txt`, and the AUR package and macOS app carry `NOTICE` too.
  `tests/packaging_font_licenses.rs` keeps that list equal to the licence files in
  `assets/fonts` and every package definition in step with it.
- The `cursor-addressing` replay fixture printed a nine-character string at the
  clamped bottom-right cell, which wrapped and scrolled the screen and defeated
  the check it was meant to make; it prints one character that completes the
  corner marker. The `tab-stops` fixture used the reset-to-default control
  (DECST8C), which is not implemented; it sets explicit stops instead.
  `tests/vttest_replay_fixtures.rs` replays both through the terminal model and
  asserts the screen their comments promise.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,629 tests, none failing), the touched suites five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards and the RustSec audit all passed. The new installer, packaging and fixture tests fail on the unchanged files, except the stamping refusal test, which asserts a refusal and passes without the script. The zypper path, the Windows zip step, the macOS app assembly and the release workflow edit run only when a release or development build runs.

## 2026-10-11 -- Remote upload cleanup is bounded

The helper `ssh` that removes a closed tab's uploaded images was started before
its watcher thread. When the thread could not be created the child was dropped,
which neither kills nor reaps it, so a stalled link left a live process and
later a zombie. The child is now owned by a shared cell until the watcher is
admitted, and a failed start kills and reaps it. The wait after a kill is
bounded: a process that cannot be killed or does not exit within two seconds is
reported as stuck, where it used to be waited on without limit, and the upload
worker's notice says the transfer could not be stopped. At most eight cleanups
run at once and a cleanup requested past that is skipped, and a tab remembers at
most 256 uploaded paths, forgetting the oldest beyond that; neither policy
deletes an earlier file, whose path may already be in use. A skipped or
forgotten file is left to the remote's own temp-file reaper, as before. The
Windows test children used by the bounded-wait tests suppress their console
window like the production spawn.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,620 tests, none failing), the touched suites five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards and the RustSec audit all passed. The new ledger, spawn-failure and cleanup-cap tests fail when the corresponding rule is removed. The bounded wait after a kill is tested with a stand-in process that survives it; the old unbounded wait cannot be run against such a process, so it has no failing-before run.

## 2026-10-11 -- Settings path picker: failed reads, bounds and focus

- A directory read whose worker panicked or was lost left the picker loading
  forever; a disconnected channel is now a failed read, shown as an unreadable
  directory, and it reaches the render signature.
- When no worker thread could be created the picker read the directory on the
  window thread. It now refuses the read instead, and reads past four in flight
  across all pickers are refused the same way, so abandoned reads of a stalled
  mount cannot pile up threads. The starting directory is resolved on a worker
  with a quarter-second wait on the window thread and falls back to the home
  directory.
- Cached listings are bounded to 16 directories and 4096 entries, oldest first.
- A file whose path is not valid UTF-8 can no longer be committed as a
  different path: such files are not listed, a notice counts them, and a
  commit of one is refused.
- Keyboard focus follows the entry rows the window really shows instead of a
  fixed eight, the footer keeps its row in bodies four rows or taller, and
  breadcrumbs and names are cut by display columns so wide scripts stay inside
  the body.
- The About view's wheel scroll is bounded by the About rows, not by an
  unrelated entry list, so a short panel can reach Copy diagnostics, and the
  unsaved-changes prompt adds its fixed lines only while the body has room.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,627 tests, none failing), the touched suites five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards and the RustSec audit all passed. Each rule has a test that fails when the rule is reverted: lost worker, fixed eight rows, lossy commit, scalar cuts, unbounded cache, uncapped reads, close-prompt rows and the About wheel bound.

## 2026-10-11 -- Comment and documentation accuracy pass

Source comments, test messages and documentation were corrected where the
full-codebase read found them stale, misleading or wordier than the code they
describe, across 280 files. Comments now match what the code does, retired
stage labels and finding codes are gone from names and messages, and the
remaining long dashes in user-visible strings are replaced with colons,
commas or full stops, so a few status, picker and notice messages read
slightly differently. Three test names lost their stage prefixes, and three
`dead_code` allowances that no longer suppressed anything were removed. The
accessibility guide and TODO now say that strongly saturated colours can
display below the minimum contrast target, and that a lower `min_contrast`
lets more of them meet it. Nothing else changes behaviour.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
the full test suite one at a time and four at a time (none failing), five
more full four-thread runs, production and documentation guards, and the
RustSec audit all passed.

## 2026-10-11 -- Settings, profiles, hosts and automation hardening, Windows launch lookup, Sixel rectangles and decoration rules

The entries below landed together. They fix defects found by a full read of the
codebase. A filled Sixel rectangle that the previous re-layout bound refused
is drawn again. Settings saves from several OdyTTY windows no longer lose each
other's changes, non-Latin key chords bind and save, and the `default` token
selects the shipped default. Profile documents are read through a strict JSON
boundary with clearer field limits, and profile shell discovery is bounded.
The connection-hosts file keeps a byte order mark, refuses to rewrite a file
that is not UTF-8, and its writer and reader agree on aliases and ports. The
automation endpoint retries transient accept failures, waits for a busy owner
and reports requests that never left as safe to retry. Windows launches no
longer resolve a bare program name from the launch directory, SSH control
sockets stay bindable under deep state paths, PowerShell keeps the user's Enter
function, and the Windows symbol tail reaches Cambria Math. Underlines,
strikethrough and the under-cursor glyph follow the same crop and owner rules
as glyph ink, and every grid builder refuses a snapshot whose cells do not
match its dimensions.

Validation: the full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting,
pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,614 tests with none
failing both one at a time and four at a time, every test target five more
times at four threads, the Python CI fixture suites, production and
documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. Each fix has a test
that fails before it, except where an entry says otherwise.

## 2026-10-11 -- A filled Sixel rectangle is no longer refused for re-layout work

The per-image re-layout budget added for widening bands also refused an
ordinary filled rectangle: 700 bands of 2500 columns (2500x4200, 10.5M pixels)
were rejected at 4188 rows. Once the height outgrew the geometric stride, each
new band chose the widest stride that still fit its drawn height, so the next
band outgrew it again and re-laid the buffer out every six rows until the
16-canvas budget ran out. A band that only adds height now keeps the stride at
the drawn width, which leaves the most height headroom the width allows (6710
rows at 2500 columns), so the rectangle needs one re-layout. Widening bands
still step their stride geometrically, and alternating widen-and-deepen input
near the pixel cap is still refused at the budget. The previous entry's claim
that ordinary rectangular images were unaffected is corrected in place.
Platform-neutral decoder code, so Linux, macOS and Windows behave the same.

Validation: the new filled-rectangle test fails before the change (refused at
4188 rows) and needs about one canvas of re-layout work after it.

## 2026-10-11 -- Settings defaults, non-Latin key chords, float precision and concurrent saves

- `visual = off` (or `none`/`plain`) with no `crt` setting now turns the CRT
  scanline pass off, as the `visual` row describes. Since CRT became a default,
  an explicit `visual = off` had no effect. An explicit `crt` still wins, and
  with neither key set the CRT default applies. The row text and
  `docs/runtime-knobs.md` say so.
- Key bindings accept any single non-whitespace character, including
  non-Latin letters such as `ä` or `ф`. The config parser and the
  live key capture share one rule, so a captured chord always saves to a value
  that loads again; before, both refused anything outside ASCII.
- A float typed in Settings keeps up to four decimal places. Two places used to
  round `1.125` to `1.13` before it was applied and saved, and a change in the
  third decimal was ignored.
- The wallpaper-visibility row takes the value it displays for every caller of
  the settings edit layer; the conversion to the stored `cell_bg_opacity` no
  longer lives only in the native panel.
- Saving settings takes an advisory lock on `odytty.conf.lock` beside the file
  (`flock` on Unix, `LockFileEx` on Windows), so two OdyTTY processes saving
  different settings at once keep both changes. The first-run stub is written
  under the same lock and never replaces a config that exists by then. A file
  system without locking saves as before.
- A background image path that is not valid UTF-8 is kept byte for byte, like
  `font` and `symbol_font`, instead of becoming a missing file.
- The `default` token for `cursor_blink` and `render_quality` now selects the
  shipped default (`on`, `high`); it used to select `auto` and `balanced`.
  Seven boolean defaults that were bare literals in two places are now named
  constants, and the process-wide geometric box-drawing, synthetic-style and
  symbol-fallback switches start from those constants, so anything drawn
  before startup publishes the settings matches the shipped look (geometric
  box drawing was off until then).
- Quick-terminal settings are checked when read: pixel sizes are capped at
  16384px, a shortcut of more than 64 characters or with control characters
  falls back to `F12` with a warning, and a profile value that is not a valid
  profile name falls back to the default profile with a warning. A bare
  number is still a pixel size, now stated in the docs.
- `docs/profiles.md` states that a profile is trusted like `odytty.conf`: its
  theme, font and palette paths may name any readable file.
- Tests: every settings test that republishes render globals holds the shared
  lock (one did not), and a structural check keeps it that way; the key-binding
  round trip generator covers 144 actions and says so when outgrown; two
  profile tests no longer read the machine's real config and environment.
  `render_quality_parses_known_values_and_aliases` now expects `default` to
  select `high`.

Platforms: the lock and every other change apply on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Gate: fmt, clippy (Linux, pinned 1.96, Windows and macOS targets), RustSec,
production-file and documentation guards pass; the full suite passes at four
threads; the touched settings, bindings, settings-panel and render-globals
groups pass once serially and five times at four threads, with and without a
display.

## 2026-10-11 -- Profile documents use a strict JSON boundary and a save cannot exceed the read limit

Profile files are read through a strict variant of the small JSON reader. A raw
control character inside a string, a number outside the JSON grammar (leading
zero, bare sign or dot, empty exponent) or beyond a 64-bit float, and a repeated
object key at any depth are refused with a message naming the problem. Before,
such files loaded and were silently rewritten on the next save: the writer
replaced an out-of-range number with zero, owned fields used the first duplicate
while preserved fields kept the last, and a control character was escaped. The
lenient reader is unchanged for Hyprland replies and palette files. A profile
document larger than the 1 MiB file limit is refused in memory as well, so a
profile whose indented form grew past the limit can no longer be saved and then
refused on the next load. The module header and parse documentation are
corrected, and the 64-bit float precision limit for unknown integers is stated
in the profile guide.

Validation: six new tests fail before the change and pass after; the lenient
reader keeps accepting the same inputs.

## 2026-10-11 -- Profile field boundaries: device names, launch text, font size, null arrays, switch typos, case collisions

Profile names that Windows treats as devices (`con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`,
`com0`-`com9`, `lpt0`-`lpt9`, with or without an extension) are refused on
every platform, because profile files are portable and the device path
silently discards the save. A save is refused when another profile file in the
directory differs only by letter case, which on a case-insensitive volume would
overwrite it. A NUL in the shell, starting directory, command program or an
argument, and an empty shell or program, are refused when the file is read and
again before a local spawn, so a damaged file reports a precise error instead of
a generic OS failure. A font size of zero or less, or beyond 32-bit float range,
is refused, and a size is written as the decimal that was entered (13.3, not
13.300000190734863). An explicit `null` for a switch host or directory array is
absent, as for every sibling field. A switch rule that can never match (a host
with a space or path separator, a directory that is not absolute or contains
`..`) still loads and is reported as a catalog warning naming the entry. The
secret-key error now names the matching word. The substring rule is kept: a
false positive is recoverable, a stored credential is not.

Validation: nine new tests fail before the change and pass after.

## 2026-10-11 -- hosts.conf: byte order marks, non-UTF-8 files, writer and reader agree, cut reads

- A `hosts.conf` saved with a leading UTF-8 byte order mark, as some Windows
  editors write, no longer hides its first `Host` block: the reader, the edit
  and remove lookups and the append collision check all see it, and editing or
  removing a host keeps the mark.
- Editing or removing a host refuses a file that is not valid UTF-8 and leaves
  it untouched. It used to rewrite the whole file from a lossy decode, so an
  invalid byte in an unrelated comment or block became U+FFFD.
- The writer refuses what the reader would drop or change: an empty alias, a
  negated (`!`) or wildcard (`*`, `?`) alias, and port 0. An empty optional
  field is written as unset instead of a bare key. The reader accepts only
  digit ports from 1 to 65535, so `Port 0` and `Port +22` read as unset.
- The append collision check scans every block instead of the reader's first
  1,024 entries, so an alias saved past that cap is not appended twice.
- A file longer than the 256 KiB read limit keeps only the complete lines
  before the limit, so a value the limit cut (`HostName long.exa`) is never
  used as a connect target. A warning is logged.
- The inline tests moved to `src/connection_hosts/tests.rs` unchanged, keeping
  the production file well under the size limit.

Platforms: Linux, macOS and Windows share the hosts file code. The OpenSSH
config reader (`src/ssh_config.rs`) has its own parser and is unchanged here.

Gate: fmt, clippy (Linux, pinned 1.96, Windows and macOS targets), RustSec,
production-file and documentation guards pass; the connection-host and SSH
test groups pass once serially and five times at four threads, with and
without a display.

## 2026-10-11 -- Automation endpoint survives transient accept failures; busy endpoints are waited for

- Unix: an `accept` failure from descriptor exhaustion (`EMFILE`, `ENFILE`),
  buffer or memory pressure (`ENOBUFS`, `ENOMEM`) or an aborted client is
  retried at the poll interval instead of stopping the endpoint for good. A
  run of more than 100 in a row (about a second) still faults with the
  existing notice; every other error faults as before.
- Unix: a client whose connect finds the endpoint's accept queue full now
  retries until its connect deadline and then reports a timeout. It used to
  fail at once with `WouldBlock`, which discovery could not classify. The
  `EINPROGRESS` poll path, which Linux AF_UNIX does not take, is kept and
  documented as such.
- A mutation that failed before any request byte was written (the endpoint
  path, the connect including its timeout, or the owner check) is marked as
  not delivered and reported as `unavailable`, which is safe to retry. A
  failure after sending stays `outcome_unknown`. Linux, macOS and Windows share
  this classification.
- Windows: the control client opens the pipe with an identification-level
  security quality of service, so the pipe server can never impersonate it.
- Windows: `odytty control --help` no longer promises endpoint discovery;
  it states that every command, including `quick-terminal toggle`, needs
  `--endpoint`. Unix help is unchanged.
- Unix: a hand-built endpointless mutation is refused with `invalid_request`
  at run time instead of relying on a debug assertion.
- Tests: the expired-request dispatch test no longer expires the request
  through `wait` first, so it now fails if the dispatcher's own deadline check
  is removed; wire-level tests feed hostile names (newline, NUL, ESC, C1 CSI
  and OSC) and bad lengths straight to `decode` for all three name-carrying
  actions.

Gate: fmt, clippy (Linux, pinned 1.96, Windows and macOS targets), RustSec,
production-file and documentation guards pass; the automation test groups pass
once serially and five times at four threads, with and without a display.
Windows-only tests run on the Windows CI leg.

## 2026-10-11 -- SSH control sockets stay bindable under deep state paths

The `ControlMaster` socket template is `<dir>/ssh-%C`, and OpenSSH binds the
master to a name 17 bytes longer than the final one before renaming it. A state
directory deeper than the Unix-domain socket limit (107 bytes on Linux, 103 on
macOS, so on macOS a user name over ten characters) made every master listener
fail, and the tab either errored or ran without reuse. The directory is now
checked against that limit; one that does not fit falls back to a private
`/tmp/odytty-<uid>` directory validated the same way (real directory, owned by
the user, never a symlink, mode repaired through the opened handle), and reuse is
off only when neither path can be bound. Warnings do not name the paths. Unix
only: Windows has no `ControlMaster` surface.

Validation: the two new limit tests and the fallback test fail without the
check.

## 2026-10-11 -- Windows launches no longer resolve bare program names from the launch directory

`CreateProcessW` with a null application name searched the directory OdyTTY was
started from before the system directories, so a `cmd.exe` or `tool.exe` planted
there won over the real one. The last-resort command interpreter is now always
an absolute path (`%ComSpec%` when drive-absolute, else
`%SystemRoot%\System32\cmd.exe`), and a bare program name from a profile or
`--exec` is pinned to the absolute file found in the executable's directory,
`System32`, the Windows directory and `PATH` entries, in that order, never the
launch directory. A name found nowhere was left as given, which still let
`CreateProcessW` search the launch directory for it; such a name is now refused
(see the entry on unresolved program names). Dropping a session no longer waits
forever on a child that survives termination (an elevated or protected process): the wait is
bounded at one second and the child-waiter thread is detached instead of joined.
The resolution rules are tested on every platform; the spawn and the bounded
wait are exercised by the Windows CI leg only.

## 2026-10-11 -- PowerShell Enter keeps the user's named PSReadLine function

The PowerShell integration replaced the Enter binding with its own handler that
always accepted with `AcceptLine`, so Vi mode's `ViAcceptLine` or a profile's
`ValidateAndAcceptLine` was lost. The snippet now reads the existing Enter
binding before rebinding and runs that function where it ran `AcceptLine`. A
script-block binding has no callable name and is still replaced. The snippet
text is pinned by a structure test (binding read before the rebind, no hard-coded
accept, balanced delimiters); PowerShell itself is not run by the suite.

## 2026-10-11 -- The Windows symbol tail reaches Cambria Math

`cambria.ttc` holds the Cambria text face at index 0 and Cambria Math, the face
with the math and symbol coverage the tail exists for, at index 1, but every
static tail loaded face 0. A tail entry now names its face index: the Windows
tail loads Cambria Math, and the macOS tail records `Menlo.ttc` face 0
explicitly.

## 2026-10-11 -- Kitty file transports are covered by portable pipeline tests

The direct transport suite is Unix-only (shared memory, FIFOs, descriptors and
permission modes), so Windows had the transport-layer binding tests but no test
of what a program in the terminal sees. A portable suite now drives `t=f` and
`t=t` through the escape sequence on every platform: the default-off denial,
placement, deletion of a marked temp file, the unmarked-file refusal, the error
reply for a missing file, and the unsupported shared-memory reply off Unix.
The off-Unix reply and the Windows opened-handle checks run on the Windows CI
leg only.

## 2026-10-11 -- The tmux persistence setting states its one-session-per-host behavior

`remote_tmux` attaches every integrated tab to a host to the single tmux session
named `odytty`, so tabs to one host and remote user mirror each other and share
one cursor and scrollback. The setting description, the runtime knobs, the
feature guide and the specification now say so and point to the per-host
`Tmux off` line for hosts where tabs must be independent shells. No behavior
changes.

## 2026-10-11 -- Profile quarantine keeps earlier copies, Windows image dotfiles, deterministic catalog tests

Quarantining a malformed profile file renamed it over any existing `.bad` copy,
so a second malformed save of the same profile destroyed the first, which can be
the only record of the user's hand-edited text. The quarantine now takes the
first free name (`.bad`, `.bad.1`, and so on up to 100) and leaves the file in
place with an error once every name is taken. The image-offer gate picked the
last path segment by forward slash only, so a Windows path ending in a
backslash and a dot-only name such as `.png` kept its directory prefix as a
stem and was offered as an image; on Windows the last backslash now also
separates segments, and on Unix a backslash stays a file-name character. The
same-length in-place edit catalog test advances the modification time
explicitly instead of sleeping, and the read-only toggle test restores the
fixture's permissions through a drop guard so a failed assertion cannot leave a
read-only file behind. The catalog cache comment no longer promises that a
reused catalog always reflects the files on disk: it states that a detected
stamp change invalidates the cache on the next load.

Validation: both quarantine tests fail before the change. The Windows dotfile
case runs on the Windows CI leg only and the two catalog test changes are
hygiene without a Linux failing-before run.

## 2026-10-11 -- Export links need a closed IPv6 host, the path picker honors its width, and guarded paths gain coverage

A scrollback export link whose host opened an IPv6 bracket but did not close it
(`http://[::1/x`), held an empty literal (`http://[]/x`) or put text between the
closing bracket and the port (`https://[::1]junk/x`) was accepted as an `href`.
Only an `http` or `https` URL with a closed, non-empty literal followed by
nothing or a port becomes a link now; the rest stay text. The settings path
picker's rows were wider than a narrow body (the indent, marker and breadcrumb
were added after the name was cut); every row is now cut to the body width on a
character boundary.

Coverage added for guarded paths that had none: concealed (SGR 8) text in the
export, wide and combining search queries, a scoped search whose origin moved,
the search bar when its status is longer than the row, exact replay overlay
line counts, every pristine-workspace disqualifier (launch profile, zoom,
stacked or floating tab, remote, attached, profile-opened and read-only
panes), running foreground jobs in sibling panes and background workspaces, a
pane closed in a background floating workspace, the input-latch sweep, layout
invalidation, background timer parking, bell routing, notification attribution
and the scroll-baseline catch-up that skips an alternate screen. Assertions
that could not fail were replaced: an empty expert-group loop, an `all || any`
search predicate, a filtered-row edit that selected behind the search, a
hyperlink alias check that accepted a retarget as inert, a WSL entry loop that
passed on hosts without WSL, and the shell discovery cache check. The
navigator preview now has cases for secrets passed as the next word of an
option and for secrets that cross the display bound. The Homebrew tap notes
record the Homebrew version the quarantine step was checked with.

Not changed: the picker still lists real, non-hidden entries only (hidden
entries and symlinks stay unlisted, now pinned by tests), and a secret token
wrapped across two screen rows is judged per row because a snapshot carries no
wrap flags.

Validation: the two production changes and the sampled behaviors each fail
their new test when reverted.

## 2026-10-11 -- Decorations, cursor glyphs and presentation seams follow the glyph rules

Underline and strikethrough lines diverged from glyph ink in three ways. They
were not cropped at the pinned-chrome seam while content glided under the tab
bar, so a line could draw over the bar; a strikethrough ignored the label
centering shift an underline and glyph already followed; and an explicit
`SGR 58` underline colour passed the minimum-contrast floor but not the
`text_brightness` lift that the default underline got through the foreground.
All three now match the glyph path. On a bidirectional row the glyph under a
block cursor is cropped to its owner's visual span like every other glyph, so
overflow ink cannot cover a neighbouring owner. Dashed, dotted and curly
underlines emit at most 4096 segments and draw nothing for a non-finite width,
`strikethrough_rect` no longer panics for a cell shorter than its line, and
`CellRegion` and colour-run coverage saturate at the top of the index range
instead of overflowing.

The public presentation seams validate their input once. A snapshot that does
not hold a cell for every position its dimensions claim, or whose cell count
overflows, now draws nothing through the cell, cursor and colour-glyph builders
and maps nothing in the bidirectional plan, instead of indexing past its cells
or building vectors for dimensions it cannot back. A painted frame with a
different cell count than the planned one resets every reordered row. The
bidirectional paragraph-cap preflight and context-row bounds are unchanged: that
work is output-neutral and unmeasured, so it is not touched.

Test hardening in the same change: the bidirectional pixel fixtures hold the
render globals for each comparison, the cursor-off assertion compares against an
expected column instead of itself, and new fixtures cover repeated full scrolls,
every erase-display mode, RIS across both screens, a disabled image count and a
Sixel header that must not allocate a buffer.

Validation: the decoration, shape and cursor tests fail without the production
changes; Linux, macOS and Windows share the code, and the pixel assertions use
the bundled and project-authored fixture fonts only.

## 2026-10-11 -- Open With reader stays inside its cap; slow-drain attach fixture shuts down explicitly

The capped desktop-file reader for Open With could keep one byte more than its
cap. It reads one byte past the cap only to detect a longer file, but when that
byte was a newline it was kept as the end of a "complete" line, so a file with
no newline inside the cap could still be accepted. The extra byte is now
dropped before the last complete line is found, so the result never exceeds
the cap and a line whose newline falls just past it is dropped like any other
cut line. This path is Linux and other freedesktop Unix only; macOS and Windows
do not use it. New tests cover a newline exactly past the cap, a source whose
only newline is past the cap, and a control with the newline on the last byte
inside the cap.

The attach test that drains a large frame slowly now gives its reader a read
deadline and shuts the client endpoint down explicitly, including on unwind.
It keeps a second handle to that endpoint open on purpose, so the reader's end
of input cannot depend on the last copy of the socket being closed. The fake
host deadline comment now states that the bound applies to each accept and
each blocking operation, not to a host's whole lifetime. Unix only; Windows has
no attach surface.

Gate: fmt, clippy (Linux, pinned 1.96, Windows and macOS targets), RustSec,
production-file and documentation guards pass; the touched Open With and
attach test groups pass once serially and five times at four threads, with and
without a display.

## 2026-10-11 -- Wide-glyph seam pixel test runs on every host

The pixel test for a two-cell glyph inking across the cell seam searched the
loaded face for a CJK or fullwidth glyph and returned early when none existed.
The bundled face has none, so the test passed without checking anything on
every host. It now loads the project-authored bidi fixture face, whose
two-cell U+754C and block-pattern letters are redistributable under the
repository license, asserts the glyph has a real outline, and checks the quad
count, the ink inside the two-cell span, the neighbouring narrow cell, and the
blank cell past the span. The unused host-font search helper is removed.

Validation: the test fails when the wide codepoint is replaced by an unmapped
one, and before the change it printed a skip message and passed. The pixel
suite, formatting, lint, production and documentation guards pass.

## 2026-10-11 -- Shell discovery bounds and a panic-free local command builder

The Unix shell registry is read through a 64 KiB and 512-line budget, keeps at
most 256 candidates, and drops a line cut by the cap, all before any candidate
is probed; before, the whole file and every candidate were collected first and
only the final list was truncated. A bare shell name is looked up on `PATH`
only, so a copy of a shell in the launch directory no longer makes a candidate
appear, and the Windows extras no longer relabel a program that is already
listed as the default. `build_local_command` returns `None` for a default-shell
plan instead of reaching an `unreachable!`. The discovery tests inject
`SHELL` (set, empty and absent) instead of requiring the test environment to
define it, put platform gating on the test attribute so it cannot pass without
asserting anything, and describe the admission rule accurately: an absolute
path or an `.exe` name found on `PATH`, never anything executed. The env-only
default-shell spawn test reads through a 64 KiB capture cap and a 30-second
deadline and kills the child on every exit.

Validation: the working-directory lookup and the default-shell-plan tests fail
before the change; the injection and registry-bound tests exercise new seams
and have no failing-before run. The Windows relabel test runs on the Windows
CI leg only.

## 2026-10-11 -- CI fixes: platform float tolerance, validation fixture names and the X11 keyboard runtime

Three corrections to the preceding batch, found by its CI run. The test
pinning a saturated colour's hue-preserving contrast lift asserted exact
equality on a second application; the lift re-bisects on the same line, so a
second pass agrees only to float rounding, and the Windows math library
differed in the last bits. It now uses the 1e-5 tolerance of the sibling
idempotence test. The `scripts/ci-validation-test.py` fixture still listed the
original three GPU tests after the script grew to twenty; it now reads both
name lists from `scripts/ci-validation.sh` and pins their counts. Linux CI
installs `libxkbcommon-x11-0`, which winit loads when it initializes X11
keyboard support; without it the virtual-display proof child failed before
running any case. Event-loop proof failures now keep the child's stderr, so an
initialization panic names its cause. In `src/native/gpu/image.rs` two test
doc comments are back on their own tests. No production behaviour changed.

Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS,
the colour and background-image test groups once one at a time and five times
at four threads, the four Python CI fixture suites, the virtual-display proof
under an isolated headless compositor (59 executed, 0 unavailable), and the
production and documentation guards passed. The validation fixture fails
before its change; the Windows float result and the Xvfb run are established
by CI.

## 2026-10-11 -- Linux CI requires virtual-display test execution

Linux CI now provisions Xvfb and xauth and runs the proxy-backed App and
session proof on a private display. The runner requires a positive executed
population and zero unavailable cases, preserves assertion failures and rejects
missing or duplicated proof output. Local runs can use isolated headless Weston
when Xvfb is absent. The ordinary full-suite step remains unchanged. macOS
proxy-backed cases retain their main-thread limitation and remain ignored;
Windows retains its native event-loop coverage. An earlier infrastructure
entry is corrected to describe passing contract checks without claiming
failing-before evidence.

Validation: four offline runner contract checks pass, including rejected empty,
mixed and duplicated proof output and preserved Cargo failure. Five isolated
Weston runs each report 59 executed cases and zero unavailable cases. The
workflow passes Actionlint with ShellCheck, and the runner passes ShellCheck.
The change touches no Rust code; formatting, production and documentation
guards and the Python tooling tests pass on the combined tree.

## 2026-10-11 -- Saturated colours keep their hue, shell history recall, explicit font files, Windows spawn environment and test hardening

The entries below landed together. They fix defects found by a full read of the
codebase. The minimum-contrast floor no longer fades saturated colours toward
the same near-white: red and magenta keep their hue as in v0.16.1. Shell history
recall decodes fish escapes and reads zsh multi-line and non-ASCII entries.
Config, theme and profile files saved with a byte order mark load. An explicit
`font` file supplies every style. GPU start-up keeps a software adapter when
the last backend stage cannot present. Windows profile variables replace the
terminal's own, and Windows spawns refuse interior NUL characters. Kitty
keyboard reports, empty launch-flag values, external palette keys, highlights
over legacy emoji sequences, failed-command jumps after history eviction,
copy-mode word end on wide glyphs, Open With labels and Windows network path
probes are corrected, and the test suites gain stronger oracles, hermetic
fixtures and CI enforcement of GPU and Python tooling tests.

Validation: the full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting,
pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,507 tests with none
failing both one at a time and four at a time, every test target five more
times at four threads, the Python tooling tests, production and documentation
guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. Each fix has a test that fails before
it, except where an entry says otherwise.

## 2026-10-11 -- Saturated colours keep their hue at the contrast floor

The displayed-colour contrast check reduced chroma along a ladder when a
saturated colour could not reach the floor by lightness alone. At the shipped
17:1 floor that turned most ANSI colours on the default theme near-white: red
and magenta both ended close to the same off-white. Chroma is no longer
reduced. A colour that already meets the floor as displayed is still returned
byte-for-byte, and a colour that a full-chroma lightness step can lift to the
floor as displayed still moves by the smallest such step (yellow, blue and
white on the default theme now reach 17:1 where the earlier float search left
them between 16:1 and 17:1). A saturated colour whose lightness alone cannot reach the floor
keeps the hue-preserving lift and may draw below the floor; red, magenta,
green and cyan on the default theme draw between 12:1 and 16:1 at 17:1. Where
no colour can reach the floor against the background, pure black or white is
still used. The accessibility guide, specification and TODO say plainly that
saturated colours can stay below the floor, and the sibling documentation for
theme generation, CVD adaptation and the theme author now names the function
they actually call. Tests pin hue retention for the default theme's saturated
roles, equality with the unclamped lift where lightness cannot reach the floor,
and the earlier byte-identical and idempotence properties. The first two fail
on the previous behaviour.
Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,453 tests passed, 34 ignored, none failing), the touched library groups (265 tests) five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards, RustSec and the fuzz API check passed.

## 2026-10-11 -- Shell history formats, byte order marks, host wildcards, spawn environment and NUL checks

The command palette's fish history reader followed a YAML layout fish does not
write. fish stores each command on one `- cmd:` line with a backslash written
as `\\` and a newline as `\n`, and does not quote the value. The reader now
decodes those two escapes and keeps quote characters as typed, so a recalled
command with a backslash, an escaped space or several lines inserts the text
that was run; `"a" "b"` no longer loses its outer quotes. The block-scalar path
and its invented fixture are gone. The zsh reader now joins a multi-line
command, which zsh writes as physical lines ending in an unescaped backslash,
into one entry, and restores metafied bytes before UTF-8 decoding, so
characters such as U+0118 no longer show as replacement characters. The
existing tests `fish_history_reads_cmd_rows_when_present` (its quoted row) and
`fish_history_accepts_multiline_blocks_and_truncated_blocks` (replaced) were
changed to the real fish format.

Config, theme, profile and external palette files saved with a leading UTF-8
byte order mark, as some Windows editors and PowerShell write, now load: the
bounded file reader removes one leading mark. A settings write-back to such a
file saves it without the mark.

A profile auto-switch host wildcard such as `*x` no longer panics when the
pattern length ends inside a multi-byte character of the host name; the
suffix comparison works on bytes.

On Linux, the file-drop eligibility scan treated a launch-group member in a
lowercase proc state, such as `t` for a traced process, as unreadable and
skipped it, so a group holding a debugged job could look like the shell alone.
Lowercase states are now read, and a stat entry that cannot be parsed refuses
the drop like every other hidden entry.

`odytty -e`, session-host commands and one-shot shell commands now start from
the same environment as a profile command on Linux, macOS and Windows: the
terminal variables plus removal of an inherited `ODYTTY_SHELL_INTEGRATION`,
which would make an integration snippet in the child skip its body. The
buttons documentation now says that the `ODYTTY_BUTTONS` advertisement is set
for shell sessions and that directly run commands inherit OdyTTY's own value.

On Windows, a spawn whose program, arguments, working directory or environment
contains an interior NUL is refused. `CreateProcessW` would otherwise read the
command line only up to the NUL and start the program with the remaining
arguments silently dropped.

The theme file module and field documentation said an omitted `clear` follows
the theme's `background`; it keeps the `plain` baseline, as docs/themes.md
states. The documentation now says so and a test pins it.

Validation: Each behavior fix has a test that fails before it. The Windows NUL
and one-shot environment tests were not run locally; they run only on the
Windows CI leg.

## 2026-10-11 -- GPU bring-up, explicit font files, image decode bounds and diagnostics wording

GPU bring-up kept a software adapter found at an earlier backend stage so a
machine whose only renderer is a software one still starts, but a surface
failure at the last stage returned an error without using it. It now falls
back to that adapter. A surface that reports no formats at all is a
surface-creation error instead of a panic.

An explicit `font` file now supplies every style. Before, the bold and italic
faces were still searched by `font_family`, which defaults to the bundled
family name, so a host-installed copy of that family mixed into the text, and
a set `font_weight` replaced the explicit file with a face of the family. Bold
and italic are now synthesized from the explicit file, as SPEC.md and the
runtime knob table state. A family resolved to a file is unchanged.

A background image whose file was removed or became unreadable after loading
was re-read on every resize event during a window drag, each time logging a
warning. The first failed re-read is now remembered, the current texture is
kept, and loading the image again from settings starts over.

An image placement whose display size in cells exceeds the 32-bit range now
saturates instead of wrapping to a small or zero extent.

The keyboard diagnostics gate was described as privacy-safe. Physical key
codes are recorded unredacted, so a log taken while typing contains the
keystrokes; the source comment and docs/diagnostics.md now say so and ask for
the log to be reviewed before it is shared.

Tests: the image decoder's size and allocation limits are now proven with
complete, valid PNG images through all four decode entry points, with a
control at the axis limit; the earlier header-only fixture failed on missing
image data whether or not the limits applied. The explicit symbol font test
now checks that the explicit face is first in the chain, and the font fixture
directories are removed on failure. The device-loss latch test, which could
only exercise an atomic load, was removed and the latch documentation names
the guarded paths. The image layer's scrollback-named test, which repeated
the plain row arithmetic, is replaced by a test of the content gap shift.

Validation: Each behavior fix has a test that fails before it.

## 2026-10-11 -- Windows profile variables replace terminal variables, attach timeout policy and pump test bounds

On Windows, a profile `env` entry naming a variable the terminal already sets,
such as `TERM`, reached the ConPTY environment block twice, once with each
value. The block now carries one entry per case-insensitive name, holding the
last value set, so the profile value wins as it does on Unix. A
Windows-only unit test covers a repeated name in two cases; it runs only on
the Windows CI leg.

Attaching to a detached session refused the attach when its write timeout
could not be set, except for any invalid-input error, which was accepted on
every Unix target with no proof that the host had gone. The attach client now
uses the session host's socket-option policy: raw EINVAL is accepted only on
macOS and only when a nonblocking poll shows the host end hung up; every other
failure refuses the attach. The attach pump test fixture now shuts the host
end down instead of only dropping it, keeps an alias open to prove that, and
bounds the wait for the pump to reach end of file.

Validation: The attach timeout tests fail before the attach change. The
Windows block test was not run locally; it runs only on the Windows CI leg.

## 2026-10-11 -- Failed-command jumps survive eviction, copy-mode word end, Open With labels and test oracles

- Jump To Previous/Next Failed Command anchors in rows counted from the first
  row ever pushed into history, so output that evicts rows from capped history
  no longer leaves the anchor naming shifted content: the next previous steps
  to the earlier failure instead of finding the same one again. A view that
  stays at the live bottom keeps its anchor as output arrives. The test fails
  before the change.
- Copy-mode `e` over a run of wide glyphs rests on the lead cell of the last
  glyph instead of its trailing spacer, so `v` `y` copies that glyph; a second
  `e` moves on to the next word. Caret placement on off-screen rows is
  unchanged. The updated motion test fails before the change.
- Open With labels turn decoded `\t`, `\n` and `\r` in `Name` into spaces, so
  `Fixture\sViewer\tPanel` reads as three words rather than merging at display.
  The test fails before the change.
- The connection form key browser takes a path's final component with the
  platform's own separators: on Unix a backslash is part of a filename, on
  Windows both separators split. The Unix case fails before the change.
- Test oracles: the ZWJ restyle fixture requires the head run to be drawn
  (`[(0,2),(2,2)]`); status filtering in failed-command navigation has its own
