  fixture with a nearer successful command and an unmarked one, and the old
  test is named for what it checks; the pane pointer round trip samples the
  first and last pixel of every cell, so an ignored grid origin shifts a
  column; the config-reload wake test uses a path in a directory it owns; the
  font-size environment variable in the launch-inputs test is restored by a
  guard that also runs on unwind, with its own unwind test.
Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,453 tests passed, 34 ignored, none failing), the touched library groups (265 tests) five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards, RustSec and the fuzz API check passed.

## 2026-10-11 -- A highlight over part of a legacy emoji sequence no longer splits it

A flag pair, skin-tone modifier or joiner sequence that arrives as separate
cells joins into one color glyph only while the cells share a rendition. The
comparison read the frame's cells after the selection, search and copy-mode
passes had recoloured them, so a selection or match covering one half of such a
sequence split the glyph and rejoined it when the highlight cleared. Those
passes now mark the cells they recolour, and the join compares only stored
attributes and hyperlink while either cell is marked. Two unmarked cells with
different stored colours still stay separate.

## 2026-10-11 -- Kitty key reports keep every character when the generated text is rejected

A composed key such as `e` followed by a combining acute accent is reported as
one event only when the event's generated text is actually admitted. Text the
encoder refuses (empty, or containing a control character) and every release
carry no associated text, so those events fell back to the identity of the
first character and dropped the rest. They now report each logical character,
the same as an event with no generated text. Windows, Linux X11, Linux
Wayland and macOS share this routing.

## 2026-10-11 -- Kitty key codes keep unshifted punctuation, and Win32 back-tab carries Shift

Kitty key reports folded shifted punctuation to its US-layout key even when
Shift was not held, so a `+` typed on a key of its own was reported as `=`. The
fold now applies only while Shift is held, and an unshifted punctuation
character keeps its own code and adds no shifted or base-layout field. Win32
input mode reported a back-tab with no redundant Shift modifier as a plain Tab
record; a back-tab now always carries the Shift bit. SPEC describes the Kitty
rule.

## 2026-10-11 -- Empty profile and working-directory values are refused in every spelling

`--profile ""`, `--working-directory ""` and `--working-dir ""` were accepted
and failed later with a less direct error, while the `--profile=` and
`--app-id` spellings already refused an empty value. Every spelling of a flag
that names something (a profile, a directory, an application id) now rejects an
empty value with the same message, for the launch command and for `odytty new`.
`--title` still accepts an empty value in both spellings: a blank window title
is a valid request, and a test pins it.

## 2026-10-11 -- External palettes refuse malformed color keys, and fixtures clean up after failures

A palette file that repeated a required color, or gave a color key an empty or
malformed value, kept its earlier value or fell back to Base16 without a word,
which contradicts the stated refusal of malformed sources. Keys the flat
providers read as colors (`foreground`, `background`, `color0` to `color15`,
`base00` to `base0F`, the named `colors.toml` stops and the rest of the
documented set) now fail the whole file when they repeat or hold no color.
Other keys stay metadata. Only `_` and `-` are folded away when matching a key,
so a key such as `colo r3` can no longer collide with `color3`.

The emoji discovery, SVG discovery and palette-follower tests now create their
fixtures in an exclusively created directory that is removed even when an
assertion fails, instead of writing timestamp-named paths and cleaning up by
hand.

## 2026-10-11 -- Integration test oracles read the production code and report what they skip

- `tests/cli.rs` ran only on Unix as a whole file. The output-format, usage,
  session-argument-parser and `--show-config` binary tests now run on Windows
  as well (the binary run redirects `APPDATA` and drops only `ODYTTY_*`
  variables); only the session host, attach and list cases stay Unix-only, per
  test, and the file header states the Windows behavior.
- Sixel bounds: fixtures declare a raster one row over the pixel budget
  (refused with `TooLarge`), exactly at it (decodes), and over an axis cap
  (clamped), so raising or removing the decoder cap fails. The old fixture's
  40,000,000 literal now matches the real 16,777,216 budget.
- The emoji and box-drawing pixel suites use the compiled-in text face, so the
  tests that never needed an emoji font are never skipped, and the ones that do
  print a countable `odytty-test-outcome: unavailable-hardware` line. The
  box-drawing off-path test now compares the two atlases it builds.
- Theme files: a `name` or `font_family` containing a whitespace-preceded `#`
  serialized to text that re-parsed to a shorter value, and a value with a line
  break could start another key. Serialization now writes such values the way
  the format reads them back (a `#` after whitespace ends the value, control
  characters become spaces), and a `font_size` hint is clamped to 6-72 when
  parsed. The round trip is checked for every parsed built-in, light and dark,
  not only the four const themes.
- Mirrors of production code: the image-placement projection used by the
  graphics compositor test is now the renderer's own (`odytty::native::
  image_placement_rect_uv`, hidden from the documentation) instead of a copy.
  The CRT model compared nothing with the shader and had drifted: it clamped
  scanline intensity at 0.18 and vignette strength at 0.16 while the shader and
  the settings allow 0.35 and 0.45. A guard test now reads the shader source and
  the settings caps and fails when the model's constants, clamp bounds,
  smoothstep edges or dither table differ; the lightbox scrim alpha copy is
  checked against the production constant the same way.
- Sixel pixel test: a decoder failure or an empty image fails the test instead
  of being reported as a skip.
- Pixel suites: `setup()` returns the bundled font and atlas directly (about
  forty-five dead "no system font" skip arms removed). GPU smoke tests report a
  missing adapter and an adapter without the HDR format as the same countable
  unavailable-hardware outcome instead of passing silently or failing.
- The license-header check covers Rust and WGSL in the build script, its
  support module and the fuzz crates, and Python under `scripts/`,
  `.github/scripts/` and `tests/`; two release scripts gained the header. The
  installer plan test no longer uses a fixed temporary directory name, checks
  the macOS, Windows-shell, unknown-system and unsupported-architecture
  branches through a stub `uname`, and rejects any spelling of an apt-get
  fix-broken fallback.
Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,459 tests passed, 34 ignored, none failing), the touched library groups and the touched integration suites (command line, Sixel regressions, emoji, box drawing, pixel smoke, GPU composite smoke, installer plan and license headers) five more times at four threads, the production and documentation guards, RustSec and the fuzz API check passed.

## 2026-10-11 -- A control socket that cannot be identified after binding is removed

Binding the Unix control endpoint creates the socket file before the guard that
removes it exists. If reading that file's metadata failed in between, the file
stayed behind and every later bind at the same path failed with `AddrInUse`.
The bind now removes the file it just created when it cannot identify it.
Unix-only; Windows uses a named pipe and has no socket file.

## 2026-10-11 -- Sixel default-mode placement, clamp and eviction have exact tests again

The Sixel routing tests for store limits, eviction and display-row clamping ran
only with display mode (DECSDM) set after an earlier tightening. Default
(scrolling) mode twins now pin the same limits with exact anchors, extents,
visible placements and the cursor row after each image, including the clamp
at the bottom of a short screen. Test-only.

## 2026-10-11 -- Animation frame keys, parked history and wide-owner boundaries follow their contracts

Kitty animation commands read `c=` and `r=` as frame indices. A value past
4294967295 is a valid placement extent but no frame index, and was read as an
omitted key, so `a=a`, `a=f`, `a=c` and `d=f` ran a different command than the
one sent. They are now refused with a malformed-control error. Restoring an
alternate-screen snapshot now gives the parked primary history the host's
scrollback limit and width policy, so returning to the primary screen no longer
reverts them. Trimming the parked history releases its button references at
once. A command-end boundary stamped on a wide owner's continuation cell follows
the owner head when the owner degrades to one column, in both the eager and the
lazy reflow. The malformed-control Kitty test now pairs each defect with an
otherwise valid payload and pins the exact reply, and the macOS shared-memory
fixture takes ownership of its descriptor and name before sizing it.

## 2026-10-11 -- One availability policy for headless GPU tests

Sixteen library tests that need a GPU adapter returned early when none was
available, several without any message, so on a runner without an adapter
they counted as passed; one helper also skipped image tests on adapters that
lack an HDR render target although only the bloom test needs one. Every
library headless GPU test now acquires its device through one helper. Without
a usable adapter it prints a named `VALIDATION_SKIP` line; with
`ODYTTY_REQUIRE_GPU_TESTS` set it fails instead and uses the software Vulkan
adapter. Only the bloom test requires an HDR target. The Linux CI validation
step now requires an execution marker from each of these tests, in addition
to the three shader tests and the shell round trips it already required.
CONTRIBUTING.md lists the three GPU tests that keep their own setup and why.

The background scrim tests set the process-wide minimum-contrast floor and
reset it to 1.0 at the end, which a failed assertion skipped; a guard now
restores the previous value on every exit. A background test that repeated
the resampler's size check under an image-layer name was removed, and a test
that searched the composite shader's source text for the dither call, which
could not fail for a dither applied to the wrong value, was removed; the
shader is still compiled and validated by the bloom pipeline test.

Validation: With the requirement set and no Vulkan driver visible, the
converted tests fail where they previously passed silently.

## 2026-10-11 -- Protocol fuzzing covers cluster owners; tooling and host-dependent tests made hermetic

- The protocol fuzzer's grid invariant was the pre-grapheme width model (a
  spacer follows a head whose first scalar is width 2), which is false for the
  owners the project now ships: a Devanagari conjunct, a Thai or Lao SARA AM, a
  VS16 or keycap sequence and a ZWJ or flag cluster each own two columns under a
  width-1 first scalar. It never tripped only because no generator emitted such
  input. The scan is now owner-aware (a spacer follows a head that owns two
  columns, carries no text and never follows padding or a spacer; layout padding
  is a plain blank; no cell keeps more than 16 extension scalars) and the wide
  seed generator draws from 31 multi-scalar owners taken from the Brahmic, Thai,
  Lao, Tibetan and emoji fixtures. With the previous invariant the new
  generators fail four smoke fuzzers; with the owner-aware one the smoke tier
  passes and a 20,000-iteration run of the mixed, rectangle, wide-slice and
  copy-churn fuzzers reports no failure.
- `tests/python_security_tooling.py` ran nowhere. It is now a CI step, and the
  workflow policy test fails when any Python test file under `tests/`,
  `scripts/` or `.github/scripts/` is named by no workflow step.
- The stem-darkening raster proof restores the process-global strength with a
  drop guard (also when a build panics), takes over a lock poisoned by an
  earlier failure so the first failure is the one reported, and drops its dead
  "no system font" skips; the pixel-smoke header no longer claims a host-
  dependent font and lists every module.
- The `git archive` provenance test runs git with a cleared environment, no
  system or global configuration, a fixed home and fixed identities, so
  commit signing, hooks, templates, attribute overrides or `core.autocrlf` on
  the machine cannot change the archive; a test pins that the fixture
  repository sees only its own configuration. Its skip is a countable token.
- The PTY alternate-screen smoke tests clear `LESS`, `LESSOPEN`, `LESSCLOSE`,
  `LESSCHARSET`, `LESSKEY` and `PAGER` before running `less` (a developer's
  `LESS=-X` disables the alternate screen), and every test prints which host
  program it executed or found unavailable as a fixed line.
Validation: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the full suite one test at a time and four at a time (7,448 tests passed, 34 ignored, none failing), the touched integration suites (protocol fuzz, provenance, stem raster, PTY alternate screen) and the two Python test files five more times at four threads, a 20,000-iteration deep run of the mixed, rectangle, wide-slice and copy-churn fuzzers, the production and documentation guards, RustSec and the fuzz API check passed.

## 2026-10-11 -- Session host launch arguments keep their documented zero-size refusal under test

`--cols 0` and `--rows 0` are refused where the argument is read, and the
defaults are nonzero, so the later dimension check was redundant; a test now
pins both. The incompatible-version rejection no longer sits between the
fairness-budget helper and its comment. Unix-only.

## 2026-10-11 -- Retained-memory wording matches its table, and a Windows link test joins the transport suite

The benchmark page said OdyTTY's retained memory on SE1 sits between Alacritty
and Kitty. The table puts it third of four, above Kitty and below Ghostty; only
its peak and after-settle medians sit between those two. The sentence now says
so. The Kitty file-transport tests also gain a Windows case that plants a
symbolic link inside the admitted root and requires the outside file not to be
delivered; it skips with a printed reason where the account cannot create
links.

## 2026-10-11 -- Corrections to the SVG refusal rationale, GPU loss recovery wording and Windows path probes

Documents with patterns, filters, masks or markers stay refused, but the stated
reason was wrong: OpenType leaves support for them optional rather than
forbidding them. Comments and docs now say the refusal comes from their raster
cost falling outside the budget model. The GPU device-loss log line and the
diagnostics and decomposition pages claimed every backend needs a restart of
OdyTTY; Vulkan, Metal and DX12 lose the device per window, so a window that is
closed and reopened renders again, and only OpenGL, which shares one device,
needs the restart.

On Windows, a path in terminal output that starts with two separators (a network
share or a device) no longer reaches the interactive path hover probe or the
canonicalizing call that admits a Kitty named-transport path, so neither contacts
a host the output chose. The profile form says a refused network or device
directory is not allowed instead of reporting it missing. The refusal check is
tested on every platform with the Windows rule injected; the Kitty case runs on
the Windows leg only.

## 2026-10-10 -- SSH aliases, form deletion, session host, Sixel, attach, Open With and notice corrections

The entries below landed together. They fix defects found by a full read of the
codebase: OpenSSH alias routing and POSIX remote startup, whole-character
deletion in form values, connection form chip, signature and picker fixes, a
Sixel re-layout work bound, one write budget per attach frame, session host
resize, launch-argument, metadata and runtime-directory hardening, profile
`HOME` handling for integrated zsh, bounded attach test hosts, a GPU isolation
test correction, Settings palette and notice repaint fixes, and complete-line
reads of capped desktop entries.

Validation: the full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting,
pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,446 tests with none
failing both one at a time and four at a time, the whole library test suite
plus the protocol fuzz and cross-script suites five more times at four
threads, production and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all
passed. Each fix has a test that fails before it, except where an entry says
otherwise.

## 2026-10-10 -- SSH aliases preserve routing and remote startup uses POSIX sh

Imported OpenSSH entries now use their alias for connections, probes, image
uploads and cleanup, leaving user, port, identity and routing resolution to
OpenSSH. Owned entries keep explicit overrides. Connection probes distinguish
rejected authentication, offered interactive methods, untrusted keys and
changed keys without promising that credentials will succeed.

Remote integration and upload commands enter quoted POSIX sh through the
login shell, including fish. Remote Bash loads the system profile and first
readable login file before its interactive configuration; local Bash startup
is unchanged. Upload noclobber protects existing files independently of the
login shell. Linux, macOS and Windows share the argument builders; Windows
connection multiplexing remains disabled.

The security integration check pins both quoting layers of the remote upload
command; the executable fish test confirms noclobber preserves existing files.
Ten original regressions fail before the changes, with the local Bash control
passing.

## 2026-10-10 -- Form-value Backspace removes complete terminal owners

Profile and connection form values, Settings value edits, and Theme Builder
save names now remove the complete final terminal text owner on Backspace.
Combining marks, bounded script clusters, flags and emoji ZWJ sequences stay
with their base. Settings still clears a selected prefill on the first
Backspace. Theme Builder keeps its ASCII name typing and ASCII hex color/seed
policies; captured names can retain Unicode from the active theme and now
remove it by owner. Save validation and terminal input encoding are unchanged.
The same handlers serve Linux Wayland, Linux X11, macOS and Windows. Both
forms keep tests in test-only module files, leaving their production sources
below 1,700 lines without changing test access or behavior.

Six regressions fail with scalar deletion, including Settings and captured-name
App input/signature checks. The ASCII input policy control passes before the
change.

## 2026-10-10 -- Connection form chip, signatures and tagged pickers

The IdentityFile row's `[Browse]` click target was found by searching the
drawn, possibly clipped text for `[Browse`, so a key path containing that text
could be mistaken for the chip when the real chip was cut off. The target now
comes from the same layout the row is drawn from, and stays a target while its
`[Browse` opening is fully drawn. The key browser's render signature ignored
its candidate names, and the connection picker's ignored the drawn theme and
source tags and the picker purpose, so a replacement that kept the query,
selection and row count could keep stale pixels; all three now take part. The
saved-host pickers opened to bind a workspace, connect from a tab or replace a
tab still honoured the manager's Tab (Add) and Right (Edit) keys, which left
the picker and dropped its pending action; they are now inert there. A host
with no `HostName` and a user or port showed `user@` or `:port` with no host;
it shows its alias, which is what ssh connects to. The "No matches" line is cut
to a narrow body like the rest. Same behavior on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation: six regressions for these changes fail before them.

## 2026-10-10 -- Sixel row re-layouts are bounded per image

After the row stride was tightened for a tall image, every added column of a
widening band chose the exact drawn width again and re-laid the whole buffer
out. A payload of a few hundred bytes (a 2500-column band, 699 new lines, then
a 3994-column band) caused about 1,500 near-64 MiB allocations and copies while
its drawn image stayed inside the pixel cap. A widening band now steps its
stride geometrically up to the widest stride the drawn height leaves room for,
and each DCS may allocate at most 16 canvases of pixels for row re-layouts in
total. An image that keeps reshaping the buffer past that budget, for example
bands that alternately widen and deepen near the pixel cap, is refused with
`TooLarge`. (The first version of this change also refused a filled
2500x4200 rectangle, because height growth kept choosing the widest stride and
re-laid the buffer out every band; the next entry corrects that.) Tests count the pixels allocated by re-layouts, not time: the
3994-column payload needs at most four canvases (about 25 billion pixels
before), and the alternating payload stops at the budget. Platform-neutral
decoder code, so Linux, macOS and Windows behave the same.

Validation: both new re-layout budget tests fail before the change.

## 2026-10-10 -- Native attach frame writes share one absolute budget

Input, resize and detach frames written to a hosted session relied on a
per-write socket timeout that was set once at attach time. A host that kept draining a little at a time let every
individual write make progress, so a large paste could hold the shared client
lock, and with it the interface thread during a resize, for as long as the
paste took. Each frame now runs against one absolute budget through the same
deadline writer the control client uses, and a failure to arm each write's
timeout is reported. A frame that cannot start inside the budget is
dropped and counted exactly as before; a frame cut short mid-way poisons the
client so nothing is written onto the desynced stream. The idle resize retry
still never waits. Attach is Unix-only (Linux and macOS); Windows has no attach
socket, so nothing changes there.

Validation: the slow-drain test (a one-mebibyte input to a host that drains a few kilobytes per tick) fails on the previous code, where the frame completes after the pace of the host, and passes now with a truncated-write error and a poisoned client; a draining-host control still delivers the same frame whole.

## 2026-10-10 -- Session host survives a PTY resize error and reads its launch arguments in any order

A client resize that raced the hosted child's exit, or a transient window-size
ioctl error, ended the session host and the shell with it, although the
child-exit path would have reported the real end with its exit code and final
output. The host now logs a PTY resize failure and keeps running; the terminal
model and every client's mirror still change together, and the next resize
repeats the PTY call. Launch arguments no longer depend on flag order: a working
directory given before `--exec` was dropped when the exec argument replaced the
command, and one given before `--shell-command` was silently discarded while
the reverse order was refused. The directory now applies to whichever
command the arguments select, `--shell-command` with a directory is refused in
either order, and arguments after `--exec` still belong to the child. A client
whose protocol or snapshot versions differ from the host's is told which side
holds which version and that the session stays usable from the version that
started it; SPEC states the upgrade limitation. Unix-only (Linux and macOS);
Windows has no session host.

Validation: seven new tests across the session-host changes in this and the
next two entries fail before them. The input-framing control passes before and
after, and the close-on-exec race has no deterministic test.

## 2026-10-10 -- Session metadata pane counts are clamped and the host input framing contract is stated

A session metadata file could report any `pane_count` up to the largest
`usize`, which flowed unchanged into the session list. The value is now
clamped to 1 through 4096 on read. The host's PTY input queue drops whole
chunks under overflow and one input frame is one chunk, so a bracketed paste
sent as one frame is kept or dropped entire; the module documents that contract
and a test pins it, including that a dropped paste leaves neither marker behind.
OdyTTY's own clients already send a bracketed paste as one frame, so no
behavior changes for them. Unix-only; Windows has no session host.

## 2026-10-10 -- Session runtime directory is created private and never chmod'ed through a link

Preparing the runtime directory created it with the umask default, changed its
mode by path, and only then validated it, so a link planted at the directory
name (possible only when the runtime base is a shared writable directory) had
its target's mode changed before validation refused it, and a fresh directory
was briefly readable by others. The leaf is now created with mode 0700 in the
same call, an existing directory is narrowed only through a handle opened with
`O_NOFOLLOW | O_DIRECTORY` and owned by the current user, and validation stays
the final authority. The client socket used to attach to a session host is
created close-on-exec atomically on Linux, so a process spawned on another
thread cannot inherit a connected attach socket and keep the host from seeing
the client leave; macOS has no atomic form and still sets the flag right after
creation. Unix-only; Windows has no session host.

## 2026-10-10 -- Integrated zsh reads startup files from a profile's HOME

A profile that set `HOME` reached the child's environment, but zsh shell
integration had already recorded the inherited `HOME` as the directory to
source startup files from, because both Unix spawn paths applied the profile
environment after integration. With no inherited `ZDOTDIR`, an integrated zsh
under such a profile read the parent's dotfiles. Both paths, the profile shell
command and the default shell, now share one helper that places a profile
`HOME` before integration runs and still applies every profile value last, so
a profile value continues to win over the discovery variables. A profile
`ZDOTDIR` still replaces the wrapper as before. Correction: the 2026-10-10
entry "Shells started by tests get their own home folder" and its source
comment described the integration hand-off as honoring a profile `HOME`
override; at that point it did not. Windows is unchanged: PowerShell
integration reads no environment, and the early copy is Unix-only so no key
reaches the ConPTY block twice.

Validation: two new tests, one per spawn path, fail with the early `HOME` placement removed; a control pins that a profile `ZDOTDIR` still wins.

## 2026-10-10 -- Attach tests bound their fake hosts and check what they name

The in-process fake session hosts in the attach tests accepted and read with
no deadline, and several tests joined their threads without a bound, so a
client that never connected or never detached could hold a test, or a session
close waiting on its pump, open indefinitely. Each fake host now accepts and
reads under a 10 second deadline and drops its stream when it passes, every
helper thread is joined under the existing bounded join, and the runtime
folders the hosts bind in are removed afterwards instead of being left under
the temporary directory. The two "Replace current" tests now drive the App's
Replace route instead of repeating its steps on the workspace set; that route
takes its attach step as a parameter so the tests run the production close
and focus. The live attached-tab test restores a snapshot with real history
rows and checks them, instead of claiming full scrollback over a screen with
none. The detach-and-switch tests keep their workspace-set scope, which their
section names. Test-only; the Replace route itself is unchanged.

Validation: the two new fake-host deadline tests fail with the old blocking accept and no stream deadlines, and both App Replace tests fail with the route's close step removed. Attach is Unix-only; these tests do not build on Windows.

## 2026-10-10 -- GPU isolation checks preserve presentation descriptor admission

The owner-isolation test now uses independent injectable owners instead of
seeding the live presentation cache with a headless descriptor. It checks
separate instance identities and preserves the first admitted presentation
policy on reuse. Existing headless instance reuse and full GPU lifetime guards
remain unchanged. This is a test-build correction on Linux, macOS and Windows;
the shipped adapter startup path is unchanged.

Validation: the live-owner regression failed before the correction, detecting one unexpected cache admission.

## 2026-10-10 -- Settings palette status change opens the frame gate

The repaint request added for a followed-palette status change did not mark
the frame for rebuild, so an idle window still skipped the rebuild and the
Settings status row stayed stale until other input. The status sync now marks
the frame for rebuild together with the repaint request, for every caller
(follower poll, arming, Settings changes and keyboard-driven refresh). The App
regression now closes the frame gate first and asserts the gate opens on a
status change and stays closed on a poll that changes nothing.

The colour-emoji default-presentation table now records its policy in the
source: the pictographic-plane ranges are deliberate whole blocks, a superset
of Emoji_Presentation=Yes, with scalars that no colour face covers drawn
through the monochrome fallback; below those planes the list is exact. No
behavior changed.

Validation: the App-level regression fails with the frame-gate marking removed.

## 2026-10-10 -- Notice banners repaint when only their tone or kind changes

The render signature for the top banner carried only its text, so raising the
same text as a neutral status instead of a failure (or as the clipboard consent
prompt instead of an ordinary notice) compared equal to the frame already on
screen and kept the old colors and row coverage. The signature now carries the
banner surface (failure, neutral or consent prompt) with the text. Distinct
text, an expired or cleared notice and the no-notice path compare as before.

Validation: both new regressions fail before the change (same text compared equal across tones and across prompt and notice).

## 2026-10-10 -- Capped desktop-entry reads keep complete lines, and the Open With fixtures own their paths

A `.desktop`, `mimeapps.list` or `mimeinfo.cache` file longer than the 256 KiB
cap was read as its first 256 KiB and parsed as if whole, so an `Exec=` line
cut inside an argument could still be offered as a complete command, and a cut
inside a multi-byte scalar discarded the entire entry. A longer file is now
read as the complete lines inside the cap: the partial line is dropped, a cut
scalar keeps the lines before it, and a file with no complete line inside the
cap, a read error or text that is not UTF-8 is refused. Files within the cap
are unchanged.

The liveness fixtures for the Open With helpers no longer pass when their child
test did not run: each child prints a completion token after its assertion and
the parent requires it, and a child that runs no test is a failure. The FIFO
fixture pins both XDG ladders inside its own directory and checks that the FIFO
entry was reached and refused, instead of depending on ambient `HOME` and
`XDG_*` entries. The profile-root and owner-bridge fixtures now use fresh owned
directories removed on unwind, and the MIME probe files live in their own
directory. Descendant cleanup of a helper is not claimed: the bounded runner
kills the helper it started, not processes that helper started.

Validation: four capped-reader regressions fail against the previous reader.

## 2026-10-10 -- New-output fade setting names its single-pane scope, and chrome test oracles tighten

The new-output fade setting's help text and its source comment now say what
the specification already states: the fade runs at the live tail of a single
pane, and split tabs, scrollback and resize snap.

Test oracles only, no production change: the armed chrome-drag label tests
now grab an inactive tab and an inactive workspace among several distinct
titles and require the whole lifted label, in the lifted style, at the drop
position with shifted neighbours unlifted (the earlier tests dragged the only,
already bold, active slot and accepted any bold glyph in the title; removing
the lifted style from the tab bar now fails the new test). The centered HUD
test is named for what it checks, static indexed cells. A new regression pins
that an OS appearance flip reaches a session model that an earlier panic
poisoned, so its OSC 11 answer follows the flip.

Validation: test-oracle changes only, so there is no failing-before run; instead the lifted-style mutation was checked (removing the pressed emphasis from the tab bar fails the new tab test).

## 2026-10-10 -- Audit corrections across input, attach, graphics, copy, parsing and tests

The entries below landed together. They fix defects found by a full read of the
codebase: attach protocol checks, Win32 text input, image and Sixel decoder
bounds, a DCS parser fix for UTF-8 payloads, copy and copy-mode correctness,
OpenSSH config parity, symlinked config writeback, contrast-floor accuracy,
failed-command navigation, and stronger test oracles.

Validation: the full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting,
pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,386 tests with none
failing both one at a time and four at a time, the whole library test suite
plus the protocol fuzz and cross-script suites five more times at four
threads, production and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all
passed. Each fix has a test that fails before it, except where an entry says
otherwise.

## 2026-10-10 -- Spawn-time environment reads in tests follow the environment lock

The shell-integration writer and profile launch resolution still read the
live environment at spawn time without the shared environment lock: the zsh
wrapper took `ZDOTDIR` (and `HOME` when the command set none) for the
original-directory hand-off, the fish wrapper took `XDG_DATA_DIRS`, and launch
resolution read the config file `config_file_path` named plus the `ODYTTY_*`
setting variables. A session spawned by one test thread while another test
had redirected those could hand zsh another test's base as its original
`ZDOTDIR` or resolve a launch from another test's config file. The
environment lock guard now marks the thread holding it; in test builds these
readers use the live environment only on that thread, and every other thread
gets process-owned values (the test child home for `HOME`, unset otherwise,
and a config path inside the test process's own base). Release builds are
unchanged and read the live environment. Windows test builds use the same
rule for launch resolution; the zsh and fish wrappers are Unix-only. The fish
data-directory test now holds the lock in its single-test child process so it
still exercises the inherited value.
Validation: the new zsh, fish and launch-input tests each fail with their
reader reverted to the live environment; a lock-holding control still reads
the redirected values. Formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows
and macOS, the touched test groups (3,926 tests) serially and five times at
four test threads both without a display and under a headless compositor,
production and documentation guards and RustSec passed.

## 2026-10-10 -- Rail host picker, wheel input, exports, startup paths, key scan, paste and Win32 text

- A host picker opened from a workspace's right-click menu now carries that
  workspace's identity: it binds the workspace that was clicked wherever it
  sits when a host is chosen, and binds nothing once it has closed. Before,
  it kept the rail index, so a workspace closing above it bound its neighbour.
- The auto-hiding rail, revealed by a fast pass across the edge that leaves
  the pointer outside the band, now schedules its hide grace itself instead of
  waiting for an unrelated event.
- A continuous scroll event whose travel is not a finite number (or is past
  `f32` range) is ignored before it touches the viewport, and a sub-row
  remainder that is somehow not finite restarts from rest.
- HTML scrollback export resolves default and ANSI colors against the focused
  pane's presented theme, so a profile pane exports its own colors.
- A non-Unicode `--working-directory` reaches the startup plan unchanged when a
  profile is selected; before, it passed through the profile schema as lossy
  text and named a different directory.
- The `~/.ssh` identity-key scan reads at most 4,096 entries, skips names over
  255 bytes, and lists at most 256 candidates, with a notice when a bound cut
  it short; its tests use owned scratch folders that are removed on failure,
  and the missing-cwd profile test restores `HOME` even when it fails.
- Plain paste is normalized and written one chunk at a time instead of being
  copied whole twice first; a broadcast paste shares one copy of its text
  among receivers in other windows. The bytes and chunk boundaries written are
  unchanged.
- A clipboard image read that fails for a reason other than "no image" drops
  the cached clipboard handle, as text reads already did.
- In ConPTY Win32 input mode, a key whose text needs more than one UTF-16 unit
  (an emoji, or composed text) sends one key-down record per unit; before,
  only the first unit (half of a surrogate pair) was sent.
- The merge picker's move/merge wording repaints when it changes on its own,
  and its badge blanks the half of a wide character it cuts through.
Win32 input mode applies wherever a ConPTY child requests it (Windows); the
`~/.ssh` scan resolves under `%USERPROFILE%` on Windows as before; the other
changes are platform-neutral.
Validation: the HTML export test fails on the unchanged tree, and the hide
wake, wheel, working-directory, key-scan, image-handle, Win32 and merge-badge
tests each fail with their change reverted; the rail picker tests pass on the
unchanged tree, which already resolved the clicked workspace through the
menu's retained identity, and pin the new identity-carrying picker. Formatting,
pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, the touched `native::`
test groups (3,519 tests) serially and five times at four test threads both
without a display and under a headless compositor, production and
documentation guards and RustSec passed.

## 2026-10-10 -- Attached sessions refuse impossible resizes and report failed snapshots and detaches

- A `Resized` frame from a session host naming dimensions the host could
  never have applied (past 4,096 columns or rows, or past the snapshot cell
  budget) is refused when it is decoded, before the attached window's mirror
  allocates a grid for it; the host's resize clamp and this check now share
  one function in the wire protocol.
- A later snapshot that does not decode or restore ends the attachment and
  logs the error, as other protocol errors do, instead of redrawing a mirror
  that no longer matches the host.
- The attach pump reads frames through the resumable reader, so a read timeout
  left on the socket can no longer drop part of a frame. Setting the attach
  write timeout must succeed; only the documented macOS peer-closed `EINVAL`
  is tolerated, since writes to such a socket fail at once.
- `detach` reports a truthful outcome on every call: once sent it succeeds,
  once refused or cut off it keeps returning that failure and never resends;
  only a send timeout that wrote nothing can be retried.
- Window border tests now give the headless App a padding band, so their
  assertions run instead of returning early, and cover HiDPI thickness, the
  padding cap and both resize edges. An attach test's drain loop fails on an
  early close instead of spinning.
Attach is Unix-only; Windows has no session-host client and is unchanged.
Validation: the resize-bound, later-snapshot, detach and write-timeout tests
each fail with their change reverted, and the HiDPI border test fails without
the padding cap. Formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and
macOS, the touched test groups (144 tests) serially and five times at four
test threads both without a display and under a headless compositor,
production and documentation guards and RustSec passed.

## 2026-10-10 -- Contrast floor checks the displayed colour

The minimum-contrast floor checked the unclamped float colour, so a saturated
foreground could pass the check and still draw below the floor once each
channel was clamped and rounded to a byte: ANSI red on dark grey displayed at
about 10:1 against a 17:1 floor. The floor is now checked on the displayed
colour. Chroma is reduced toward neutral only when the lightness move alone
cannot reach the floor, and when no colour can, pure black or white is used,
as the documentation already stated. A colour that already meets the floor as
displayed is returned byte-for-byte, and a colour that fails moves by the
smallest lightness step that clears it. Theme generation, CVD adaptation and
the theme author keep the unclamped search because they gamut-map and re-check
bytes themselves; their golden palettes are unchanged. Tests cover saturated
text on black and on dark grey, a saturated palette across ratios and
backgrounds, idempotence, and colours just above the floor that must stay
identical.
Validation: the colour, CVD, text, grid, theme-author and palette-generator
groups pass serially and in five four-thread runs; the full suite passes
serially and at four threads (16,198 test results, none failing); formatting,
pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy, Windows and macOS cross-target Clippy, the production
and documentation guards, RustSec and the fuzz API check pass.

## 2026-10-10 -- Graphics, input and palette regression oracles tightened

Several core fixtures passed for reasons unrelated to what they named. The
Kitty graphics routing fixture now sends a valid 1x1 image and checks its
stored pixels, placement and acknowledgement, not only that the APC text is
consumed. The Sixel and Kitty limit tests admit real images first and check
the exact images that survive eviction, their placements and decoded byte
counts, so rejecting every image no longer passes; the Sixel extent test
checks the exact clamped columns and rows at two anchors. The iTerm2
over-limit test builds a real PNG at the exact payload boundary, so a
truncated decode cannot satisfy it. The Kitty private-marker case checks the
flags it set, the cursor fixture checks retained command text and the cursor
column, and the visible-row search fixture checks that rows are borrowed
slices. A zero-strength palette fixture starts from an unfloored input and
checks that only contrast correction is applied while structural fields are
untouched. Historical golden fingerprints are unchanged. Test-only change; no
runtime behavior differs.

## 2026-10-10 -- Desktop entries and split color clusters read as specified

Linux Open With read desktop entries more strictly than the Desktop Entry
specification in three ways. Spaces around `=` were kept in the value, so
`Type = Application` was refused; `Name` kept its string escapes, so
`Fixture\sViewer` showed literally; and a desktop id such as
`foo-bar-editor.desktop` could not reach `foo-bar/editor.desktop`, because only
the leading dashes were tried as directory separators. Values now ignore the
whitespace after `=`, `Name` and `Type` decode `\s`, `\n`, `\t`, `\r` and
`\\`, and every choice of dashes as separators is tried (fewest first, up to
six dashes; longer ids keep the leading-prefix forms, which bounds the reads
one id can cause). `Exec` keeps its own quoting rules. Every derived path still
has to be made of normal names inside `applications/`.
When a flag pair, modifier sequence or ZWJ sequence reaches the color renderer
as separate cells, the next cell used to join the color run even if it was
hidden or drawn in a different rendition. It now joins only while it is not
hidden and has the same rendition as the first, so a hidden or restyled part of
a sequence is no longer painted away. Source-owned clusters are unaffected.
Tests: a project-authored cluster font drives the real color-run builder for
hidden and differently styled flag, modifier and ZWJ parts; desktop tests cover
spaced separators, escapes and mixed-dash directory names, with unit tests for
the candidate order and the dash bound. The new tests fail on the previous
code.

## 2026-10-10 -- Failed-command navigation steps through each failure; test oracles tightened

Jump To Previous/Next Failed Command measured from the middle of the view.
The jump puts the target at the top, so the next previous jump found the same
block again and the next jump skipped any failure within half a screen below
it. The jump now remembers where it landed and steps from there for as long as
the view stays at the same top row; any scroll, or another tab, falls back to
the earlier reference (the selection start, else the middle of the view). The
regression builds two failed and two successful commands that scroll off a
24-row grid and walks previous and next across both ends.
Several tests could not fail for what they claimed and now do: Cut asserts its
edit bytes without a silent clipboard branch; the split-pane Ctrl+click test
requires the open notice and an empty PTY write; failed-command navigation
asserts the landing row; the layout tie-break test pins one pane, the vacuous
pointer round trip is replaced by one through the production pointer mapping
with a non-zero grid origin, and a dead helper value is gone; the rename-field
replacement helper no longer loops forever when the caret is not at the end;
the large-jump cursor fixtures use an 80x24 grid with an in-grid column; the
config-reload and frame-callback wake tests run over an owned (or absent)
config path instead of the host's, and the onboarding test owns the directory
whose child must be absent; the rename drag seam goes through the real pointer
route; and the convenience key drivers are labelled logical-routing only.

## 2026-10-10 -- Dropped paths show direction controls as escapes

A dropped file path was inserted into the command line with every Unicode
scalar except controls and line separators left literal, so a name such as
`x<U+202E>gpj.exe` could display reordered while the shell received the
logical bytes. Paths containing a direction embedding, override or isolate
control (U+202A..U+202E, U+2066..U+2069) now take the byte-escape path in Bash,
Zsh and Fish, which shows the exact bytes the shell receives, and PowerShell
refuses them as it does other characters it cannot reconstruct visibly. The
predicate is the one the paste preview uses. Combining marks, emoji and the
left-to-right and right-to-left marks stay literal. Paste is unchanged. Linux
and macOS behave the same; Windows already refuses file-drop insertion, so
nothing changes there.

## 2026-10-10 -- Copy mode reads wide glyphs as one word and yanks one cell

Copy-mode word motions read each cell's own character, so the blank spacer
after a wide glyph counted as a separator and `w`, `b` and `e` crossed a CJK
run one glyph at a time, in visible and scrolled-off rows alike. They now
resolve a spacer to its lead, the rule the mouse word selection already used,
through one shared helper. `v` followed by `y` with the caret still on the
anchor used to copy nothing and leave copy mode, because the mouse rule that a
click without a drag selects nothing was reused for a keyboard selection the
user had started explicitly. The selection is inclusive of the caret cell, so
that yank now copies the one character under it. A caret resting on a wide
glyph's trailing spacer still yields no text. Three copy-mode tests that could
not fail for their stated property now assert the exact settled cell, the same
result under two scrollback depths, and the absence of a range without a
selection. Same behavior on Linux, macOS and Windows.

## 2026-10-10 -- Font picker widths, floating no-ops and CVD cache tests

The font picker counted characters, not terminal cells, so fullwidth or
combining family names were cut too late or too early and sized the panel
wrongly; it now measures and cuts with the overlay painter's owner widths, and
cuts fall between owners. A filter with no match reported "no monospace fonts
found", which told the user the machine has none; it now reads "no family
matches the filter", and the original message stays for an empty inventory.
Moving the selection back to the first family scrolled the group header off the
top and reported more rows above for a row nothing can select; the header now
stays attached to its first family.
A floating-pane move or resize that changed nothing (a key held against the
content edge) still stored a rectangle for a pane that follows the default
position, so that pane stopped following it when the window grew. A no-op now
returns before touching any state.
The colour-vision theme cache test could not tell a working cache from one
that recomputed every time. It now replaces the stored entry with a marked
value and checks that the same key returns the mark and a new key does not.
Protan and Tritan modes, and a light background keeping its light-side
neutrals, are covered.

## 2026-10-10 -- The documented dim amount is the one the renderer uses

`DEFAULT_DIM_AMOUNT` was documented as a gentler analog of the linear halving
but at 0.40 it darkened to 0.6 cubed, about 0.22 of the linear colour, harsher
than half; nothing used it, and the amount the renderer applies was a second
constant. The two are now one value, 1 - 0.5^(1/3), the amount for which the
OKLab dim equals the linear half exactly, and a test pins that equality. The
grid and TODO prose that called the dim hue-preserving and chroma-aware as an
improvement over the linear scale now say what the colour module's own note
states: a uniform OKLab scale is a uniform linear scale, so the output is
unchanged. No rendered pixel changes.

## 2026-10-10 -- Oversized colour emoji cache builds a clamped texture

A colour emoji cache wider or taller than the graphics device texture limit is
a supported state in which every lookup and insert declines, but building its
texture still asserted that nothing had been clamped. Debug and test builds
panicked; release builds clamped silently. The assertion is gone for the colour
cache, the texture is clamped to the limit, and only the in-limit corner is
uploaded. A test builds the texture on a headless device limited to 256 pixels
and requires no validation error and a clamped size. The text glyph cache keeps
its assertion because its builder already bounds the geometry by the device
limit.

## 2026-10-10 -- Images crossing a bottom margin are removed when the region feeds scrollback

A full-screen program that reserves a footer below a top-anchored scroll region
and scrolls into history moved every image anchored at or above the margin as a
whole, including one whose lower rows lay in the footer, although the footer
rows stay fixed. The rule now matches the region scroll that does not feed
history: an image crossing the bottom margin is removed, an image wholly below
it stays, and images above it or already in history still move. Tests pin the
straddling case, and new tests cover deleting virtual placements by image id
with and without a placement id, with and without a real placement still
showing the image.

## 2026-10-10 -- Loading animations wait on a shown frame, and image decoders refuse oversized input themselves

A loading animation (`s=2`) that reached trailing frames marked as base data
only stepped onto the last of them and displayed it while it waited for more
frames. It now waits on the last frame that is meant to be shown, schedules no
wake, and resumes through the base-data frames once a displayable frame
arrives. Frame storage also remembers the canvas size it was built for and
refuses a frame or composition command that names another size, instead of
copying rows at the wrong stride. The Sixel decoder now enforces the 1 MiB
payload cap itself and keeps eight parameters per command instead of one per
semicolon, so the guards no longer depend on the terminal capturing the payload
first, and the row counter cannot overflow. Direct mutation of an image's frame
list is no longer part of the public image-store API; the scene's frame
commands remain the path that checks the byte budget first.

## 2026-10-10 -- A floating pane hides text behind it even when it sits inside one glyph's box

When a floating pane overlapped a quad of the pane behind it without reaching
two opposite sides of that quad, the quad was drawn whole and showed through
the floating pane: a pane interior to a tall glyph, or touching one edge in its
middle, cut nothing. The cut now keeps the largest of the four strips around the
overlap, which also covers the corner and full-span cases it handled before with
the same results. Tests sweep hole placements across, along and inside a quad
and require the kept rectangle to avoid the hole in every one.

## 2026-10-10 -- A URL scheme followed only by punctuation is no longer a hint

Text such as `http://.` produced a hint for the bare scheme `http://` once the
trailing dot was trimmed, because the body was checked for content before the
trim. The check now runs after it, so a scheme with nothing left behind it
yields no hint, while `http://a.` still hints `http://a`. A test covers
punctuation-only bodies for several schemes and the surviving-body cases.

## 2026-10-10 -- A device control string no longer ends in the middle of a UTF-8 character

A raw 0x9C byte inside a device control string ended the string, although
0x9C is the last byte of many UTF-8 characters (U+0C9C is E0 B2 9C), so a
payload carrying such text was cut mid-character and the remainder printed as
ordinary output. Raw 0x9C is now payload, as it already was in operating system
strings; `ESC \` remains the terminator. Tests cover the byte machine and the
full parser with a payload ending in that scalar.

## 2026-10-10 -- Settings written back to a symlinked config keep the link

A configuration file that is a symlink, the usual layout when a dotfile manager
owns the real file, was replaced by a regular file the first time OdyTTY wrote
settings back, so later edits in the managed repository never reached it again.
The writeback now follows the link chain and replaces the real file in place,
keeping the link and the target's own permissions; a link whose target does not
exist yet creates it there. Tests cover a relative link, a chain of links and a
dangling link. Windows resolves links through the same code; the tests run on
Unix.

## 2026-10-10 -- SSH config import follows OpenSSH on repeated keywords, repeated hosts and cut files

Four differences from OpenSSH in the host list read from an SSH config are
fixed. A file longer than the read limit was cut mid-line and the cut line was
read as complete, so a `HostName` could become a host that does not exist; only
whole lines are kept now. The first value of a keyword in a block wins, where the
last used to; a later `Port` that does not parse no longer erases an earlier one.
Two `Host` blocks naming the same alias are merged with the first value of each
field winning, where the second block used to be dropped. `HostName =x`, with the
equals sign attached to the value, is accepted. Tests cover each case, including
a file read from disk with a cap that cuts a line.

## 2026-10-10 -- Copy keeps trailing space-like characters and no longer panics on odd ranges

Copying a row trimmed every trailing Unicode space, so a row genuinely ending in
a no-break space, an ideographic space, an em space or a tab lost it; only the
grid's own padding, plain spaces, is now trimmed. The copy and highlight
functions also no longer panic on a reversed range or a grid without cells; a
reversed range copies the same text as its ordered form. The mouse and copy-mode
copy path now shares one row extractor with the reference functions so they
cannot drift apart. Tests cover each case, including the live copy path. A
selection holding only the second half of a wide character still copies nothing,
the same rule as for shaped clusters.

## 2026-10-10 -- Query fields delete whole characters, Kitty key reports keep every character, and session runtime files refuse planted links

Backspace in the search bar, the command palette and every other overlay
query field now removes the final whole character, including retained marks,
emoji sequences, flag pairs and script clusters, instead of one code point.
Search generation still changes once per edit and not for a Backspace in an
empty field. Forward Delete in the tab rename field keeps the caret before a
character formed when the removal joins its neighbours, including a
pointer-selected span. Backspace and insertion keep their existing caret
policy.

With Kitty keyboard reporting, a key that produces several characters but
arrives without platform generated text now reports every character; before,
all but the first were dropped. Keys that do carry generated text still send
it as one associated field. The routing is shared by Linux Wayland, Linux X11,
macOS and Windows.

The detached-session metadata writer opened its file with create-and-truncate
and then changed the mode by name, and the startup lock changed its mode by
name too, so a link planted inside the owner-private runtime directory could
redirect the truncation or the mode change to another file. Metadata is now
published through the private atomic write path (an exclusive owner-only
sibling, then a rename), and the startup lock opens through the no-follow
owner-private helper without truncation. A planted link at either name is
refused and its target keeps its bytes and mode; a loose regular lock file is
still repaired to owner-only and the lock stays exclusive. Detached hosting is
Unix-only, so Windows behavior is unchanged.

Test-suite fixes. Four protocol fuzz checks could pass while the behavior
they name was broken. DECRQSS churn now requires one complete, exact report
per selector and replays each report into an independent terminal; the
reset invariant prints a glyph straight after a full reset and requires the
default rendition and protection; the button churn requires complete OSC
sequences to leave the whole snapshot unchanged; and an invalid
`ODYTTY_FUZZ_ITERS` is reported with a warning as
`source=default-after-invalid-env`. Each new check fails when its defect is
introduced into the library and passes on the current library. Two
session-host fixtures that infer a closed peer now shut the socket down in
both directions before dropping it, so a descriptor inherited by a concurrent
PTY fork in another test can no longer keep the peer open. The Kitty
transport outside-root test injects its admitted roots and creates its
outside file in a sibling directory, so it no longer fails when the checkout
itself lives under a temp folder.

Validation: the query, rename, Kitty key and planted-link tests each fail
before their fix and pass after. The full gate on the combined group ran once: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96
Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,302 tests with none failing both one at
a time and four at a time, the touched groups and the protocol fuzz suite five
times at four threads, production and documentation guards, and the RustSec
audit all passed.

## 2026-10-10 -- Tab chrome seam visibility judged on the drawn composite

The tab bar and workspace rail seam is chosen so that, drawn over the panel,
it differs from the panel by a minimum luminance. That check mixed the
encoded sRGB bytes, while the GPU blends chrome in scene-linear light with
straight alpha, so the measured composite was not the one on screen: a byte
mix darkens every midpoint (half white over black reads as luminance 0.22
rather than 0.5). The seam is now judged on the scene-linear composite. For
the 145 built-in themes at panel strengths 0.5, 0.8 and 1.0 the chosen seam
color is unchanged; a palette whose near-black inactive color passed the
byte-domain check while the drawn seam stayed below the floor now gets a
lifted seam. The visibility tests for the seam, the veiled active fill and
the auto-hide reveal now use the same scene-linear oracle, the reveal case
draws the band cell at its translucent alpha before the wash as the floating
rail does, and one panel strength drives both the tint and the wash. Every
existing floor still holds. Same behavior on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation: a new test with a near-black inactive color on a dark gray panel
fails with the byte-domain check and passes with the scene-linear one; a unit
test pins the oracle at half and layered blends. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Shells started by tests get their own home folder

A shell a test started inherited whatever `HOME` and XDG base folders the
test process held at that moment, read without the shared environment lock.
While another test had redirected them, the shell's own startup state (fish
creates `.config/fish` and `.local/share/fish`) landed in that test's private
folder; otherwise it landed in the real home. Test builds now give every
child they spawn on Linux and macOS a home folder owned by the test process,
with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` and
`XDG_CACHE_HOME` under it; a profile's own environment still wins. The SSH
connection-reuse socket folder in test builds also lives in process-owned
state instead of being resolved from the live environment. Release builds
are unchanged: shells inherit the user's environment and the socket folder
stays in the state directory. Windows children are unchanged; PowerShell and
`cmd.exe` keep the runner's profile folders.

Validation: tests check the environment every spawn path gives a child, a
real shell started on another thread while the environment is redirected,
and the socket folder chosen inside a redirected environment; each fails
with its change removed. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Notice banner and workspace rail labels keep clusters whole

The notice banner, the clipboard-write prompt and the workspace rail's labels
still drew text one scalar per cell, so a combining mark took a cell of its
own and an emoji ZWJ sequence or a wide character drew as separate narrow
glyphs. All three now draw by the terminal's own clusters, the rule overlay
rows, the search bar and the top tab labels use: a mark stays on its base, a
wide cluster takes a real two-cell glyph, and a cluster that does not fit
before the edge is left out whole. A rail label cut short keeps or drops each
cluster whole, and `workspace_rail_width = auto` sizes the rail to the cells
the longest name draws in rather than its scalar count. Plain ASCII labels
lay out as before. Same behavior on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation: App-level tests raise a notice and rename a workspace with a
decomposed accent and an emoji ZWJ sequence, check the painted cells, the
rail's auto width and that each change re-keys the frame's render signature;
widget tests cover owner truncation and painting. Each fails with its change
removed. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Correction to the Windows session-test entry, and test tidying

The earlier 2026-10-10 entry on session tests said five tests had always
returned early on Windows. Four had; the event-loop proof test ran and failed
only because its child run failed. It also said the profile environment
tests wait for the prompt; they wait for the shell's first output, which on
Windows can be the PowerShell banner. The entry now says both, and the
helper's comment matches.

The pristine-window layout open test's message for a real layouts folder
that appears during the test now says that another test running beside it
can also have created it, and its temporary folder is removed when an
assertion fails too. When the event-loop proof test's child dies without a
test summary, the failure message shows the tail of its stderr instead of an
empty list of failing cases. Test and documentation changes only.

Validation: The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Replay keeps recorded cells; candidate window and freeze watchdog follow their own subject

A review of the overlay cluster, IME and watchdog changes found five gaps.

- The replay preview rebuilt each recorded row from its text and measured it
  again with the narrow chrome rule, so a frame recorded with
  `ambiguous_width = wide` lost its two-cell owners and the text after them
  shifted left. The preview now paints the owners and spans the recorded
  frame held. A wide glyph a one-column frame stored in a single cell keeps
  one cell, and a cluster the recording split with a control sequence now
  stays split instead of being joined.
- Overlay chrome removes control characters before segmenting text. A
  control used to end the open owner at measure time, but the cut strings
  carry no control, so a repaint joined the owners again and measuring,
  cutting and painting disagreed for a control followed by a combining mark.
- The process host's freeze watchdog now picks the stalled window with the
  same rule that holds the report back. A window that presented once while a
  sibling held the report, then began to owe a new frame, held the report but
  was not chosen, so an idle window was mirrored and the stall went
  unreported.
- When the watchdog's mirrored window changes, the outgoing window's
  diagnostic class, rate limits, slow-retry liveness and owed-frame timing
  are kept for it and the incoming window's are restored. A
  callback-outstanding class earned by one window no longer suppresses
  another window's classic stall record, and slow-retry liveness no longer
  compares two windows' redraw counters.
- The IME candidate window follows the cursor the presented frame drew,
  captured with that frame's snapshot, instead of reading the live cursor
  after the frame. Output that moves the cursor between the snapshot and the
  follow step no longer places the window at a cell no frame drew; the next
  frame adopts the move.

Two comments that still described scalar overlay widths now name the owner
metric. Same behavior on Linux, macOS and Windows; the callback-outstanding
class is Wayland-only.

Validation: new tests cover an ambiguous-wide recording and a one-column
recording painted through the replay overlay, a control before a mark through
measure, cut and paint, the three-window stalled-subject trace through the
shared watchdog, a callback class followed by a second window's classic
stall, per-window slow-retry liveness, and the candidate window after a
cursor move between frame and follow in a single pane and a split. Each fails
with its fix removed. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Sixel size limit counts drawn pixels, color tab labels stay pinned, long Arabic runs keep shaping

Three presentation defects are fixed.

Sixel decoding refused an image when buffer slack, not drawn size, crossed the
pixel limit: a wide band rounded the row stride up (2,500 columns to 4,096), and
a later narrow tall band that was 2,500 by 4,200 drawn pixels, well inside the
16,777,216-pixel limit, was rejected because the retained stride made the
buffer too large. The limit now applies to the drawn extent, and the buffer is
repacked to a tighter stride when slack would not fit. Oversized drawn extents
are still refused and report their real size.

Color glyphs in tab and rail labels ignored the glide offset that the text path
already honored. During a scroll glide a label with an emoji moved with the
content, and in a top bar it was cropped away at the content seam. Chrome color
glyphs now stay at their pinned position and keep label centering in both glide
directions; content color glyphs still glide and are still cropped at the seam.

Arabic joining runs and ligature substitutions longer than 255 cells lost all
shaping, because an overlay key addresses at most 255 cells, so one unrelated
extra character in a long line could switch the whole run to unjoined letters.
Such runs are now split into consecutive overlays at cluster boundaries, never
through a cluster, and cell ownership, selection and copy are unchanged. A
single cluster wider than the limit is still left unshaped.

All three changes are shared code with the same behavior on Linux, macOS and
Windows.

Validation: new tests for the sixel stride, the chrome color glyph position in
both glide directions, and long length-changing and same-length runs fail on the
previous code and pass now, with controls for over-limit extents, content
gliding and seam cropping. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Attaching to a hosted session stays inside one time limit

Attaching a window to a hosted session gave the initial snapshot wait a
fresh clock after connect and hello had already used theirs, and the wait only
checked the clock between frames. A host that answered the hello slowly and the
snapshot slowly, or that delivered one large frame in small slices without
pausing, could hold the interface thread well past the budget, and a snapshot
that finished late was still accepted. The whole exchange now runs against one
absolute deadline: every snapshot read goes through the same deadline reader
the hello uses, a partly received frame resumes exactly where it stopped, and a
snapshot that completes after the deadline is refused. The restore batch's
remaining-budget accounting is unchanged. The deadline reader polls the socket,
so the per-read timeout option and its peer-closed quirk on macOS no longer
apply to this wait. Attach is Unix-only (Linux and macOS); Windows has no
attach socket, so nothing changes there.

Validation: tests for a trickled frame, a snapshot completed after the
deadline, and a hello plus snapshot that together exceed one budget fail on the
previous code (the trickle test never returns there) and pass now, alongside
the existing mid-frame stall test. The CLI attach diagnostic loop was audited
as a sibling and is already bounded by per-call deadlines and an iteration
cap. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Settings palette status row repaints when a followed palette changes state

A followed external palette whose status changed (retained after its source
disappeared, or applied again once it returned) updated the Settings status
row without requesting a frame, so the row stayed stale until the next input.
A status change now requests a repaint, and an App-level regression drives the
real follower poll and checks the request.

Validation: the App-level regression fails with the repaint request removed. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Kitty keyboard reports carry the key event's real generated text

Kitty associated-text reports use the keyboard event's generated text with
all modifiers applied. Releases, missing text and control-only text no longer
invent printable characters. Multi-scalar keyboard-event text stays in one
colon-separated field; keypad reports use the same admission rule. Logical key identity and
legacy and Win32 encoding retain their existing behavior. The shared native
routing applies on Linux Wayland, Linux X11, macOS and Windows. Synthetic key
encoders infer ordinary text only for press/repeat characters without Ctrl
or Alt. Existing keyboard encoder tests move to a separate module to keep
the production file below its size limit.

Validation: three App-level regressions fail on the previous code. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Hints keep wrapped paths and URLs whole and keep accents inside paths

Hint scanning skips synthetic layout padding before a wide cell, preserving
URLs and paths across soft wraps and resize reflow. Paths keep non-ASCII
scalars retained with an admitted terminal owner, including decomposed
accents and script marks. Real whitespace and ASCII delimiters still end
paths; copied text keeps its exact logical spelling and source cell span.
The shared scanner applies on Linux Wayland, Linux X11, macOS and Windows.
Path prefixes remain Unix-style (`/`, `~/`, `./`, `../`); Windows drive-letter
and UNC path discovery remain outside this scanner's supported prefixes.

Validation: the wrap, reflow and retained-mark regressions fail on the previous code. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Headless GPU test fixtures hold their driver lifetime through teardown

Parallel image tests could destroy an unused EGL context while other tests
allocated Vulkan textures or retired devices, deadlocking the graphics driver.
Native headless fixtures now share process-lifetime instances by backend set,
using a test-only owner so test descriptor flags cannot seed production roots.
Both native and composite integration fixtures serialize their full GPU
lifetime through handle teardown. Devices, queues, textures and error scopes
remain test-owned. Native image batches and required GPU validation use the
same lifetime rule. The native renderer's instance ownership and rendering
are unchanged. The fixture policy applies on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation: the lifetime regression fails when its guard is released early and the root-isolation regression fails when tests use the production owner; ten mixed-filter four-thread runs (911 tests each) and five composite runs passed. The full gate on the combined group passed: formatting, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy for Linux, Windows and macOS, 7,281 tests with none failing both one at a time and four at a time, the touched groups five times at four threads, production and documentation guards and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Search bar, rename prompt and tab labels keep clusters whole

The search bar, the tab and workspace rename prompt and the top tab labels
still measured and drew text one scalar at a time, so a combining mark took
a cell of its own and an emoji ZWJ sequence or a VS16 symbol split into
separate glyphs, as overlay rows did before. All three now measure, cut and
draw by the terminal's own clusters, the rule overlay rows use, and a wide
cluster takes a real two-cell glyph. The rename field's caret, selection,
mouse placement, Left and Right, Backspace and Delete move over whole
clusters, so the caret never lands inside one; a typed combining mark joins
the cluster before the caret. A tab title cut short keeps or drops each
cluster whole. Plain text lays out as before. Same behavior on Linux, macOS
and Windows. The vertical tab rail still counts one column per scalar.

Validation: App-level tests drive search, rename and a program-set tab title
through key chords, an IME commit, PTY title bytes and pointer presses,
compare the painted cells, and check that each change re-keys the frame's
render signature; with the tab-label truncation test they fail on the
previous code. New unit tests cover cluster spans and the rename field's
caret, window and click mapping. The combined gate for this group of five changes passed: formatting, current, pinned Rust 1.96, Windows and macOS Clippy, 7,238 tests with no failures both serially and at four test threads, the changed test groups in seven four-thread runs, production and documentation guards, and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Tests no longer write shell-integration files into shared config folders

Spawning a shell in a test resolved the shell-integration folder from `HOME`
and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` without the shared environment lock. While another test
had redirected those variables, the wrappers landed in that test's private
base (which made the layout-listing isolation test fail at four test threads
whenever a display let sessions spawn); otherwise they landed in the real
config folder, or in a relative synthetic base inside the checkout. Test
builds now take that folder from the shared config fixture when the spawning
thread is inside it, resolved under the lock, and from one scratch folder
owned by the test process otherwise. Release builds still use the folder next
to the config file. Linux and macOS share this Unix path; Windows writes no
integration file, since PowerShell integration is passed inline.

Validation: three new tests cover a spawn inside the fixture, a spawn on
another thread while a base is redirected, and a spawn outside any fixture;
the last two fail with the previous lookup. The layout-listing isolation
test failed 6 of 6 four-thread runs beside session-spawning tests under a
headless compositor before the change and passed 12 of 12 after.
