The combined gate for this group of five changes passed: formatting, current, pinned Rust 1.96, Windows and macOS Clippy, 7,238 tests with no failures both serially and at four test threads, the changed test groups in seven four-thread runs, production and documentation guards, and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Bound complete session-host control exchanges

Unix control-client polls now use one absolute frame-read deadline, reject
zero or out-of-range durations before consuming data, and preserve partial
frames for exact retry. Polling retains buffered final frames after peer
close without ignoring socket-timeout errors. Input, resize, detach and
shutdown share a two-second total send deadline. A zero-progress timeout
drops one frame and keeps the stream; partial delivery closes the socket,
reports truncation and refuses later commands. Input length is checked
before allocation. The shared host writer propagates socket-option errors,
except for macOS EINVAL when a nonblocking poll proves peer closure.
Linux and macOS use this Unix transport; Windows detached hosting remains
unsupported.

Admission tests now require EOF after a bounded slow snapshot, distinguish
read errors from EOF, retain thread ownership across failed joins, and drain
the snapshot before sending shutdown during unwind cleanup.

Validation: formatting, current and pinned-1.96 Linux all-target Clippy,
93 session-host tests serially and in five four-thread runs, production and
documentation guards, and RustSec passed. Three control regressions fail on
the prior implementation; the unwind-cleanup test also caught shutdown
before snapshot admission and passes after the drain correction. The combined gate for this group of five changes passed: formatting, current, pinned Rust 1.96, Windows and macOS Clippy, 7,238 tests with no failures both serially and at four test threads, the changed test groups in seven four-thread runs, production and documentation guards, and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Commented palette colours and recovery status

Flat palette colours now accept whitespace-separated comments after bare,
single-quoted and double-quoted values, including OdyTTY, Base16 and both
flat `colors.toml` schemas. Malformed suffixes remain invalid. Restoring
identical valid source bytes after a missing or malformed file clears the
retained status without reapplying the theme. Reconfiguring the same source
also returns from watching to applied after a successful unchanged read.

Validation: 34 focused tests pass serially and in five four-thread runs.
Formatting, Linux Clippy, pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy, production-file and
documentation guards, and both-lockfile RustSec audit pass. The combined gate for this group of five changes passed: formatting, current, pinned Rust 1.96, Windows and macOS Clippy, 7,238 tests with no failures both serially and at four test threads, the changed test groups in seven four-thread runs, production and documentation guards, and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Dynamic analysis self-test emits a passing-test summary

The simulated Cargo command used by the Miri partition self-test now emits a
nonzero passing-test summary. The empty-filter guard previously classified
its silent successful exit as an empty selection, stopping the scheduled
workflow before any dynamic analysis ran. The self-test also checks that
zero-test and missing summaries fail required partitions and remain
classified as empty selections in diagnostic probes. Production Miri and
sanitizer filters, partitions, and execution remain unchanged.

Validation: the original self-test failed before the correction; all four
self-tests pass in five runs afterward. Simulated address, thread, and memory
sanitizer lanes retain their declared filters and classify passing, zero-test,
and missing summaries correctly. Production and documentation guards pass.
No interpreted or instrumented Rust execution was needed for this fixture fix. The combined gate for this group of five changes passed: formatting, current, pinned Rust 1.96, Windows and macOS Clippy, 7,238 tests with no failures both serially and at four test threads, the changed test groups in seven four-thread runs, production and documentation guards, and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Session tests run their checks again, and 28 more application tests run on macOS

The session transport and lifecycle tests each built their own winit event
loop. winit allows one per process, so every one after the first returned
early and passed without checking anything, and a loop built there first
also made the shared-loop application tests return early. They now use the
shared test loop, the event-loop proof test covers their modules, and a loop
built elsewhere in the test binary before the shared one fails the dependent
tests. One of them had broken while it was skipped: it started a shell in a
folder that does not exist, and now uses a temporary folder.

Application tests that never start a session no longer need an event loop,
so 28 tests that macOS ignored now run there and on hosts without a display.
The 56 that start sessions stay ignored on macOS. The automation listener
fault test, the accepted-connection close-on-exec check, and the palette
history and image decode FIFO and large-file probes now also run on macOS.
Tests for Wayland, Hyprland, fontconfig, /proc, the Linux installer, X11
PRIMARY and the Linux desktop identity stay Linux-only. The automation
truncated-frame test now retries a read interrupted by a signal, which had
made it fail intermittently beside tests that start child processes.

Four tests that had always returned early on Windows now run there, and the
event-loop proof test, which runs them in a child process, failed with them;
all five were corrected to match ConPTY, and none was a product defect. The held-resize
transport test asserted on the seed pane, which is headless and has no local
PTY; it now holds a second real pane beside the one whose resize failed. The
profile environment tests never released the shell that a window holds until
its first grid, and typed POSIX syntax into PowerShell, where `$ODY_TEST`
names an unset shell variable; they now settle the launch geometry as a
window does, wait for the shell's first output, and print the value with `$env:ODY_TEST`
in PowerShell or `%ODY_TEST%` in `cmd.exe`. The explicit-shell and restore
tests named `/bin/sh`, so no tab opened on Windows; they use `cmd.exe` there.
The event-loop proof test now names the failing cases of its child run.

The pristine-window layout open test, which now also runs on macOS and on
hosts without a display, created the real state and layouts folders. It now
runs against a fresh redirected base and checks that the folders it prepares
are inside it.

Validation: with the old session helper and a display, the event-loop proof
test and the workspace tests fail, and the layout open test fails with its
redirect removed. The combined change passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,209 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), the full four-thread pass (7,209 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (556 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. The Windows cases run only on the Windows CI leg.

## 2026-10-10 -- Devlog entry for replay identity describes the preview correctly

The 2026-10-08 entry on replay cache identity said the replay preview shows
base characters only. The preview kept each cell's cluster scalars at that
point; the entry now says so. Documentation only.

Validation: no test applies, since only the devlog changed. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,209 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), the full suite with four test threads (7,209 passed, 0 failed), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,311 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Glyph cache growth uses the full graphics device texture limit again

The 8,192 pixel axis ceiling added to the monochrome glyph cache also capped
its growth, which roughly halved the number of distinct glyphs it could hold
on graphics devices with 16,384 pixel textures. Once full, new glyphs draw the
missing-glyph box. The ceiling now applies to the initial cache only; growth
uses the device's texture limit within the same 192 MiB bitmap budget. The
documentation now states that CPU reservation failure falls back to
monochrome glyphs while GPU allocation and upload failures use the renderer's
existing error handling, why the colour and monochrome budgets differ, and
the VT510 basis for DECSERA keeping attributes while DECSEL and DECSED keep
the current-blank policy. Two earlier entries are corrected: out-of-range
font metrics use an 8 by 16 fallback cell rather than being clamped, and GPU
failures are not covered by the colour-glyph fallback. Same behavior on Linux
Wayland, Linux X11, macOS and Windows.

Validation: three cache-capacity tests fail before the change. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,209 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), the full suite with four test threads (7,209 passed, 0 failed), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,311 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Input method previews keep their start, emoji clusters stay whole in overlays, and multi-window freeze detection is per window

- **IME preview.** A composition longer than the columns left before the
  right edge showed a later part of the text instead of its start; it now
  keeps its first characters. In a split tab the focused pane now shows the
  composition inline, as a single-pane tab does.
- **IME candidate window.** It is placed after each frame settles its display
  order, so with bidirectional display on it opens at the cursor cell that
  frame draws, not the previous frame's position.
- **IME commits.** A commit from a composition that ended on another pane is
  refused however late it arrives; elapsed time no longer lifts the refusal.
  A key press, a click or a window focus change lifts it, after which a commit
  without a composition (an emoji picker, an on-screen keyboard) reaches the
  active pane.
- **Overlay text.** Palette, picker, dialog, paste preview and replay rows
  split an emoji ZWJ sequence, a VS16 symbol or a script cluster such as Thai
  SARA AM into separate glyphs. They now measure, cut and draw text by the
  terminal's own clusters, so each is one glyph in the cells the terminal
  gives it, and a narrow cut keeps or drops it whole.
- **Freeze watchdog.** With several windows, a stalled window's report could
  be hidden by another window's redraw count. Frame and redraw counts are now
  kept per window, and a frame presented before a window began to owe a new
  one no longer counts toward it.
- **Corrections.** The WSL listing note now states that the listing blocks
  the window that asked for it for up to two seconds; the Settings route to
  theme capture and a replaced paste confirmation are now tested through real
  key input.

Validation: twelve regression tests fail with their fix removed or on the previous code, including an aged late-commit refusal, a direct commit after each input transition, real redraws with reordering in single and split tabs, a two-window watchdog with unequal redraw counters, and overlay and replay rows compared cell by cell with the terminal. A new core test pins the overlay cluster rules to the terminal's print path. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,209 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), the full suite with four test threads (7,209 passed, 0 failed), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,311 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec.

## 2026-10-10 -- Opening the command palette no longer creates or changes saved-layout folders

Opening the command palette or listing saved layouts created the state
and layouts folders when they were missing, and tightened the permissions of
an existing layouts folder and its files. Listing now only reads: a missing
folder lists nothing, and existing permissions stay as they are. Ownership,
file-type and symlink checks still apply to every listed file. Saving,
opening and deleting a layout still prepare the private state folder and
repair its permissions. On Windows, listing uses the same read path as other
state reads and never created or changed permissions there.

Validation: two tests that list layouts and open the palette in a missing folder, and in a folder with wider permissions, fail before the change. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,192 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (640 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. The full four-thread pass stalled in the GPU composite integration tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- Selective rectangle erase keeps colours and protection

DECSERA (selective erase in a rectangle) cleared characters but also
reset their stored colours and attributes, including next to protected
characters and at split wide glyphs. It now replaces the text with spaces
and keeps each cell's colours, attributes and protection, as the VT510
specifies. Hyperlinks on erased cells still clear. DECERA, DECSEL and DECSED
keep their current behavior.

Validation: four parser tests fail before the change. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,189 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,796 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. A full four-thread pass stalled in four GPU image tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- Text glyph cache checks font sizes before allocating

Extreme font metrics or raster sizes could make the monochrome glyph
cache request a multi-gigabyte bitmap. Cell geometry is now checked before
any allocation: invalid or oversized metrics use an 8 by 16 fallback cell,
physical raster sizes are bounded to 512 pixels, and the initial bitmap is
capped at 8,192 pixels per side and 192 MiB. The device's texture limit is
applied before memory is reserved, at startup and on growth. Both bundled fonts keep their exact
geometry up to 72 pixels at 4x scale with line height 2.0.

Validation: three admission and geometry tests fail before the change without allocating a large bitmap. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,189 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,796 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. A full four-thread pass stalled in four GPU image tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- Colour emoji cache holds four times as many glyphs

Colour glyph capacity rises from 4,096 to 16,384, within the graphics
device's texture limit and a 256 MiB ceiling. Allocation and growth use
checked sizes; CPU admission or reservation failure falls back to monochrome
glyphs with unchanged cell metrics. GPU allocation and upload failures follow
the renderer's existing error handling. Startup and font or scale
changes share the same path.

Validation: two atlas tests fail before the change. When a setting is overridden by an environment variable at startup, the

## 2026-10-08 -- Windows automation shutdown no longer hangs on a half-sent message

Stopping the Windows automation endpoint while a client held an incomplete

a non-retryable error after pending I/O is drained. Unix keeps its existing
time limits.

## 2026-10-08 -- Input no longer waits on busy background tabs, and withdrawn broadcast pastes are announced

- **History trimming.** Pointer, button, wheel and key events reconcile
  trimmed history only for the active tab, so a busy background tab no longer
  delays input. Every tab is still reconciled on each redraw and by copy,
  copy mode, hints and search. Unmeasured: no speed claim is made.
- **Command copy and export** reconcile evicted rows first, so a command still
  on screen is no longer reported unavailable.
- **IME.** After a composition ends on another pane, a commit on the active
  pane is refused for one second only, so a later emoji picker or on-screen
  keyboard commit arrives.
- **Broadcast paste.** A confirmation withdrawn because the receivers changed
  now shows `Broadcast receivers changed; paste not sent.`

## 2026-10-08 -- Divider and tab drags end cleanly when a key, menu or new press interrupts them

A pane-divider drag interrupted before its release (by a shortcut such as
Settings or copy mode, a right-click menu, or the window edge) now keeps that
left release for the window, so a mouse-reporting program in the pane below
never gets a stray release. Opening a context menu while a tab or workspace
reorder or a seam resize is held now ends that drag without applying it: the

changes nothing.

## 2026-10-08 -- A failed New Window no longer closes OdyTTY, and one busy window cannot hide another's freeze

When a new window's surface could not be created, the whole process exited
and every window closed. Now only that window is discarded and the window
that asked shows a notice; a failure of the first window at startup still
ends the launch. The freeze watchdog also stopped counting a window that drew
nothing while a sibling kept animating. A present is now reported only when
every shown window that owes a frame has drawn; minimized or hidden windows
do not count. With one window nothing changes.

## 2026-10-08 -- A synchronized-output hold right after a split closes draws the whole window

When a split collapsed to one pane while that pane's program had a
synchronized-output batch open, the next frame could show the old pane-sized
picture as the whole window until the batch ended. The saved frame now
records whether it covers the whole window and is reused only then;
otherwise the frame draws normally and the hold resumes from the next one.

Validation: an App-level test through the real redraw handler fails before the change. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,189 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,796 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. A full four-thread pass stalled in four GPU image tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- Overlay text, paste preview, dialog repaint, merge numerals, attach and drop notices, and test hygiene

- **Overlay rows.** Names in pickers and menus drop hidden format characters
  (direction marks, overrides, isolates, zero-width space, byte-order mark,
  soft hyphen) as well as controls; joiners and emoji tags stay. The command
  palette shows them as escapes. Open With and the Session Navigator cut rows
  by display columns, and overlay text draws wide glyphs at full width with
  their combining marks.
- **Paste confirmation.** A one-line paste containing a direction override or
  isolate now asks first, because the command can display in a different order
  from what the shell receives. A direction mark alone does not. The preview
  shows invisible format characters as escapes.
- **Dialogs** that replace another of the same kind repaint with the new text.
- **Theme picker.** The wheel previews the selected theme, and the scroll
  arrow stays while entries remain below.
- **Recorded output replay** keeps combining marks and clusters and no longer
  adds a blank after each wide glyph.
- **Merge picker.** Only the first nine windows get a numeral, so no two share
  one.
- **Notices.** `odytty attach <id>` for a session that cannot be attached, and
  a Wayland drop that names no local file, now show a notice.
- **Images over the texture limit** no longer darken at transparent edges.
- **Robustness.** Wheel deltas are bounded; secure-input requests are ordered
  under one lock; a pending input-loss notice can no longer be lost; on Windows
  the WSL listing cache is no longer locked while `wsl.exe` runs, and a failed
  listing is retried after five seconds; panic messages are capped at 1 KiB.
- **Tests** that could not fail now assert something, and two obsolete tests
  were removed.

Validation: each behavior fix with a reachable failing case fails its test with the fix removed; the races, the Windows-only cache and the panic cap are covered through their seams. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,189 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,796 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. A full four-thread pass stalled in four GPU image tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- IME preview and candidate window, pill caps, attention badge, hovered paths and profile workspaces

- **IME pre-edit preview.** A decomposed accent took two cells, emoji and
  script clusters split, and a wide character left a plain blank. The preview
  now uses the terminal's own cluster and width rules, so it covers the cells
  the committed text will. An owner that does not fit is left out whole, and
  covering half of a wide glyph blanks the other half.
- **IME candidate window in splits.** In a right or lower pane it opened
  beside another pane. It now anchors at the focused pane's cursor.
- **Button pill caps** no longer replace a space carrying a mark, an inverse
  or underlined space, or a wide glyph's tail.
- **Pane attention badge.** Marks, wide tails and inverse or underline
  attributes leaked into the badge cell; it now replaces the cell.
- **Hovered paths.** A filename with a decomposed accent never resolved. The
  scanner now sees each cell's full text and maps matches back to their cells.
- **Editor templates.** A filename containing `{line}` or `{col}` was
  rewritten. Templates now expand in one pass.
- **PRIMARY selection.** An empty selection no longer drops the clipboard
  connection.
- **Floating rail.** Dragging a workspace slot now repaints the rail.
- **Profile workspaces.** New Workspace with a profile, through the default
  profile, or reopening a closed one falls back to home with a notice when the
  starting folder is missing, and carries the profile's theme.
- **Profile auto-switch.** A switch shows the new theme at once, a profile with
  no theme clears the old one, and one pane's switch no longer blocks another
  pane at the same host and folder.

Validation: each new test fails with its own fix removed, except the PRIMARY read, which is covered through its shared helper. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,189 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,796 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. A full four-thread pass stalled in four GPU image tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- Four doc comments sit on their own items again

Four recent insertions placed a new item between an existing doc comment
and the function it described, so the old text documented the wrong item:
the context-menu opener, the command-line argument check, an automation host
test and the hovered-path open. Each comment is back on its own item, and the
context-menu comment now states the current Paste rule (enabled when the
active pane accepts input; the clipboard is not probed). Comments only.

## 2026-10-08 -- CI runs on short-lived ci/ branches, and the test guide adds a four-thread pass

CI now also runs on `ci/**` branches, so a change can be checked on all
three platforms before master moves to that exact commit. No packaging or
publishing workflow gains a branch trigger, and tag publication still checks
the exact commit's CI. The contributor guide now runs the full suite a second
time with four test threads, which matches CI and exposes tests that collide
on shared state, and runs changed test groups five times that way.

Validation: the changed workflow passes actionlint with ShellCheck, and the trigger and guide contract checks pass on the changed files. Those infrastructure checks do not establish a failing-before behavior regression. ## 2026-10-08 -- Persistence tests keep layouts and state in their own temporary folder

Tests that save layouts or profiles redirected the config folders into a
temporary directory but not the state folder (LOCALAPPDATA on Windows,
XDG_STATE_HOME on Linux when set). On Windows, one layout test's save was
therefore still on disk when the next test ran, and that test failed. The
shared test fixture now redirects and restores all five platform folders,
and the two helpers that duplicated it now use it. Test-only change.

Validation: a new test that sets outside values for every folder fails before the change on the state folder. The combined batch passed formatting with the current and pinned 1.96 toolchains, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, the locked serial Rust tests (7,189 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored), five four-thread runs of the touched test groups (1,796 passed, 0 failed each), production and documentation guards, the fuzz lockfile check and RustSec. A full four-thread pass stalled in four GPU image tests on the local graphics driver and stopped at its time limit; the serial pass covers those tests.

## 2026-10-08 -- Selections, hints and copy mode survive history trimming at the scrollback limit

At the scrollback limit, every new line of output cancelled the current
mouse selection, a drag in progress, hint labels and copy-mode positions,
because removing the oldest rows shifted all row numbers. These now move with
the text they point at, and only an end point whose row was removed clears.
Hint labels keep their names, and a copy-mode caret stays active if only its
selection anchor was removed. Clearing the history, resizing, or trimming
inside one very long line still resets them. Separately, scrolling up right
after a full-screen program exited could jump to the oldest row; the history
position now survives the alternate screen.

Validation: five output-driven App-level tests fail before the change. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Layout overwrite target, merge picker keys, merge focus, pointer write failures and shutdown

- **Save Workspace as Layout.** If a workspace before the one being saved
  closed while the overwrite question was open, confirming saved the
  neighbouring workspace. The question now names the workspace itself, and
  saves nothing (with a notice) if that workspace has closed.
- **Merge and move picker keys.** The digit or Escape that answered the
  picker sent its key repeats and release to the terminal behind it. They are
  now dropped. Keys from a window that already closed no longer reach the
  picker, and a key that types more than one character no longer selects.
- **Merging windows.** Merging a focused window into another did not tell its
  focused program that focus left. A focus-out report is now sent.
- **Mouse and palette input.** A failed write of mouse reports, prefix or
  palette input, or selection edits was silent. It now shows the same "Input
  not delivered" notice as typed keys.
- **Shutdown.** The quick-terminal shortcut is released when the window host
  ends, and nothing new starts after the last window closes.

Validation: the layout overwrite, picker key, merge focus, pointer write and registration teardown tests fail with their fix reverted alone; the closed-window picker routing and the exit latch need a live event loop and have no failing-before test. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- IME commits, chip hover, opening removed paths, image paste staging, hints and lost GPU device

- **IME.** A composition started on one pane and ended after switching panes
  could type its late commit into the newly active pane. That commit is now
  refused, also when the composing pane moved to another window.
- **Button chip hover.** When output drew or removed a Button Protocol chip
  under a still pointer, the hover and hand cursor did not update. They now do.
- **Opening a path.** Ctrl+click on a hovered path opened it even if the file
  had been removed since the hover. The path is now checked again first, and
  a missing one opens nothing and loses its underline.
- **Image paste staging.** A failed write left the temporary file behind, and
  on Windows an existing file could be replaced. The file is now created
  exclusively on every platform and removed on failure.
- **Hints.** Hint labels are now read under one lock, so output arriving in
  between cannot shift them.
- **Lost GPU device.** Settings applied just before a redraw ran font and
  texture work on a lost GPU device. The loss is now detected first, and
  settings then update the terminal only.
- **Tests.** The Bash gutter test now skips only on Bash older than 5.1,
  fails when the shell emits no prompt mark, and stops a hung shell after 30
  seconds. The opener reaper test fails when `/proc` cannot be read and
  watches only its own child. The IME tests check what reached the shell.

Validation: the IME, chip hover, path open and staging tests fail with their fix reverted alone; the hint lock and device-loss ordering have no failing-before test (a race, and no GPU in headless tests). The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Command copy, workspace close, failed font apply, autosave retry, probes, session ids and cursor glide

- **Command copy.** Copying a command or its output could copy the wrong
  rows if output trimmed the history at the same moment. Copy now reads under
  one terminal state and reports the command unavailable if it changed.
- **Workspace close.** A stale workspace position from a menu closed the
  active workspace, or quit OdyTTY when only one remained. It now does
  nothing.
- **Failed font apply.** When a settings change failed to load its font, the
  ligature, script shaping, alternate zero and symbol switches stayed changed.
  They are now put back.
- **Autosave.** A failed save of the workspace shape or working folders was
  treated as saved. It is now retried after 30 seconds.
- **Test Connection.** The probe could wait without limit when a helper such
  as a `ProxyCommand` kept its error output open. It now waits at most half a
  second. A failed status check now ends the probe process.
- **Session ids.** Two detached sessions created in the same millisecond got
  the same id. A per-process counter now keeps them distinct.
- **Cursor glide.** A font or scale change during a cursor glide finished the
  glide at the old cell size, and a second move during a glide made the
  cursor jump back. The glide now snaps on a cell-size change and restarts
  from where the cursor is drawn.

Validation: the workspace close, both autosave retries, session id and both cursor glide tests fail with their fix reverted alone; command copy, the font switch restore and the probe wait are covered at helper level, as their races and failures cannot be reproduced headlessly. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Broadcast paste confirmation, rail seams, log size limit and non-Unicode arguments

- **Broadcast paste.** If another window changed the set of broadcast
  receivers while a paste confirmation was open, confirming it sent the text
  to the new set. The confirmation is now withdrawn when the set changes, and
  a confirmation made before the window noticed the change sends nothing.
- **Pinned right rail.** On a window that is not a whole number of cells
  wide, grabbing the pinned right rail's edge without moving could widen it by
  one column. The width is now measured from the rail's drawn edge.
- **Rail and top-bar seams.** Dragging or double-click resetting the rail
  width now updates an open Settings panel, as the top-bar height drag does,
  and the top-bar drag reads padding from the surface it is drawn on.
- **Log size.** A single log record larger than the 2 MiB limit was written
  whole; it is now cut and marked `[record truncated]`. Several OdyTTY
  processes sharing one log now count each other's writes and follow each
  other's rotations.
- **Memory report.** A failed header write was never retried, and two
  samples at once could write a data line before the header. Both are fixed.
- **Command-line arguments.** An argument that is not valid Unicode crashed
  OdyTTY at startup. It is now refused with a message and exit status 2.
  `odytty control` endpoints keep their raw bytes.
- **Automation split.** A refused split of a pane that was not focused left
  that pane focused. Focus now returns to the previous pane.

Validation: each behavior fix fails its tests when reverted alone; the argument check has pure parser tests only, because the old path panicked inside the standard library. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Right-to-left selection autoscroll ends on the cell under the pointer

With Reorder Right-to-Left Text on, a selection drag that scrolled the view
resolved the pointer through the display order of the row shown before the
scroll, so the selection could end away from the character drawn under the
pointer. After an autoscroll, the display order is now worked out again for
the newly shown rows before the end point is chosen. Copied text stays in
logical order. With reordering off nothing changes.

Validation: three App-level tests (up and down in a single pane, up in a split) fail before the change and with the re-plan step removed; rows with wide cells have no dedicated test. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Poison tests leave the process panic hook alone

Tests that deliberately poison a terminal lock swapped the process-wide
panic hook and restored it afterwards. Two of them running at once could
restore each other's hooks in the wrong order and silence panic messages for
the rest of the test run. They now share one helper that leaves the hook in
place and stops the expected panic with `catch_unwind`. Test-only change.

Validation: the interleaving is not reproducible on demand; after the change no test replaces the panic hook. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Context menus and close confirmations keep held mouse releases out of programs

Opening a context menu with a right press (Shift+right in a program that
uses the mouse, or a right press on the tab bar or workspace rail), then
closing it and letting go, sent the program a mouse release with no press. A
context menu or a close confirmation also left an earlier reported press or a
local selection drag active after it closed, and a divider drag interrupted
by an overlay or copy mode sent its release to the program. The context menu,
the window and shell-exit close confirmations, and the layout-open and
attach-choice confirmations now take ownership of held buttons and end
reported and local drags. The context menu keeps the selection for Copy.

Validation: six App-level tests through the real input path fail before the change, each again with its own fix reverted alone; the layout-open and attach-choice confirmations have no dedicated test. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Theme Builder keeps the edited colour visible when a notice appears

In the Theme Builder on a short window, editing one of the last colour roles
and then getting a configuration-reload or validation message could push the
edited role and its input out of view, because the message took rows from the
role list. The role list now always keeps the edited role and its input
visible when at least one role row fits, and the scroll arrows, click targets
and redraw follow the same window. Scrolling with the wheel while not
editing still moves freely until the next keyboard navigation.

## 2026-10-08 -- Working-directory reports refuse network-style paths and partial host matches

A program could report its working directory (OSC 7) as a path starting
with two slashes, a path containing backslashes or control characters, or
under a host name that only shared its first label with this machine (for
example a different machine in another domain). Those reports were stored and
then used for new tabs, splits and saved layouts. They are now ignored on
every platform, and a supplied local host name must match in full, ignoring
case. Ignored reports leave the previous directory, the screen and the
cursor unchanged. Valid paths, including Windows drive paths and escaped
spaces, are still accepted.

## 2026-10-08 -- Bloom smoke test allows a one-step halo difference next to a bright patch

The bloom smoke test failed on the Linux CI runner: one body-text pixel came
out one shade brighter with bloom on. That pixel sits within the blur's reach
of the scene's bright patch, so it receives a tiny real halo, and whether that
halo crosses an sRGB rounding step depends on the GPU implementation. The
check next to the bright patch now allows one RGB step and keeps alpha exact.
A new scene with nothing above the bright-pass threshold requires bloom on and
off to give identical bytes, so bloom from text below the threshold is still
caught. The shader is unchanged. Test-only change.

Validation: the reported CI failure is the before-change evidence; forcing a zero threshold makes the new scene fail. The full local gate, run once on the batch of eleven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (7,100 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Session test helpers name whether they act on a tab position or a pane

Some test helpers read a session by its position in the list of every
pane, while the matching write helpers used the focused pane of a tab in the
active workspace, so in splits or other workspaces a test could check a
different pane than the one it changed. Position helpers now all resolve the
same tab and focused pane and say so in their names, pane helpers take a
stable pane identity, and a missing pane is reported instead of read as
cleared state. The contributor guide describes the rule. Test-only change.

Validation: four helper tests and the profile restore test fail before the change. ## 2026-10-08 -- Every terminal-state read recovers after an internal error

The previous fix covered paste, split drawing, cursor blink and selected
delete. The remaining places that skipped a session whose state was left
poisoned by an internal error (a panic) now recover it the same way the PTY
reader and renderer do. Before, such a session lost Kitty keyboard encoding,
mouse, focus and alternate-screen wheel reports, stopped handling bells,
notifications, progress and OSC 52 clipboard requests, was not resized with
the window, kept stale cell metrics, theme colours and host name, lost hover,
hint, search, copy-mode and prompt-jump targets, and saved or showed no working
folder or title. An attached session also stopped applying output, resizes
and snapshots from its host. A split-pane drag test now fails instead of
skipping when its setup produces no history.

## 2026-10-08 -- Rectangle and selective erases end a wrapped line at the right edge

When a line had wrapped onto the next row, a DEC selective erase or a
rectangle erase, fill or copy that overwrote its last column left the two
rows joined. Resizing the window then merged unrelated text into one line. Such
writes now end the wrapped line, as ordinary erases already did. A protected
last cell that a selective erase keeps, writes that stop short of the edge,
and attribute-only changes keep the wrap.

## 2026-10-08 -- Automation fixture test finishes when its setup fails

The automation test that creates two fixture directories at once made both
workers wait for each other. If one failed during setup, the other waited
forever and the test run hung. The workers now wait on a release signal that
also fires when the test gives up, with a two-second limit, and only finished
workers are joined. Linux and macOS run this test; Windows does not. Test-only
change.

Validation: a new setup-failure test for each worker fails at its two-second limit when the release signal is held. ## 2026-10-08 -- Profile catalog load count is kept per test thread

Tests that check how often startup loads the profile catalog read one
counter shared by the whole test process, so a catalog load from an unrelated
test running at the same time could make them fail (seen once on Windows CI).
The counter now exists only in test builds and counts loads on the asserting
test's own thread. Test-only change.

## 2026-10-08 -- Test Connection results stay with the form that asked

A Test Connection result could arrive after the connection form was edited,
retried or closed and reopened, and was then shown for values it never
tested, including a "Reachable" for a changed host. Results now apply only to
the form and request that started the probe; edits, retries and reopening
discard earlier results, even when the values match again. A probe that stops
without returning a result shows an error instead of leaving the form on

## 2026-10-08 -- FIFO file-type test can no longer hang the test run

The Linux test that checks the file-type sniff refuses a named pipe (FIFO)
without blocking ran the sniff on a thread, and on timeout still waited for
that thread, which could stay blocked. The sniff now runs in a separate test
process that must write a completion marker and is killed and reaped after
ten seconds. Further tests confirm the deadline ends a process blocked on
opening or reading a FIFO, and that a process exiting without its marker
fails. macOS uses the system file-type service and Windows has no FIFO case.
Test-only change.

## 2026-10-08 -- Session Navigator hides cookie values written without a space

The Session Navigator preview hid `Cookie:` and `Set-Cookie:` values only
when a space followed the colon. A compact header such as
`curl -H Cookie:a=1; b=2` showed its first value. A cookie header label, in
any letter case and with or without quotes, now hides the rest of the row.

The full local gate, run once on the batch of seven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,995 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Paste, split drawing, cursor blink and selected delete keep working after an internal error

If an internal error (a panic) left a session's terminal state marked as
poisoned while the session stayed open, several paths treated it as off or
missing instead of recovering it as other paths already did. Paste routing,
the paste confirmation, file-drop and broadcast pastes and the paste encoder
read bracketed paste as off, so a program that asked for bracketed paste
could get a plain paste, or a confirmed paste was dropped. A split skipped
drawing that pane, a blinking cursor stopped blinking after typing, and Delete
over a selected input line sent a single ordinary Delete. Each of these now
recovers the state the same way the single-pane view already did.

Validation: five App-level tests with poisoned state cover six of the sites and fail when any one of them is reverted; the file-drop and broadcast paste reads have no dedicated test. The full local gate, run once on the batch of seven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,995 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Mouse releases owned by a menu or modal stay out of the terminal

Copy mode, the rename prompt and overlays such as menus capture mouse
presses, but nothing remembered that a captured button was still held.
Closing the surface with Escape before letting go sent the later release to a
mouse-reporting program with no matching press. Entering copy mode or the
rename prompt also left an earlier reported press or selection drag armed, so
after the modal closed, motion could send held-button reports or extend the
old selection. The window now records each button whose press it owned, and
each button already held when one of these surfaces opens, and consumes that
release even after the surface has closed. A new press of the same button
starts an ordinary gesture, other buttons are unaffected, and losing focus
clears the record. As before, the program gets no release for a press it
received before the surface opened.

Validation: six App-level tests through the real input path fail before the change, each with one part of it reverted. The full local gate, run once on the batch of seven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,995 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Split-pane selection drags keep extending past the pane edge

In a split, a held selection drag stopped at the edge of the pane's drawn
grid. Moving into its padding, a divider, another pane, or past the window
edge left the selection where it was and never scrolled the pane's history,
and the edge bands that start autoscroll were measured against the whole
window instead of the pane. A held drag now clamps to the owning pane's
nearest edge cell and scrolls that pane when the pointer reaches its own first
or last row or goes past them. Focus does not move during the drag. New
presses and hover keep the strict mapping, so a pane's padding still has no
cell. In a single pane or a split, a held drag over the top tab bar keeps
extending and scrolling the selection instead of hovering a tab.

Validation: six App-level tests through the real input path fail before the change, each with one part of it reverted. The full local gate, run once on the batch of seven commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,995 passed, 0 failed, 34 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Notice chip opacity test always runs on the bundled font

The test that checks the single-pane notice chip draws an opaque background
skipped itself when no system font could be loaded, so a host without fonts
passed it without checking anything. It now loads the bundled JetBrains Mono
face (OFL) and fails if that face does not parse. An earlier entry now says
that four message texts received wording or punctuation corrections, since
one of them also changed its words. Test-only change.

Validation: the test no longer depends on host fonts. ## 2026-10-08 -- Configuration reload keeps editor questions and their controls visible

When the configuration reloaded while the key-binding editor was asking to
reassign a chord or to save unsaved changes, the reload notice replaced the
question and its key choices. The question now stays on screen with the notice
shown separately, and the conflict state is part of the redraw check. The
Theme Builder likewise keeps its colour, save-name and seed entry instructions
visible during a reload or validation message.

## 2026-10-08 -- Connection form keeps the focused control visible in short windows

In a short window the Add or Edit connection form cut off its lower rows,
so Advanced fields and Save or Cancel could be focused without being shown,
and clicks were mapped to the full form rather than the rows on screen. The
form now scrolls to keep the focused control visible, its help text gives way
first, and clicks map only to the rows that were drawn.

## 2026-10-08 -- Socket and scratch-directory tests use one exclusive allocator

Several automation, attach, session-host, font and integration tests built
their scratch directory names from the process id and a per-file counter, so
two tests in one process could share and delete the same directory. All of
them now use the shared allocator that creates each directory exclusively, and
automation fixtures remove their directory when dropped. Test-only change.

The full local gate, run once on the batch of six commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,963 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Image and clipboard base64 decoding rejects malformed endings and bounds its allocation

The base64 decoder shared by Kitty graphics, iTerm2 images and OSC 52
clipboard writes reserved memory from the encoded length even when the decoded
limit was small, and accepted a lone trailing character, nonzero unused bits
and padding that did not match the data. The reserve and every appended byte
are now held to the decoded limit, and malformed endings are rejected; valid
padded and unpadded payloads are unchanged. A regression test also confirms
that an SVG colour glyph with an extreme turbulence filter is refused before
it is rendered.

Validation: seven decoder, parser and clipboard tests fail before the change. The full local gate, run once on the batch of six commits landed together with this one, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,963 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-08 -- Opening a file with a shell entry checks shell options before offering it

An "Open With" desktop entry such as `bash -o posix -c %f` or
`fish -d all -c %f` was offered, because the check that refuses a file landing
in a shell's code argument stopped reading options at the first option
operand. Shell options are now read with each shell's own operand rules
(including bash, ksh, mksh, yash and fish). A file value after a shell option
whose operand count is not known, or used as an option's operand, is refused,
so entries such as `bash -T %f` or `nu --config c %f` are no longer offered,
while `bash -o posix %f` and `fish --command=true %f` still are. Kitty file
transfer tests now confine their outside control file to a scratch directory,
and the documentation states precisely what the directory-binding check
guarantees.

Validation: the refused-form and accepted-form shell tests fail before the change. ## 2026-10-08 -- Comment and documentation corrections across the source, SPEC, TODO and features guide

A review of source comments and the main documents corrected stale or
inaccurate prose. SPEC now lists harfrust, swash, tiny-skia and flate2 among
its dependencies, says file links open only for a local host, and states that
Arabic joining and complex-script forms do not depend on ligature settings.
The features guide has a regenerated contents list, the prompt-aware action
paragraphs back in their own section, the `notify-command-finished` binding
row, and the Windows hint for opening a layout. TODO folds the old release
preparation list into the v0.17.0 section. Source comments across the atlas,
box drawing, terminal core, colour, hints and connection modules were
corrected, internal tracking labels were removed, and em-dashes in comments
were replaced. Four notices (the second-window session notice, the shell
integration notice and two detach and switch errors) now use a colon in place
of a dash, and a benchmark message names the font load failure. An earlier entry's count of changed message texts is corrected.

## 2026-10-08 -- Workspace rail repaints for bells and host binding, and bare typing keys stay with the shell

A revealed floating workspace rail did not repaint when a background tab rang
its bell or a workspace was bound to a host. The rail's redraw check now
covers every field it draws. A global key binding on a bare typing key (a
character, Space, Tab, Backspace, Enter or Escape without Ctrl, Alt or Super)
was refused by the config parser and the editor but could still reach the
runtime table; the runtime table now refuses it too and keeps the default
binding.

## 2026-10-08 -- Workspace rename and Save as Layout prompts keep their workspace

A Rename Workspace or Save as Layout prompt tracked its workspace through the
workspace's first tab, so closing or moving that tab while the prompt was open
lost or retargeted it. Prompts now follow the workspace itself through tab
changes and rail reorders, and do nothing if the workspace closes. Cropping a
pane snapshot no longer reads a cell from the next row when a column lies past
the source width, and the FIFO detection test can no longer hang the test
run.

theme. Each pane now paints them from its own theme. In a single pane, the
transient notice chip and open menus are held opaque whenever cell

The Session Navigator preview hides credentials after sensitive header names,

## 2026-10-08 -- Partial font names keep monospace families, and symbol fallback reads only covering fonts

A partial font name such as `DejaVu` picked the shortest matching family
before checking for monospace faces, so it chose a proportional family and
fell back to the bundled font. Monospace families are now filtered first.
Linux symbol fallback could spend its eight load attempts on fonts that do not
contain the missing character; candidates are now limited to fonts fontconfig
reports as covering it. When `XDG_DATA_DIRS` leaves out the standard folders,
`/usr/share/fonts` and `/usr/local/share/fonts` are still scanned.

Holding a shortcut repeated almost every action, so holding New Window opened

A COLR v1 sweep gradient whose start or end angle lies below zero or above

## 2026-10-08 -- Wayland tab drag-out keeps the grabbed point under the pointer

On Wayland, a tab dragged over another window attached at the wrong place,
off by that window's padding and workspace rail, and on mixed-scale displays
also by the scale. Attachment now adds the destination tab strip's position at
the destination's display scale. Pointer motion from a different seat no
longer moves a drag another seat started. X11, macOS and Windows are
unchanged.

## 2026-10-08 -- Kitty file transfers and Open With close remaining path and code gaps

A program could still, within a narrow timing window, make a Kitty image
transfer read a file outside the temporary folders by swapping a folder on the
checked path for a link. OdyTTY now opens the checked folder by walking each
part of its path without following links, so a planted link refuses the
transfer. Where a platform reports no object number for shared memory (as on
macOS), the shared-memory name is no longer deleted after a read, because
nothing proves it still names the object that was read; the read itself still
works. Open With now refuses a desktop entry that would hand the selected file
to an interpreter as code, such as `sh -c %f` or `python3 -c %f`, while running
the file itself (`sh %f`) still works. On Unix a short window between the last
identity check and deletion remains, because POSIX has no deletion by
descriptor; the threat model states it. Windows transfers already deleted
through the open handle and are unchanged.

Validation: the planted-link, equal-metadata shared-memory and interpreter-code tests fail before the change. ## 2026-10-07 -- Shaping caches follow font changes

The text-shaping caches could reuse results from a replaced font when the new
font happened to occupy the same memory. Each loaded font now carries its own
identity, and both shaping caches clear when any of the four style faces
changes. A face taken from a font collection keeps its index when shaping. An
Arabic letter or mark missing from the font keeps the plain fallback drawing
instead of an empty box, and a zero-width screen is handled safely.

## 2026-10-07 -- Symbol fallback prefers regular faces and survives odd file names

Symbol fallback could pick a bold or thin face before the regular one,
depending on file order. Regular faces now come first within each symbol

One font file name that is not valid UTF-8 no longer discards every other

## 2026-10-07 -- Linux font discovery follows configured data folders and counts each font once

Font discovery on Linux looked only in fixed folders, so fonts installed
under configured XDG data folders, or in Nix and Guix profiles, were not
found. It now follows XDG_DATA_HOME and XDG_DATA_DIRS and checks those
profile folders when present. Linked folders and files are counted once, so
aliases no longer use up the scan limit, and a too-deep folder no longer
stops the scan of its shallower siblings. Fonts set up only through custom
fontconfig folders remain outside this scan.

## 2026-10-07 -- A malformed font can no longer request a huge glyph image

A font with broken vertical metrics could ask for an enormous glyph coverage
image. Invalid sizes and positions, and glyph images larger than 4,096 pixels

contrast and brightness values leave colours unchanged. Ordinary glyphs draw

## 2026-10-07 -- Fonts inside large collections load reliably

Colour-font discovery skipped a usable face inside a font collection file
larger than the 256 MiB limit, although only the small face is kept. Large
collections are now probed by their table directory only. Every font load now
checks the file it actually opened, extracted faces get correct table
checksums, and digital signatures made invalid by extraction are dropped. The
