## 2026-10-07 -- Missing-glyph fallback stays responsive after a resolver crash

On Linux, a crash inside the background lookup for a missing glyph's font
left later lookups waiting for the rest of the session. A crash is now
contained: pending work clears, runtime fallback switches off for that
session, and windows redraw. macOS and Windows use fixed fallback lists and
do not run this lookup.

Validation: the crash test fails before the change. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- A partial font name picks one family for every style

A partially typed font family could take its regular, bold and italic faces
from different families that all contained the typed text. A partial name
now selects one family (the shortest matching name, then alphabetical), and
bold and italic faces prefer normal width before weight, so they no longer
pick a condensed or expanded face. This applies on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation: the mixed-family and width tests fail before the change. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Settings edits reject bad input and step the same way by key and pointer

Typing something other than a number from 0 to 1 into wallpaper visibility
silently set it to fully visible. Invalid input now shows a message and
changes nothing. Arrow keys and the pointer stepper now share one step grid,
so an off-grid value steps to the same place either way. Left and Right in
Settings search no longer change a setting. The About row stays selected
through live updates. Turning the wallpaper off (none, off, or false) no
longer switches on the image treatment, and an unset wallpaper or automatic
readability setting is saved explicitly, so an unrelated edit cannot bring
the default wallpaper back. Choosing an image applies its dependent settings
together or not at all.

Validation: the visibility, stepping, search-arrow, About-row and
wallpaper-off tests each fail before the change. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Key-binding editor follows the selection, and pane keys resolve one way

The key-binding editor moved its selection without scrolling, so the
highlighted row, and the row waiting for a new chord, could sit below the
visible list. The list now follows the selection, the wheel moves the
selection with the list, and clicks and scroll arrows use the rows actually
drawn. Messages wrap to the editor width, and the unsaved-changes prompt puts
its save, discard and keep-editing keys on their own line.

Pane keys pressed after the prefix were compared three different ways. The
engine ignored Shift for character keys while the editor did not, so an
action could stay listed with a key that no longer reached it. Both now use
one table: a new binding removes any older binding on the same key, and the
older action shows as unbound. Binding a global action no longer deletes a
pane action's binding on the same chord, or the reverse.

A global action can no longer be bound to a key that types text or edits a
command line (a character, Space, Tab, Backspace, Enter or Escape) without
Ctrl, Alt or Super. The editor asks for another chord, and the config skips
such an entry with a warning. Named keys such as F9 may still be bound alone.
A key pressed exactly at the 2-second prefix deadline is ordinary input, a
shifted prefix such as Ctrl+Shift+5 works whether the keyboard reports 5 or
%, and doubling a Ctrl+Space prefix sends NUL.

Validation: the list, wrapping, chord-space, reassignment, typing-key,
config, deadline, shifted-prefix and Ctrl+Space tests each fail with their
fix removed. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Context menu rows activate only with the left button

A right press on an open context menu activated the row under the pointer,
including destructive rows such as Close Tab, Close Workspace and Remove.
Menu rows now activate only with the left button, as other overlay rows
already did; a right press on a row does nothing. Enter and Space still
activate the focused row.

New Workspace, Rename Workspace and Close Workspace rows now show their
chords. The menu repaints when its command-output rows, chord text, rail
clearance or clicked rail slot change, instead of showing the previous frame.
Hidden rows no longer widen the box. When the rail leaves too little room the
menu uses the whole window instead of collapsing to one column, an empty menu
ignores keys safely, and showing or hiding rows keeps focus on the same item.

Validation: the right-press, chord, repaint, width, clearance, tiny-window,
empty-menu and focus tests each fail with their fix removed. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- The window border stays on after a split

The optional window border (`window_border`) was drawn only for single-pane
tabs, so splitting a tab removed it, and zoomed, stacked and floating split
tabs had none. One helper now draws the ring around the content area: the
single-pane grid as before, or the whole pane area of a split tab in every
arrangement. It stays in the padding band, clear of the panes, tab bar and
rail, and pane dividers and floating-pane frames are unchanged.

Validation: the tiled-split, zoomed-split and off-path tests fail without the
split-tab ring. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- A scrolled-back pane with full history keeps its place as output arrives

Once a pane's scrollback reached its limit, every new line while scrolled
back jumped the view to the bottom and closed search. The view now advances
with each line pushed into history, so the text being read stays still until
it is evicted, and then the view stops at the oldest retained row. A view at
the bottom stays live. Search keeps its query and options while stale matches
and selections clear, a scoped search keeps its guard, output fades survive,
and a resize still resets the view as before. Single panes, split panes and
background tabs share the same bookkeeping on every platform.

Validation: the stable-text and oldest-row tests fail against the previous
eviction handling. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Workspace rail shows progress, drops control characters, and stays clear when short

The workspace rail never showed a workspace's progress, although the top tab
strip shows the same state for tabs, so a background workspace whose only
news was a running progress report showed nothing. The rail now paints the
progress glyph at its activity edge, taking the cell over the unseen-activity
dot as on the top strip, and the floating rail repaints when progress
changes. Rail labels drop control characters, as top-strip labels already
did, so a workspace name containing ESC or a newline paints only its
printable text, and auto-width counts the same characters. A rail only one or
two rows tall no longer paints overflow marks into its reserved control rows.

Validation: the progress, floating-rail repaint, control-character and
short-rail tests each fail with their fix removed. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Unix socket rejection tests set up their bad input before checking it

Two malformed-frame tests sent a complete valid request before its trailing
bytes, so the server could dispatch the request before the rejection
condition existed, and the early-close handshake test relied on every copy of
the peer socket closing, which a concurrently forked test child could delay.
Both trailing-byte tests now queue all their bytes before the connection
handler runs and assert that nothing wakes or dispatches, and the early-close
test shuts down the write side while a duplicate handle stays open. Production
framing, connection handling and peer authentication are unchanged. Windows
has no Unix socket test surface.

Validation: with a retained duplicate peer and no shutdown, the early-close
test fails as before. The full local gate, run once on the combined batch of commits ending at `a6c6a79d`, passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,871 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count. The Unix socket and host suite passed twenty further
default-thread runs.

## 2026-10-07 -- Opening a layout no longer closes a busy lone pane without asking

Opening a saved layout onto a window judged pristine replaces its only
workspace and closes the pane without a prompt. The judgement looked only at
shape (one default-named workspace, one tab, one pane), so a window whose
only pane was running a long job, an SSH connection, a launch-profile shell,
a read-only pane, or a stacked tab was closed silently. Pristine now also
requires the pane to be a writable local shell, opened without a launch
profile on the pane or the workspace, in an unzoomed tiled tab, whose
foreground job is known to be idle. Sessions expose the three-way foreground
state: the close confirmations still treat an unreadable job as idle so they
never nag, while this destructive path treats it as busy. Windows ConPTY never
reports the foreground job, so on Windows opening a layout always shows the
Replace / Add / Cancel prompt.

Validation: the new pane-state, busy-pane append, and App-level busy or
unknown prompt tests fail with the shape-only check or with an unknown job
treated as idle. The full local gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96
and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,744
passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored including nested child results), production and
documentation guards, and RustSec; eight touched suites passed five runs at
the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Failed tab reopen and refused pane moves leave state as it was

Reopening the last closed tab from the Session Navigator took the item out of
its recently-closed list, launched a tab, and then put the closed title on
whichever tab was active. When the launch failed, the surviving tab was
renamed and the item was lost. The tab launch now returns the pane it
created; the title goes on that tab only, and a failed launch (or a failed
workspace relaunch) puts the item back so it can be retried. Moving the
focused pane to a new window sends the program a focus-out as it leaves;
when the new window could not open and the pane came back as the same active
pane, no focus-in followed, so a program with focus reporting stayed told it
had lost focus. The refused move, and a detach refused after the focus-out,
now send the matching focus-in.

Validation: the failed-reopen and refused-move tests fail with their fixes
removed. The full local gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and
cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,744 passed, 0
failed, 31 ignored including nested child results), production and
documentation guards, and RustSec; eight touched suites passed five runs at
the default thread count.


## 2026-10-07 -- Split panes page, search, follow, and select by their own size

Several actions measured a split pane by the whole window instead of the
pane itself. In a split, Shift+PageUp scrolled by
the window's rows (almost two screens of a half-height pane), a search jump
centred the match on the window's height and could leave it below the pane,
copy mode moving down let the caret leave the pane's screen, a triple-click
line selection ran to the window's last column, and one malformed touchpad
delta and the scroll glide lag were bounded by the window height. Each now
uses the focused pane's own grid (the window grid, unchanged, on a single-pane
tab). The selection-delete row bounds and the click-to-position row count read
the pane's terminal too; neither could differ in practice because their rows
already lie inside the pane. The glide doc no longer lists split tabs as
ineligible.

Validation: each of the five App-level split tests (page, search jump,
copy-mode follow, triple-click line, touchpad clamp) fails with its fix
removed on its own. The full local gate passed formatting, Linux,
pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests
(6,755 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored including nested child results),
production and documentation guards, and RustSec; nine touched suites passed
five runs at the default thread count.


## 2026-10-07 -- Linux CI must actually run the GPU pipeline and shell quoting tests

The only tests that compile the cursor glow, cursor streak and cell drawing
pipelines returned early as passes when no GPU adapter was present, and the
file-drop quoting round trips through bash, zsh and fish passed silently when
a shell was missing. CI runners have no GPU, and none installed zsh or fish,
so these checks may never have run in CI. Linux CI now installs Mesa's
software Vulkan driver, zsh and fish, and a required step runs the three
pipeline tests and four quoting round trips in each shell, failing unless
every one prints its execution marker. Outside that step, a missing adapter
or shell prints a named skip line instead of passing silently. macOS and
Windows run the pipeline tests with captured output on whatever adapter they
have; the Unix shell round trips are not built on Windows.

Validation: a fixture test proves the required step fails for each missing
execution marker and for a failing cargo run. Locally all three software
Vulkan pipeline tests executed, bash and fish round trips executed and zsh,
not installed here, printed its skip line. The full local gate passed, and
the focused tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Restore test environment and colour globals when a test fails

Several profile tests redirected HOME, XDG_CONFIG_HOME and APPDATA to a
temporary directory and put them back only when the test body returned
normally, so one failing assertion left every later test in the run pointing
at a temporary home. Theme tests also published session colours and palettes,
which are process-wide, without the shared render-globals guard, so in a
parallel run they could race other colour tests and leave a theme installed.
The environment redirects now share one drop guard that restores all three
variables before the locks are released, including while a panic unwinds,
and the theme tests hold the render-globals guard, which restores the colours
it captured. Production behavior is unchanged.

Validation: a new test covers present and absent variables, both redirect
modes and panic unwinding. The full local gate passed, and the full library
passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Session Navigator preview redacts more credential forms

The opt-in Session Navigator preview redacted `password=` style values and
Bearer tokens, but showed `Authorization: Basic` credentials, `curl -u
user:password`, `--password VALUE`, cookies, and keys spelled with Cyrillic or
Greek lookalike letters. It now redacts the credential after any
`Authorization:` scheme, every `Cookie:` and `Set-Cookie:` value, the value
after an option whose name holds a sensitive word, the value after `-u` or
`--user` when it is a `user:password` pair, and lookalike spellings of
sensitive keys. A row is redacted whole before it is cut to the display
width, so a secret crossing that cut is never shown in part. A bracketed IPv6
remote host keeps its address. The short `-p` option stays visible because it
usually names a port or directory. Redaction remains best-effort, and the
documentation says so: a bare short password or a token wrapped across two
rows is not recognised.

Validation: each new rule has a test that fails with the rule removed. The
full local gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target
Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests, production and documentation
guards, and RustSec; the touched suites passed five runs at the default
thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Session Navigator typing always filters; row commands move to Ctrl chords

The Session Navigator is a type-to-filter list, but with an empty query the
letters `r`, `d`, `m`, `x`, `X`, `o` (and `i`, `p` with a second window) acted
on the highlighted row instead of filtering. Typing the start of a name such
as `dev` duplicated a live tab at once, and `ops` reopened a closed item.
Every printable character now filters. The row commands are Ctrl chords that
act on the highlighted row with or without a query: `Ctrl+R` rename, `Ctrl+D`
duplicate, `Ctrl+M` move, `Ctrl+X` close or kill (each after the existing
confirmation), `Ctrl+O` reopen, and `Ctrl+I` / `Ctrl+P` merge or pull with a
second window. The separate `X` kill key is folded into `Ctrl+X`, which
already asked for the same confirmation on a detached session. Overlay key
mapping carries a Ctrl+letter (no Shift or Alt) as a command input that every
other overlay ignores, as it ignored the chord before. The command palette,
connection manager, workspace and profile pickers, and open-with list already
filtered on every printable character; the profile manager keeps its `/`
filter key. The legend, the feature guide, the keybinding reference, and the
specification describe the chords.

Validation: the new type-to-filter, close-chord, and Ctrl+letter mapping tests
fail with the old letter shortcuts or the old mapping restored. The full local
gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target Windows/macOS
Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,744 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored including
nested child results), production and documentation guards, and RustSec;
seven touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.


## 2026-10-07 -- Split-pane clicks reach mouse-reporting programs and map to the drawn cell

A left press in the focused pane of a split tab began a local selection even
when the program had mouse reporting on, so vim or htop with the mouse
enabled never received the click; a Ctrl/Cmd click that opened a link there
also let its release reach the program alone. A press that keeps the focused
pane now continues through the same ladder as a single pane: open modifier,
reporting, buttons, then selection, with Shift still selecting locally. A
click that moves focus to another pane only focuses it; a reporting program
there receives neither the press nor its release. The pointer, SGR-pixel
reports, and the click-to-position half-cell decision measured a split pane
from its padded rect, while its glyphs are drawn from an origin shifted by the
sub-cell remainder next to a divider, so a click could address the column
beside the one under it; all three now use the drawn origin, and the
remainder strip holds no cell. Click-to-position under bidirectional display
measured the half-cell against the logical column and now uses the screen
column the cell is drawn at, with the boundary mirrored inside right-to-left
runs. Dragging the scroll thumb under a shown tab bar subtracted the bar
height twice and jumped on the first move. Pointer motion now honours an open
overlay, the rename prompt, and copy mode before chrome hover and pane hit
routing, so no motion report or hover runs beneath them and a context-menu or
rename drag over another pane still reaches the modal.

Validation: new App-level tests drive the real pointer path with split
geometry and a recording writer; each fails with its fix removed on its own
(split reporting, drawn origin, thumb drag, modal motion, bidi half-cell). The
full local gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and cross-target
Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,750 passed, 0 failed, 31
ignored including nested child results), production and documentation guards,
and RustSec; ten touched suites passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Palette rows keep the layout, workspace, pane, host, or profile they named

A command-palette row whose id carries an index (Delete Layout, Open Layout,
workspace switch, pane focus, host and profile rows) re-read its list when it
was accepted and took the entry at that index. A list that changed while the
palette was open retargeted the row: after another window deleted layout
`alpha`, the row labelled "Delete Layout: alpha" deleted `beta`, and a
workspace or pane closing shifted the switch and focus rows onto a
neighbour. Every indexed row now selects from the targets captured when the
palette opened: workspaces and panes by identity, hosts, profiles, and
layouts by name. Delete Layout also checks that the file still has the length
and modification time seen when it was listed, and leaves a removed or
rewritten file alone with a notice. Rename, host, and profile binding rows act
only while the workspace the palette opened over is still active. Layout
lists skip files whose stem is not a valid layout name, because every layout
action addressed the sanitized stem and reached a different file. Two source
comments that had drifted from their items were corrected.

Validation: new tests fail with each fix removed on its own (workspace rows,
pane rows, the active-workspace guard, the delete identity check, and the
listing filter). The full local gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and
cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests (6,748 passed, 0
failed, 31 ignored including nested child results), production and
documentation guards, and RustSec; the seven touched suites passed five runs
at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Refuse Windows network and device working directories

On Windows, any program's output, including output relayed from a remote
host, could report a working directory such as `\\server\share` through OSC 7.
Opening a new tab, duplicating a tab, or restoring a layout then probed that
path, which makes Windows contact the named network host and can offer the
signed-in user's credentials to it. Windows now refuses working-directory
paths that begin with two slash or backslash separators, including UNC,
device and mixed forms, before any filesystem probe or ConPTY child creation.
OSC 7 decoding, interactive and restored directory validation, profile
directory validation and direct PTY launches share the check. A refused OSC 7
report leaves the previous directory unchanged, and a refused spawn directory
falls back to a permitted home directory or the default directory. A shell
legitimately inside a network share therefore opens new tabs in the home
directory. Unix double-slash paths keep their local meaning. Tests use injected
probes for refused paths and never touch a network fixture.

Validation: the no-probe regression fails before the prefix guard and passes
after it. The full local gate passed formatting, Linux, pinned-1.96 and
cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked serial Rust tests, production and
documentation guards, and RustSec for both lockfiles; the 34 focused cwd tests
passed five runs at the default thread count. Windows-only tests run on the
Windows CI leg.

## 2026-10-07 -- Mutation survivor headings retain checked claim markers

Mutation report checks recognize survivor headings with ASCII hyphens and
colons, as well as numbered Closes follow-ups. Removing a nearby claim marker
now fails regardless of heading punctuation. Fenced examples, HTML comments
and generated tables are excluded; historical stage-1 narrative counts remain
separate from current survivor claims. The campaign documentation uses ASCII
punctuation and states the exact checked scope. This portable Python check
changes no terminal behavior.

Validation: regression fixtures fail against the prior matcher and pass with
the corrected matcher, including five repeated self-test runs. The full local
gate passed formatting, Linux and cross-target Windows/macOS Clippy, locked
serial Rust tests (6,674 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored including nested
child results), production and documentation guards, RustSec, shell checks
and the documentation guard self-tests.

## 2026-10-07 -- COLR v1 gradients blend in linear light and keep their gaps transparent

COLR v1 gradient stops were interpolated directly from sRGB byte values, which
drew dark midpoints between bright stops; the format requires premultiplied
interpolation, and stops now blend in premultiplied linear-light RGB before
conversion to the sRGB atlas. Radial gradients painted the first stop colour
where no circle of the gradient reaches a pixel; those pixels are now
transparent, including identical circles and zero-radius roots. Sweep
gradients lost whether a point lay before the first stop when the angle was
wrapped before padding; the signed position is now kept through pad, repeat
and reflect extension. Some colour emoji therefore look slightly different.
Solid fills, bitmap strikes, COLR v0, SVG glyphs and the raster limits are
unchanged. The code is shared by all platforms.

Validation: nine callback and pixel regressions failed before the change and
all 23 COLR tests pass after it. The full local gate passed, including pinned
Rust 1.96, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the guards, RustSec and the locked
fuzz API check.

## 2026-10-07 -- Preserve coverage metadata without publishing caller flags

The coverage runner passes metadata values as arguments to a quoted Python
program, preserving compiler flags containing quotes, backslashes and newlines.
Exact inherited and effective flags remain in local private metadata. The
shareable metadata and coverage summaries retain known scalar evidence and
redact caller flags and path-bearing values. Coverage counters, source identity
checks and historical measurements are unchanged. CI exercises the runner with
project-authored tool stubs, without an instrumented build. The runner supports
Unix with Bash 4 or newer; Windows execution is unsupported.

Validation: metadata quoting and report-privacy regressions fail before the
change; all three offline runner cases and classifier self-tests pass five
repetitions each. The full local gate passed formatting, native and pinned
Rust 1.96 Clippy, Windows/macOS cross-target Clippy, serial locked tests,
production/documentation guards, RustSec for both lockfiles, ShellCheck,
Actionlint and the tooling self-tests. All 58 Rust test-result lines passed,
with 31 ignored cases. No coverage campaign or historical evidence was rerun.

## 2026-10-07 -- Output fade, clipboard consent and About copy follow real state

New-output fading counted new rows from the retained history length, so once
history was full (10,000 lines by default) it stopped recognizing new rows and
an active fade stayed on the wrong rows. It now counts rows pushed by the
terminal, sampled with the frame snapshot, and a width-only resize snaps the
fade like any other layout change. The fade applies to single-pane tabs only,
as before; the documentation now says so.

The OSC 52 clipboard consent prompt remembered only one consumed key, so other
keys held while it was open could leak repeats or releases into the shell
after another key decided. Every consumed key is now kept until its release,
also across cancellation, and a repeat can never approve or deny a request.
Tracking is capped at 256 held keys; beyond that the prompt consumes all keys
until the window next gains focus. About's Copy Diagnostics reported success
even when the clipboard write failed; it now reports the actual result.

Validation: seven regressions failed before the change. The full local gate
passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the
guards, RustSec and the locked fuzz API check. The touched suites passed five
runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Shell integration keeps zsh startup files, Bash DEBUG traps and fish data paths

Zsh integration pointed `ZDOTDIR` at a wrapper directory that held only a
`.zshrc`, so zsh never read the user's `.zshenv`, `.zprofile` or `.zlogin`.
With the common layout that sets `ZDOTDIR` inside `~/.zshenv`, the user's
real zsh configuration was not loaded at all, and `ZDOTDIR` stayed redirected
for the whole session. The wrapper directory now holds forwarding `.zshenv`,
`.zprofile`, `.zshrc`, `.zlogin` and `.zlogout` files. Each forwarder sources
the user's file from the user's directory, carries any `ZDOTDIR` change into
the later files, and the user's directory is restored before `.zshrc` and for
the rest of the session. Unset and explicitly empty `ZDOTDIR` stay distinct.
If any forwarder cannot be written, the spawn is left unmodified.

The Bash snippet replaced an existing DEBUG trap (as installed by bash-preexec
users) instead of chaining it. The existing trap now runs after the
integration hook with the original command status, in local shells and over
SSH. Fish integration set `XDG_DATA_DIRS` to only its own directory when the
variable was unset, dropping the system vendor and completion paths; it now
falls back to `/usr/local/share:/usr/share`. Shell behavioral tests now fail
when an installed shell emits no prompt marks, and report a missing shell as
an explicit skip. Windows PowerShell injection is unchanged.

Validation: six regressions failed before the change and a function-tracing
probe failed before its capture correction. The full local gate passed,
including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the guards,
RustSec and the locked fuzz API check. The shell suite ran 46 tests with two
zsh skips where zsh is not installed, and the same at default thread count
five times.

## 2026-10-07 -- Moved panes keep their replay frames; rail menus act on the clicked workspace

Building a new window for a moved tab or pane no longer resets the moved
sessions' replay recording. The window constructor reapplied the session
replay setting captured at launch to the adopted sessions, so with replay off
at launch and turned on later, the moved recorders were switched off and their
recorded frames cleared, also when the new window then failed to open and the
tab returned to its source. Adopted sessions now keep the recording and
shell-integration state they arrived with, and a window opened for a move
starts from the source window's current settings instead of the launch-time
ones. A live tab drag already built its provisional window this way.

Actions from a workspace rail-slot menu now follow the clicked workspace by its
immutable identity. The menu used to keep the slot index and the workspace
name; if a background exit shifted the rail while the menu was open and a
workspace with the same name slid into that slot, Close Workspace closed that
other workspace and its shells. Rename, close, move, bind, unbind, save as
layout and the host picker opened from the menu now resolve the clicked
workspace's current slot, and do nothing once it has closed.

New tests in the window host move tests and `context_menu.rs` cover a refused
and a successful move with recorded frames, live settings, a rail shift with
duplicate names, and identity across rename and reorder. The previous rail
test simulated a shift by renaming the slot, which an identity check
correctly ignores; it now checks rename and reorder instead.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,680 with zero failures and 31 ignored. The window
host, context menu, live tab, replay, workspace and move tests passed five runs
at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Locally handled keys stay local; split panes select and report their own size

A key press handled by OdyTTY itself (typing in search, the multiplexer prefix
and its pane command, a shortcut, a modal prompt, or dismissing a held pane)
now also keeps that key's repeats and release from the pane. Before, every
local press returned early while the release continued to the encoder, so a
program using Kitty event reporting received a release whose press it never
saw; Windows Win32-input mode behaved the same. A release whose press reached
the pane is still sent. Search, `toggle-read-only` and `toggle-broadcast` now
act once per press; holding the search shortcut used to open and close search
on every repeat.

Select All in a split pane selects that pane's own rows and columns, read with
its history length under one lock. It used the whole-window grid, so a pane
shorter than the window got a range past its last row, and the shared copy walk
could then loop without progress when the selection was copied or set as the
primary selection. The walk now stops at the last live row, and stops if it
cannot advance.

SGR-pixel (1016) mouse reports in a split are clamped to the focused pane's
pixel size instead of the window's, so a drag or release past the pane edge no
longer reports a point outside that pane's screen.

Merging or pulling a whole window now carries its `--hold` state to the
surviving window, as tab and pane moves already did. A running launch session
keeps its hold for its later exit, and an already held, exited pane still
closes on its first key instead of staying as a dead pane.

Comments that described the App grid as the focused pane's grid are
corrected: it is always the whole-window content grid.

New tests in `local_key_capture.rs`, `split_pane_reports.rs` and the window
host tests cover each change; the Win32-input case runs on Windows only, where
that encoder exists.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,685 with zero failures and 31 ignored. The input,
split-pane, window-host, copy-mode, broadcast, read-only and session tests
passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Split panes hold synchronized output separately; chrome, copy mode, tab moves and the status gutter repaint when their state changes

Synchronized output (mode 2026) in a split tab now holds per pane. A batch in
the focused pane used to freeze every pane of the tab until it ended or timed
out, and a batch in any other visible pane was ignored, so its partial output
appeared mid-batch. Each visible pane now presents its previous frame while its
own batch is open and the other panes keep updating, and each pane keeps its
own 150 ms release deadline in the wake set. The retained frame is moved, not
copied. A held split pane keeps its cursor as last presented until the hold
ends.

Switching copy mode between character and line selection at an unchanged caret
(`v` then `V`) now repaints: the selection kind joins the frame-cache key, so
the band widens to full rows at once instead of on the next motion.

A settings or config change to `tab_bar_height` with a visible top bar now
reflows the content grid. The live-settings path compared only bar and rail
visibility and the rail side, so a taller bar covered content rows until
another resize.

The pinned workspace rail's resize seam now works whenever the rail is shown.
It was gated on the top tab bar, so a single tab beside an always-on rail, or
one tab in each of two workspaces, lost the seam.

Moving the active tab to another workspace now completes activation when the
move changes the active session at the same workspace index (the source keeps
other tabs, or workspace 0 closes and its neighbor takes index 0). Focus
reports, input latches and pane chrome used to stay on the moved tab until the
next activation.

The single-pane success and failure gutter now uses the same scrollback offset
as the rendered text and follows its sub-row scroll shift, clipped to the grid
rows, so a bar stays beside its prompt during a scroll glide instead of
jumping ahead to the final position. At rest the bars are unchanged.

Platform-neutral: Linux, macOS and Windows share these paths.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,682 with zero failures and 31 ignored. The touched
App, copy-mode, gutter, synchronized-output, split-pane, workspace, tab and
live-tab-drag tests passed five runs at the default thread count. These paths
have not yet been checked by hand on a display.

## 2026-10-07 -- Sextants, octants, triangular blocks and quarter ladders follow their Unicode names

Sextants (`U+1FB00..U+1FB3B`) and octants (`U+1CD00..U+1CDE5`) are now drawn
with Unicode's cell numbering, row by row (sextant 1 2 / 3 4 / 5 6, octant
1 2 / 3 4 / 5 6 / 7 8). Releases v0.1.6 through v0.16.1 numbered the cells
column by column, which drew 54 of the 60 sextants and 218 of the 230 octants
transposed: `U+1FB01` SEXTANT-2 appeared middle left instead of upper right,
and `U+1FB06` SEXTANT-123 appeared as the left half instead of the top row plus
middle left. The devlog entry of 2026-06-19 stated that the layout and the
triangle geometry had been verified against a font; that statement was wrong.
The mask tables were right; only the cell placement changed.

The triangular blocks `U+1FB68..U+1FB6F` now fill the quarters between the two
cell diagonals, with the base on the named edge and the apex at the center.
They had been drawn with the apex on the named edge and the base on the
midline. `U+1FB82` UPPER ONE QUARTER BLOCK and `U+1FB87` RIGHT ONE QUARTER
BLOCK now cover two eighths; they had duplicated the one-eighth blocks.

Dashed box-drawing lines keep at least one pixel per dash, so double, triple
and quadruple dashes no longer vanish on very small cells. Geometric coverage
refuses a cell whose pixel count does not fit in 32 bits instead of wrapping,
heavy strokes use saturating arithmetic, and the box-thickness multiplier is
clamped to the settings range at the renderer as well as in settings.

A new test derives every expected sextant and octant cell, triangle quarter,
and ladder height from the Unicode 17 character names, kept with their
license under `tests/fixtures/unicode-legacy-computing/`. The frozen coverage
hashes changed only for the 282 corrected code points; every other row is
byte-identical. The glyph corpus text describes the corrected layouts.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,682 with zero failures and 31 ignored. The
box-drawing unit tests and the legacy-computing, diagonal-pixel and glyph
corpus tests passed five runs at the default thread count. On-screen drawing
of these ranges has not yet been checked by hand.

## 2026-10-07 -- Remote New Workspace no longer closes an existing tab after a failed spawn

A New Workspace that connects to a host (a global default launch profile that
routes to SSH, Open in New Workspace from the connection manager, or a profile
with a host from the profile picker) first starts a local placeholder
workspace, connects, then closes the placeholder. When the placeholder failed
to start, the flow still took the active tab of the existing workspace as the
placeholder: a successful connection then opened in the existing workspace and
closed that user tab with its live panes, and the host or profile binding was
written onto the existing workspace. A failed placeholder now stops after its
notice, and only the placeholder created by the same action is ever closed.
The three entry points share one helper. Platform-neutral; on Windows the
connection uses the same `ssh.exe` path as before.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,676 with zero failures and 31 ignored. The
workspace App tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Stabilize concurrent socket and profile regression fixtures

Stale-socket tests wait for connection refusal before testing cleanup, connect,
and kill semantics. A listener inherited by a concurrently spawned child can
remain open until exec or exit after its original descriptor is dropped.
The bounded fixture probe distinguishes connection refusal from unexpected
errors and uses nonblocking connects. Linux descriptor-table lookup skips
unrelated descriptors that disappear while other tests close them, with a
synthetic disappearing-entry regression. Unix socket behavior is unchanged;
Windows has no Unix fixture surface. Profile-edit ownership tests on every
platform hold the existing catalog-counter guard after the environment guard,
so their catalog reloads cannot change another test's startup-isolation counter.

Validation: both new socket regressions failed with the former helpers; a
full-library repetition exposed the missing catalog-counter guard. All 17
affected tests passed 20 runs at default thread count. Eleven full-library
repetitions passed; two other parallel attempts ended without a complete
result and remain a separate validation concern. The full local gate passed:
formatting, native and pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy, Windows and macOS cross-target
Clippy, serial locked tests, production and documentation guards, RustSec for
both lockfiles, ShellCheck, and documentation-guard self-tests. All 58 Rust
test-result lines passed, with 31 ignored cases.

## 2026-10-07 -- Image placements follow scrolling, erasing, reset and delete targets

Placement bookkeeping now matches the screen operations that move or remove
text. A full-screen scroll moves placements already in scrollback history, so
they keep aging and are dropped at the scrollback limit; before, an anchor
stopped moving once it went negative. A margin-region scroll leaves placements
wholly outside the region, such as a header or footer image, in place; before,
it removed them. Erase in display follows the row-wise erase range: `ED 0` now
removes images on later rows left of the cursor, `ED 1` images on earlier rows
right of the cursor, and a placement wholly in history is no longer treated as
on row 0. A reset from the alternate screen also removes the saved primary
screen's placements, which used to reappear over the reset grid.

Kitty deletes now touch only their targets. `d=A`, `d=C`, `d=P` and `d=I` with
`p=` free only the images whose placements they removed, once nothing else
references them; an image transmitted for later display survives. `d=a` and
`d=A` leave placements wholly in scrollback, as kitty does, and `d=c` removes
placements covering the cursor cell rather than only those anchored there.
`d=I` without `p=` still frees its image even when it has no placements.

Animation frames composited with alpha blending keep exact source-over
arithmetic until the final divide. A one-alpha color over a transparent pixel
used to turn black, and partial alpha drifted by a level.

New tests in `placement_scroll_tests.rs` and `frames_tests.rs` cover each case.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,656 with zero failures and 31 ignored. The
graphics, Kitty, placeholder and core graphics tests passed five runs at the
default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- SVG glyphs whose id is on the root element now draw

SVG emoji fonts commonly put the glyph id on the document's root `svg`
element. The renderer looked the glyph up as a node of the converted tree,
which has no node for the root element, so every such glyph drew nothing and
kept the monochrome fallback. All 3,838 documents of Twitter Color Emoji
SVGinOT 15.1.0 and 3,687 of 3,688 in Noto Color Emoji SVG 2.100 use this
layout, so neither font drew a color glyph. A document whose root element
carries the glyph id is now drawn whole, its ink box fitted into the slot like
any other SVG glyph, and held to the same raster budgets. Every document of
both fonts now draws at 20 by 20, 40 by 40 and 1024 by 512, except the one Noto
document the existing pattern rule refuses. Committed fixtures only placed the
id on nested elements, so no test caught this; new tests use project-authored
root-id documents.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,655 with zero failures and 30 ignored. The SVG and
COLR v1 tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

---

## 2026-10-07 -- SVG glyphs are costed against aggregate raster budgets

SVG-in-OpenType documents were bounded per canvas, by size, node count, nesting
and reference expansion, but nothing bounded the raster work those nodes cause.
Each nested opacity, clip or blend group holds a layer up to the canvas size
while its children render, so a small document could keep hundreds of layers
live at once. Filters also allocated intermediates sized by their own region.

Documents with filters (including CSS filter functions), masks or markers are
now refused before conversion, like patterns. OpenType leaves support for any
of these optional in glyph documents, and their raster cost is outside the
budget model. The converted tree is then costed the way the locked
resvg 0.45.1 renders it: nested isolation layers, clip buffers and coverage
masks, path coverage and edge rows, and dashes. A glyph needing more than 2^30
pixels of raster work or 256 MiB of live buffers at its slot size is refused
before its canvas is allocated. These are the COLR v1 budgets. A refused glyph
keeps the monochrome fallback. Real fonts are not affected: no glyph of
Twitter Color Emoji SVGinOT 15.1.0 or Noto Color Emoji SVG 2.100 is refused,
from 16 by 16 up to 1024 by 512. The largest needs about 15.5 million pixel
passes and 4.3 MiB of live buffers. The cost model follows the locked resvg
release and must be re-checked when it changes.

New tests: filter, filter-function, style, stylesheet, mask and marker documents
are refused; 150 nested layers at the largest slot fall back while 15 render;
2,400 full-slot fills fall back at the largest slot and render at 32 by 32. The
budget arithmetic is pinned with small hand-counted documents.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,653 with zero failures and 30 ignored. The SVG and
COLR v1 tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-07 -- Conformance extraction preserves unrelated source directories

The pinned conformance runner now extracts into `upstream-src` and replaces
only a tree carrying its exact ownership marker. An unrelated `src` directory
is preserved, cache paths inside repositories are refused, and source or
marker symlinks are rejected. Invalid archives are unpacked into a private
scratch tree and leave the previous successful extraction intact. Build and
automatic suite discovery use the same marked tree. Old `src` caches require
fresh extraction and are left untouched. Linux and macOS retain their POSIX
harness behavior; Windows remains unsupported by the upstream suite.

Validation: synthetic regressions failed before the change, and all 64 runner
self-tests passed after it in five repeat runs without network access or a
product session. Full local gate passed: formatting, Linux, pinned Rust 1.96,
Windows and macOS clippy, serial locked tests (6,645 passed, 0 failed,
31 ignored), production and documentation guards, RustSec, and shellcheck.

## 2026-10-07 -- Linux MIME handler removals respect source priority

The Open With picker now applies each MIME association removal at its own
or lower priority. A lower-priority file cannot hide a higher-priority added
or cached handler. Defaults remain first, duplicate ids remain collapsed,
and the existing same-file removal policy stays in place. macOS retains its
native application enumeration; Windows enumeration remains unsupported.

Validation: formatting, Linux clippy on Rust 1.96 and the development
toolchain, Windows/macOS cross-target clippy, full serial tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec for both lockfiles, and the locked fuzz
API check pass. Five MIME precedence cases and 56 desktop tests pass.
The library suite has 6,105 passes and 18 existing ignores.

## 2026-10-07 -- Closing a window no longer blanks its siblings on the GL backend

On the GL backend, closing any OdyTTY window left every other window black
while the process kept running. Cancelling a live tab drag showed the same
failure because cancellation closes the provisional window. The cause: each
window opened its own GL device on the shared EGL context, the GL backend binds
its vertex array only when a device opens, and dropping a device deleted the
array the surviving windows still had bound. Released v0.16.1 builds are
affected on any machine where the renderer runs on GL. Vulkan, Metal and DX12
were not affected.

GL windows now share one adapter, device and queue per presentation instance.
Surfaces, pipelines, buffers and atlases stay per window and retire in the
existing order, so ordinary close, drag cancellation, retirement of an emptied
source window, merge and quick-terminal hide all keep the survivors drawing.
Device loss on the shared GL device reaches every attached window through one
global event and pauses them until OdyTTY restarts. Other backends keep a
separate device per window.

Validation: a headless GL pixel regression failed before the change (after a
sibling was released, the survivor produced only its clear color) and passes
after it across three sibling retirements. It needs a GL host, so it is ignored
by default and was run explicitly five times. Device-loss routing tests passed
five runs at default thread count. Full local gate passed: formatting, Linux,
pinned Rust 1.96, Windows and macOS clippy, serial locked tests (6,645 passed,
0 failed, 31 ignored), production and documentation guards, and RustSec.
Device acceptance on a GL-only virtual machine is pending.

---

## 2026-10-07 -- Corpus intake writes LF line endings on Windows

The compatibility corpus tool wrote its self-test fixtures and its reject and
quarantine records in text mode, so on Windows every line ended in CRLF and the
tool's own carriage-return check then rejected the files it had just written.
The Windows CI leg failed on every self-test as a result. All text writes now go
through one UTF-8 binary writer for both overwrite and append. Staging still
copies validated input bytes unchanged, and existing CRLF inputs stay invalid
rather than being normalized.

Validation: two new self-tests force Windows newline translation on Linux and
failed before the change; all 42 self-tests pass after it, also with the
translation forced for the whole suite. Corpus validation, shellcheck, and the
documentation and production-file guards passed. No Rust code changed.

## 2026-10-06 -- Corpus intake validates encoded privacy and prospective writes

Compatibility intake checks raw, NUL-stripped and both UTF-16 byte orders for
identity-bearing content, with the same Windows placeholder allowlist. Unknown
top-level TOML tables are rejected. Acceptance validates the complete prospective
corpus before writing and removes its new fixture if manifest replacement fails.
Rejected candidates retain their staged files and leave the corpus unchanged.

Nerd Font fixture labels now name the bundled jeepney, PHP, heart-box, git-commit
and cheese glyphs. Codepoints are unchanged. Font-backed fixture tests check all
ten samples in both bundled Symbols faces and verify the five corrected names.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96, Linux,
Windows and macOS clippy, documentation and production-file guards, and RustSec.
Serial locked tests passed 6,635 with zero failures and 30 ignored. All 40
offline corpus self-tests passed five repeat runs; the six-case corpus and
byte-identical generator output validated. Actionlint and workflow policy checks
passed. CI now runs offline corpus self-tests and validation on all three OSes.

## 2026-10-06 -- Color atlas admission respects device limits and cell spans

Color glyph insertion now checks both atlas dimensions against the active
device limit and requires the bitmap span to match its key before cached
reuse. Lowering the limit hides oversized resident lookups without changing
pixels, revision, or dirty state; restoring the limit recovers those slots.
Growth uses only complete pages that fit the limit. The shared atlas path
applies on Linux, macOS, and Windows.

Validation: formatting, Linux clippy on Rust 1.96 and the development
toolchain, Windows/macOS cross-target clippy, full serial tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec for both lockfiles, and the locked fuzz
API check pass. Seven public atlas boundary cases and seven internal atlas
tests pass. The library suite has 6,102 passes and 17 existing ignores.

## 2026-10-06 -- CI and release gates reject empty coverage and malformed inputs

Miri and sanitizer filters classify empty test selections explicitly. Required
empty selections fail; probes remain diagnostic, while undefined behavior and
sanitizer reports fail regardless of process exit status. RustSec requires both
lockfiles and rejects a return of ttf-parser. Release queries retry empty API
responses, reject unusable fetch commands immediately and retain fetch errors.

Release jobs share validated version derivation, AUR packaging uses the release
tag except for versionless backfill, and Homebrew SSH requires the pinned key
algorithm. Active branch CI runs survive later pushes, both deep-fuzz families execute after
independent failures, Windows development builds retain exact commit provenance,
and shellcheck covers every tracked shell script. Offline fixtures exercise the
gate contracts without running instrumentation or publishing artifacts.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including pinned Rust 1.96 clippy,
Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, RustSec and documentation guards. Serial locked
tests passed 6,634 with zero failures and 30 ignored. All 24 offline Python
fixtures, release-query shell fixtures, complete shellcheck and actionlint
checks passed. These fixtures use stub tools; no instrumentation or release
publication was performed.

## 2026-10-06 -- Advertised Wayland toplevel tab dragging

Live tab drag adds an advertised xdg-toplevel-drag transport. An independent
seat-owned pointer observer binds the original button serial to the source
surface incarnation, tab token and geometry. The provisional toplevel attaches
before its first buffer, and compositor drop completion commits custody once.
Returning to the source strip restores held reorder; a later departure attaches
a fresh provisional window. Raw releases and DnD focus changes do not finish
custody. Escape and source retirement also resolve a docked drag.

Motion wakes coalesce until their host event is consumed, with the latest
bounded target state retained. Local rollback preserves terminal objects and retains unresolved protocol
objects until legal completion. A refused grab restores the source even when
its ordinary pointer release arrives after acknowledgement. One unresolved
transaction bounds protocol custody; unavailable or ambiguous capture retains
release-time creation. Existing URI Copy admission and clipboard handling share
the same listener. Hyprland IPC and X11, macOS and Windows native paths keep
their existing behavior. No child spawn or PTY policy changes.

Validation: the full local gate passes, including native and pinned Rust 1.96
Clippy, Windows/macOS cross-target Clippy, locked serial tests, production-file
and documentation guards, RustSec, and the locked parser-graphics API check.
Wayland, custody and tear-out tests each pass five default-thread runs.
Display acceptance remains separate.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Malformed edit metadata disables prompt editing

Private edit reports now require the complete grammar: four fields plus an
optional newline list, with strictly increasing newline offsets below the
buffer length. A malformed recognized report invalidates exact geometry
until valid metadata or a new prompt boundary, so selected-input deletion
and click-to-position send no edit bytes. Unknown report names retain their
forward-compatible behavior. The shared parser applies on Linux, macOS,
and Windows, and the invalid state follows primary/alternate screen storage.

Validation: formatting, Linux clippy on Rust 1.96 and the development
toolchain, Windows/macOS cross-target clippy, full serial tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec for both lockfiles, and the locked fuzz
API check pass. The malformed-report tests pass five runs at the default
thread count. The library suite has 6,071 passes and 17 existing ignores.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Shared-memory image reads use the transmitted length on macOS

macOS reports a POSIX shared-memory object's size rounded up to a whole page
(16 KiB on Apple silicon), and the `t=s` Kitty reader capped that rounded
size rather than the payload. A small image in a small decode budget was
refused as too large, and any raw image within one page of the cap was refused
on macOS while it loaded on Linux. An uncompressed raw RGB or RGBA payload is
now read at its exact pixel length, with the object's size only as an upper
bound, and the cap applies to the bytes actually read. PNG and compressed
payloads still read the whole object, as before. A new test pads an object to
twice the decode cap on any Unix and loads the 16-byte image at its start; it
failed before this change. The shared-memory tests now pass the payload length,
and the rebinding test uses a replacement in a different page count. Where a
platform reports no object number for shared memory, two macOS objects of the
same owner, mode and page count compare equal; the documentation states that
limit.
The macOS copy maps only the bytes it reads. `S=` and `O=` remain unsupported.
Windows keeps `t=s` unsupported and is unaffected.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, pinned 1.96 clippy,
the serial suite (6,596 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and
documentation guards, and the RustSec audit pass; the touched shared-memory
tests pass five runs at the default thread count. The macOS reader is
compiled and linted here and runs on the macOS CI leg.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- COLR v1 glyphs are costed against aggregate work budgets

Fontations bounds COLR v1 Paint nesting at 64 and refuses cycles on the
active path, but that does not bound total work: layers can share a subgraph,
so a small table can expand exponentially, a `PaintGlyph` walks its
child twice when the fill-glyph shortcut does not apply, and every clip, layer
and fill costs a full pass over the raster. Before either traversal starts,
the glyph's Paint graph is now costed once per distinct paint into an upper
bound on paint visits, raster pixel passes and live intermediate buffers, and
a glyph over 65,536 visits, 2^30 pixel passes, or 256 MiB of live buffers at
its slot size returns no color raster and uses the monochrome fallback, as any
other failed color glyph does. Once a bounds or raster callback fails, later
callbacks no longer allocate or paint. A new project-authored fixture,
`colr-v1-budget.ttf`, with its generator, holds an ordinary glyph, doubling
shared subgraphs, nested glyph clips, a 255-layer list and 30 nested
composites; each over-budget case rendered before this change and is refused
now, and the cost of each refused case is checked against the budget it
exceeds. The existing color fixtures and their pixel checks are unchanged.
Against the 4,141 glyphs of the COLR v1 Noto Color Emoji subsets served by
Google Fonts, no glyph is refused at slot sizes up to 200 by 200 pixels; 2
are refused at 300 by 300 and 18 at 512 by 512. The raster code is shared by
Linux, macOS and Windows.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,584 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), and production and documentation guards.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Open With keeps the program in place and derived ids inside applications

The Desktop Entry mapper now admits the program token before expanding any
argument. An `Exec` with no program, an empty program, or a field code other
than `%%` in the program position (`Exec=%f`, `Exec=%i %f`, `Exec=viewer%f`)
is refused and the entry is not offered; previously `%f` or `%u` there expanded
to the selected file, and a stripped code such as `%i` left the file as the
first element, so the file itself would have been launched as the program. A
mapping whose first element is not the admitted program is refused as well.
The unit test that let an empty `Exec` degrade to a lone path now expects the
refusal enumeration already applied to it.

Desktop-id resolution now checks every candidate derived by dash-to-slash
expansion, not only the original id: a candidate is read only when each
component is a nonempty normal name, so `..-outside.desktop` no longer reads
`applications/../outside.desktop`, a leading dash no longer derives an
absolute path, and a doubled or trailing dash no longer resolves a different
id. Ids such as `org-gnome-eog.desktop` and `foo-bar-editor.desktop` resolve as
before. This Desktop Entry resolver runs on Unix systems other than macOS;
macOS uses its bundle mapper and Windows does not use it.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,582 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), and production and documentation guards.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Snapshot restore refuses history lines over the live ceiling

Restoring a snapshot refuses one whose scrollback holds a logical line with
more than 2^20 source cells, the per-line ceiling the live scrollback store
enforces. The store never retains such a line, so no capture of a real session
produces one; restore previously accepted it and rebuilt a line the live store
could never hold. The check runs before history is rebuilt and reports the new
`LogicalLineTooLarge` snapshot error. It counts only scrollback rows, so a
line continuing into the visible grid is measured by its history part, and it
does not count layout padding slots or the blank fill of a line's final row,
because capture re-projects history at the current width. An honest capture of
a line held at exactly the ceiling, one continuing into visible rows, and one
captured after a width change all restore unchanged. Attaching to a session
whose snapshot is refused fails with that error, as any other refused snapshot
does; the session-host attach path is Unix-only, and the check is in the shared
core. A stored-cell hydration test now hydrates from the marks originally
offered, not from the cell's already truncated list.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,583 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed. Clippy
under the pinned Rust 1.96 toolchain reported only an existing lint in a file
this change does not touch, and passed with that one lint allowed.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- macOS shared-memory copy retries its wait and rechecks size

The macOS reader for Kitty `t=s` shared memory copies the segment in a child
process. Its single `waitpid` call now retries when a signal interrupts it, so
the child is always reaped and an interrupted wait no longer fails the
transfer. The reader also re-reads the segment size after the copy and refuses
a segment resized during it, the check the Linux reader already made; both
readers now share that check. The wait and size calls go through a small seam
so tests can interrupt the wait and resize the segment deterministically. The
seam's helper tests run on every Unix; the isolated-copy tests run only on
macOS, where only the CI leg executes them. Linux behavior is unchanged, and
Windows keeps `t=s` unsupported.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,526 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed. The
isolated-copy tests compile for macOS here and run only on the macOS CI leg.

## 2026-10-06 -- Command-end boundaries follow layout padding across reflow

A command-end boundary recorded with an OSC 133 `D` mark is a cell offset
inside its logical line, measured with the line's layout-padding cells. Reflow
and scrollback projection drop that padding and add new padding at the new
width, but carried the offset unchanged, so after a resize the finished
output of a command could reach into the following prompt. The visible reflow
and the scrollback projection now follow the boundary to its cell, the same
way the cursor is followed, and the scrollback mark queries return the
projected boundary. A mark stamped on a continuation row, when its line began
above the visible screen, is now measured from the start of the line when the
rows join.

A wide character printed into a one-column grid now degrades to one column, as
reflow already does, instead of wrapping onto a fresh row each time and
leaving blank rows behind. OSC 133 sub-command letters must be the whole first
field: `AX`, `BX`, `CX`, `DX` and `PX` no longer stamp marks, end commands,
or change click-to-position and edit-region state.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,534 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), and production and documentation guards.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Refuse invalid desktop entries and label root bundle paths

The "Open With..." mapper now refuses desktop entries the Desktop Entry
specification makes invalid: an `Exec` with an unlisted field code such as
`%z`, a literal `%` not written as `%%`, or an unterminated double quote.
Unlisted codes were previously stripped and an open quote ran to the end of
the line, so such an entry still produced an argv. Listed codes, including the
deprecated ones the specification says to ignore, expand as before. On macOS a
bundle path with no final component (`/`) now labels its row with the path
instead of an empty name. No launcher is invoked by any test.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,527 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Snapshot decode reserves only what the input can hold

Snapshot decode now sizes its section-table and row reservations by the bytes
actually remaining, and spends the cell budget row by row before reserving a
row's cells. A short input that declared a large section table, or a row
wider than its remaining bytes, previously reserved the declared size before
the first short read refused it, and a one-cell budget was applied only after
every row had been read. Accepted snapshots decode exactly as before; an
over-budget snapshot is refused with the same error, earlier.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,535 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Snapshots carry the print state resumed output continues from

Snapshot format v6 appends the print state to the layout section: the SGR
rendition and DECSCA protection the next character takes, and up to three
leading bytes of a UTF-8 scalar the output stream had not finished. Output
resumed after a restore previously printed with the default rendition, and a
scalar split by the snapshot decoded as replacement characters. Decode and
restore admit pending bytes only when they start exactly one unfinished
scalar, and cap them at three. Link ids are not carried, as for cells.
Formats v1 through v5 restore at the default rendition with nothing pending.
A partial escape sequence is still not carried. The session-host handshake
already requires matching snapshot versions, so a v6 client and an older host
refuse each other as before.

The full-envelope byte pin, the v2 charset fixture and the v4 extension
fixture now account for the twelve appended default bytes; no other expected
output changed.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,531 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed.

## 2026-10-06 -- Kitty control values, crop extents, queries and RGB budget

Kitty graphics commands now refuse a known control key whose value does not
parse as its type, such as `a=TT`, `t=dd`, `o=zz`, `w=x`, `m=x`, or an id past
the 32-bit range. Such a key was previously treated as absent, so `a=TT` ran
as a plain transmit, `t=dd` read the payload as direct bytes, and `w=x` showed
the rest of the image. Unknown keys stay ignored, and a value only the
unsigned reading of `X=`/`Y=` accepts is still valid.

A zero or omitted crop width or height now measures the rest of the image from
the crop origin `x=`/`y=`, not from the image edge, so the default cell extent
of a cropped placement matches its visible region. A query (`a=q`) now reads
its payload through its `t=` medium under the same named-transport gate as a
transmission, instead of decoding a path or segment name as pixels. A command
that abandons an unfinished chunked transfer answers at its own quiet level
instead of inheriting the abandoned transfer's. Raw RGB data is refused at the
decoded-image budget before its RGBA expansion is allocated.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,547 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Kitty cursor movement follows the protocol default

Behavior change: the Kitty `C=` cursor movement policy was inverted. A
placement without `C=` (or with `C=0`) left the cursor where it was, and
`C=1` moved it to column 0 of the row below the image. The protocol defines
the opposite, and the published graphics documentation already described it.
Now the default moves the cursor right by the placement's columns and down by
its rows less one, as kitty's own put handler does; a column at or past the
right edge wraps to the start of the next row, an overshoot past the bottom
margin scrolls the region by that many lines through the ordinary linefeed
path, and the cursor is then clamped to the screen, or to the margins under
origin mode. `C=1` leaves the cursor in place. Relative placements (`P=`)
never move the cursor; their parent keys remain unimplemented, which the
graphics documentation now states. Virtual placements are unchanged.

Two existing tests pinned the inverted policy and now assert the protocol
behavior: the default transmit-and-display test and the former `C=1` movement
test. Ten new tests cover the default, explicit `C=0`, `C=1`, the right edge,
the bottom row, a bottom overshoot, a scroll region, relative placements,
display of a stored image, and virtual placements. Comments now state that
only frame commands default `f=` to 32-bit RGBA, that frame deletion accepts
an image id or number, and that zlib payloads inflate incrementally; a stale
test heading no longer calls shipped behavior out of scope.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,533 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec passed; five default-thread runs of the touched tests passed.

## 2026-10-06 -- Keep tab-drag input checks compatible with Rust 1.96

Tab-drag input custody now rejects synthetic presses with an early return before testing modifier keys. This avoids a Rust 1.96 Clippy boolean simplification error. Escape, modifier-only events, real key presses, and negotiated key-up reports retain their existing behavior.

Validation: the full local gate passes, including pinned Rust 1.96 Clippy, Linux/macOS/Windows Clippy, locked serial tests, production-file and documentation guards, RustSec audit, and the locked parser-graphics API check. Existing custody, Hyprland and tear-out tests pass five runs each at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-06 -- Core fixtures check overflow owners, notification admission and full state

Core fixtures now exercise the sixteen-mark boundary and its separately retained overflow owner, assert duplicate notification admission before queue eviction, check the intermediate state of a rectangle attribute reversal, and require complete grid and hyperlink state to survive ignored window operations and reflow. The mark-density comparison counts only retained scrollback cells, and fixture comments state their current scope. Test-only change; production behavior is unchanged.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Linux/macOS/Windows clippy, locked serial cargo test, production-file and documentation guards, RustSec audit, and the locked parser-graphics API check pass.

## 2026-10-06 -- Shared-memory test fixtures use flags macOS accepts

The exclusive shared-memory fixtures added to the graphics fuzz and Kitty
transport tests passed `O_CLOEXEC` to `shm_open`. macOS accepts only the
access, creation, exclusive and truncate flags there and returned `EINVAL`,
so the macOS test leg failed while Linux, which always sets close-on-exec for
shared memory, passed. The fixtures now pass only `O_CREAT`, `O_EXCL` and
`O_RDWR` (or `O_RDONLY` for the lookup), and a scan of every other
`shm_open` call found no other unsupported flag. Test-only change.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, and the graphics fuzz and Kitty transport test modules (serial
and five default-thread runs) passed. The macOS runtime result comes from CI.

## 2026-10-06 -- Hyprland live tab follow with bounded release fallback

Live tab drag now follows the logical global cursor on Hyprland through
bounded, window-specific IPC. The provisional window floats during the drag
and tiles on the monitor and workspace captured at release. Surface-incarnation
initial titles prevent delayed work from targeting a replacement window.
The mailbox holds one latest frame or drop, and movement is frame-throttled.

An IPC failure retires the floating preview without relying on a failed
connection to retile it. Terminal custody remains reversible until a
release-time replacement opens or cancellation restores the source. A
replacement is not mapped during an unresolved external focus gap. The shared
process-focus policy handles Hyprland activating the new window. With live tab
drag off, the previous release-time path remains unchanged. Other Wayland
compositors retain release-time placement. X11, macOS and Windows use their
existing native follow path; no child spawning or PTY policy is added.

Validation: formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the serial suite
(6,462 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production and documentation
guards, RustSec and the locked fuzz manifest passed. All five default-thread
runs passed the 33 focused custody tests and the 20 Hyprland tests.

## 2026-10-06 -- Reversible live tab drag on native positioning backends

Live tab drag in Settings follows the pointer on X11, macOS and Windows.
The default-on control creates a provisional tab window while the pointer is
held outside; release commits once, and cancellation restores tab order,
source focus identity and split ownership. A sole source remains alive until
completion. Provisional painting preserves terminal geometry, PTYs, recorders
and autosave ownership. Pointer offsets follow destination scale and native
decoration insets, and placement is clamped to the pointer's monitor.

Escape, returning to the strip, window close, suspension and failed creation
end provisional custody safely. Focus changes within OdyTTY keep the drag
alive; losing all process focus cancels after a bounded 150 ms grace period.
A release during that gap waits for classification. Cancellation requests
source focus only while OdyTTY holds focus, and ordinary input at either
custody window cancels first and routes through the restored source. Turning the setting off keeps
release-time creation, including its existing cursor and repaint behavior at
focus, overlay and session boundaries. Returning to the strip resumes the held
reorder gesture. Source resizing updates return geometry without ending
custody. Modifier-only events, synthetic presses and automation polling
preserve the drag; real key presses and mutating automation commands cancel.
Release never requests focus from another application. Wayland retains its existing release-time behavior;
menu moves, the menu's sole-tab refusal, and pane and workspace drag limits
are unchanged. Windows adds no child spawn or provisional ConPTY resize.

Validation: the local landing gate passed formatting, Linux, Windows and macOS
clippy, the serial suite (6,452 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production
and documentation guards, RustSec and the locked fuzz manifest. All five
default-thread runs passed the 30 focused custody and input tests.
