## 2026-10-06 -- Strengthen snapshot, placeholder and transport test coverage

Snapshot tests now use the actual little-endian wire format, distinguish short reads, and restore oversized-metadata snapshots before comparing retained text and cursor state. The grapheme oracle includes real wide owners and excludes source-less wrapping padding. Placeholder lookup coverage checks all 297 entries. Parser coverage explicitly pins dynamic color and layout-padding fields while preserving every historical golden hash. Invalid Kitty queries require a nonempty framed error.

Portable opt-in file and temporary-file tests exercise owned bytes on all targets. The Windows reparse-point case is explicitly ignored until symlink creation apparatus is available; POSIX shared memory remains unsupported on Windows.

Validation: the complete local gate passed, including three-target lint checks, serial locked tests, both guards, RustSec and the locked fuzz API. Five default-thread portable transport runs passed. The old wide-owner oracle failed on an inserted source-less space; the corrected oracle passes with the same source text. Native Windows and macOS execution awaits CI.

## 2026-10-06 -- Legacy mouse encoders reject oversized coordinates

The default and UTF-8 (mode 1005) mouse encoders narrowed a cell coordinate
to 32 bits before adding the protocol offset. An extreme coordinate panicked
in a debug build or wrapped in a release build, and on 64-bit targets a value
just above the 32-bit range could alias a small, valid cell. Both axes are now
converted and offset with checks, and a coordinate that does not fit is
dropped as unrepresentable, the same as any coordinate past the protocol's
range. Screen-sized coordinates encode exactly as before. SGR, SGR-pixel and
urxvt reports write decimal values and were not affected.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,437 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the mouse coordinate and encoding tests,
production-file and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed.
All eight new coordinate tests failed before the change.

## 2026-10-06 -- Restore, ambiguous-width reflow and line operations report their changes

Several state changes skipped the notifications callers rely on. Switching
the ambiguous-width policy reflowed the grid without advancing the render
revision, raising the prompt-mark latch, recounting button references, or
re-deriving the prompt input anchor; it is now finalized like a resize,
except that no prompt-start collapse is applied because no shell repaint
follows a policy change.

Restoring a snapshot into an existing terminal restarted the render revision
from a fresh screen, so a changed screen could report its previous revision,
and it reverted host settings such as the ambiguous-width policy, button
gates, cell metrics, scrollback limit and base colors to defaults. The
restore now keeps host-owned configuration, continues the revision sequence,
and raises the prompt-mark latch when marks existed before or after.
Constructing a terminal from an owned envelope now validates it before
allocating a grid of the requested size, so absurd dimensions return an error
instead of a capacity panic.

The prompt-mark change latch now also covers lowering the scrollback limit
when it trims history while marks exist, and insert-line, delete-line, region
scroll (SU, SD, reverse index and linefeed at a region bottom) when they move
or discard a marked row. Lowering the limit also releases the button
references of trimmed lines at once. Ordinary eviction of the oldest history
line remains reported through the scrollback trim epoch.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,439 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the restore tests and the core library tests,
production-file and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed.
All ten new restore and latch tests failed before the change.

## 2026-10-06 -- Failed Sixel keeps the pending wrap; input edge keeps wide owners

A Sixel stream that failed to decode, or whose image the store refused,
reported the unchanged cursor as a cursor move, which cleared a pending wrap.
After a full row, the next character then overwrote the last cell instead of
starting the next row. Both failure paths now report no cursor change, so the
cursor and its pending-wrap state are untouched; the error is still counted.

The heuristic right edge of a shell input region found the last content cell
and stopped at a wide glyph's lead, so with the cursor before a final wide
glyph the region ended between the lead and its continuation. The edge now
extends through the owner's continuation cells. The certainty level and the
fail-closed editing policy are unchanged.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,431 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the graphics-state and input-region tests,
production-file and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed.
Both new tests failed before the change.

## 2026-10-06 -- Button spans follow overwrites, invalidation and alternate resize

Several paths left button geometry or references out of step with the cells.
A point chip treated a space carrying a combining mark, underline,
strikethrough or inverse as empty and could be painted and clicked over that
output. Ordinary printed output over a finished label kept the label
clickable over the new text, and overwriting one half of a wide label glyph
blanked the other half without removing its span. Invalidating a code, or the
iTerm2-style invalidate-all, while that button's label run was still open
freed the definition but let the run stamp spans that referenced nothing, and
those spans used up the per-line span limit. Resizing while the alternate
screen was active rebuilt reference counts from the alternate grid only, so
buttons on the stored primary screen and in its scrollback were freed and
gone on return.

Point chips now treat only a visually empty cell as blank. Printed output and
wide-partner blanking release the spans they overwrite, the same as erases. An
open run whose definition is freed ends without stamping, and the
invalidate-all spelling of both button protocols ends an open run. The
reference rebuild counts the stored primary rows and scrollback.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,438 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the button tests, production-file and documentation
guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. All nine new button tests failed
before the change.

## 2026-10-06 -- Open With refuses desktop entries with quoted field codes

The Linux Open With picker expands a desktop entry's `Exec` line into an argv
vector and passes the file as one argument. It previously discarded which
tokens had been quoted, so an entry such as `sh -c "eog %f"` placed the file
path inside a shell code argument, where a crafted file name would run as
code. The Desktop Entry specification leaves field codes inside quoted
arguments undefined and requires `%F` and `%U` to stand alone.

The tokenizer now records which tokens contain quoted text. An entry with any
field code other than `%%` in such a token, or with `%F` or `%U` inside a
longer argument, is refused before any argv is built and is not offered in
the picker. Ordinary entries are unchanged: a separate `%f`, `%F`, `%u` or
`%U`, a quoted program path, `%%` inside quotes, and an unquoted
`--file=%f` all expand as before. Desktop entries are a Linux surface;
macOS builds its Open With rows from application bundles and Windows has no
desktop-entry parser.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,434 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the desktop tests, production-file and documentation
guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. The three refusal tests failed
before the change.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Resize trace moves into the private state directory

`ODYTTY_REFLOW_TRACE` previously appended to a fixed file name in the shared OS
temp directory with an ordinary open, so a planted symlink at that name could
redirect trace lines into another file and a planted FIFO could block the
resize that wrote the trace. The trace now writes `odytty-reflow-trace.log`
inside the same state directory as `odytty.log`, prepared as an owner-private
leaf and opened through the no-follow, non-blocking, ownership-checked append
helper used by the runtime log, panic log and memory report. A symlink, FIFO,
or foreign-owned object at the trace path is refused, the trace line is
skipped, and the resize completes normally. On Windows the trace moves from
`%TEMP%` to the per-user state directory (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\odytty`) and keeps
its inherited ACL; Windows has no symlink or FIFO check in this path. The
trace still records geometry and cursor coordinates only. New Linux
regression tests run the resize in an isolated child process with both the
old and new destinations planted inside a private fixture directory.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,433 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the trace tests, production-file and documentation
guards, and the RustSec audit all passed. The new trace tests failed before the
change.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Kitty named transports bind reads and deletions to their objects

The opt-in Kitty file, temporary-file and shared-memory transports checked
that a path's directory resolved inside an allowed temp root, then opened the
path again by name, so a directory swapped for a link after the check could
redirect the read outside the temp roots. Deletion after a successful read
also went by name, so a name rebound in between could remove a different
object.

On Unix the transport now opens the directory first, requires its canonical
path to name that same open directory inside an allowed root, and opens the
file relative to the directory handle without following a final link. On
Windows the opened file handle's own final location must lie inside an allowed
root before any byte is read. For `t=t`, Unix deletes relative to the admitted
directory and only while the name still names the file that was read, and
Windows marks the open handle itself for deletion. For `t=s`, the name is
reopened and compared with the object that was read before `shm_unlink`. A
rebound name is kept and the transfer fails with `EPERM:object-changed`. Named
transports remain off by default. The graphics security notes now describe the
Windows final-component handling as it already was, rather than as a plain
open.

New tests rebind names and swap directories at the exact points a concurrent
process could, using exclusively created fixture directories and
shared-memory names that are removed even when an assertion fails.

Validation: cargo fmt --check, Clippy with -D warnings on Linux, Windows and
macOS targets, serial locked tests (6,439 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), five
default-thread runs of the transport and graphics fuzz tests,
production-file and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit all passed.
The four rebinding and directory-swap tests failed before the change. The
Windows handle path is compiled and linted here and runs only on the Windows
CI leg.

Kitty file and temporary-file tests now use exclusively created private scratch directories with cleanup on unwinding. POSIX shared-memory fixtures use short per-process names, exclusive creation, owned descriptors and cleanup guards. Missing-object tests never remove existing objects during setup. One-character name admission now uses a fixture-owned object. New checks pin collision protection and panic cleanup.

Validation: the full local gate passed, including Linux, Windows and macOS cross-target lint checks, serial locked tests, documentation and production-file guards, RustSec and the locked fuzz API check. Test-only change. The full Unicode bidi corpus checks now fail on an empty

counts. Graphics fuzz recovery checks require a distinct glyph at the target

## 2026-10-06 -- Tab and workspace drags end when their release is lost

A tab or workspace rail drag could outlive its own release. When the close
confirmation opened mid-drag, or any dialog took the button release, the
gesture stayed recorded: a tear-out drag kept its New window badge on screen,
and the next left press anywhere was swallowed as that drag's press, so its
release committed the stale gesture and reordered tabs or workspaces far from
where the drag began. Both close confirmations now end the chrome drags
before the dialog opens, a dialog or pointer-owning modal that receives a
button event ends them uncommitted, and a left press that arrives while a drag
is still recorded ends it and is routed as an ordinary press. The source tab
closing or exiting mid-drag, Escape, focus loss, and a resize already ended
or revalidated the gesture and are now covered by tests.

The workspace rail is the only other drag surface: its slots are workspaces,
not tabs, so a rail drag still only reorders workspaces, and dragging a
workspace out of the window remains unsupported. Hyprland's release-time
destination query stays on the UI thread within its existing 400 ms budget.

Validation: the close-confirmation, overlay-release, and lost-release
regressions for both the tab strip and the workspace rail fail on the prior
code. Full local gate: formatting, three-target clippy, serial tests (6425
passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production-file and documentation guards, and
RustSec all pass; the 93 tear-out and drag tests pass five runs at the default
thread count.

## 2026-10-06 -- Keep synthetic font licenses beside fixtures and clarify owner reordering

The project-authored SVG, bidi, Arabic harakat, Latin-less proportional, and
keycap font fixtures now carry a copy of their GPL license in the fixture
directory, linked from their provenance README. The Noto subsets retain their
individual SIL Open Font License files. Font bytes, generators, rendering,
and terminal behavior are unchanged.

The feature reference and shaping roadmap now consistently describe how
bidirectional display reordering moves a shaped complex-script owner as one
whole span, keeping its cells adjacent and in order. The roadmap's introductory
support summary includes font-backed shaping inside enabled owners; shaping
across owner boundaries and scripts outside the enabled groups remain outside
the model.

Validation: formatting; clippy on Linux and for Windows and macOS; locked
serial tests (0 failed); production-file and documentation guards; RustSec
for both lockfiles; and the locked fuzz-bin check all pass. The documentation
guard and its 16 regression tests, plus formatting, pass after the prose-only
corrections.

## 2026-10-06 -- Tab tear-out into a new window

A tab dragged from the horizontal strip at least 16 physical pixels beyond the
window edge shows a New window badge, which joins the render signature.
Releasing there moves the tab and its panes into a new window through the
existing same-process move transaction: sessions, PTYs, history, images, and
selection stay intact and no shell is spawned. Escape and focus loss cancel,
and returning inside the window keeps ordinary tab reorder. A lone tab can
tear out; its emptied source window closes and hands over the saved-layout
role. A refused window creation restores the tab to its source. The palette
and right-click move commands keep their lone-tab refusal. Dragging an
individual pane out is unsupported.

Placement follows the platform. On X11, macOS, and Windows the new window
opens at the global release point, clamped to the monitor under it. On
Hyprland the release-time global cursor selects the monitor and workspace
(a visible special workspace takes precedence), and the new window is tiled
there through bounded, owner-verified IPC that targets it by its unique
initial title and process id, never by focus. If that placement is
unavailable, the tab stays in its new window, Hyprland places it, and a
notice says so. On other Wayland compositors, including KDE Plasma and GNOME,
the compositor chooses placement. Live follow of the window during the drag
is unsupported.

Validation: the pointer regression fails on the prior code, as do the
special-workspace, recycled-address, resize, and inactive-tab regressions on
their prior paths. Full local gate: formatting, three-target clippy, serial
tests (6416 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), production-file and documentation
guards, RustSec, and the locked fuzz manifest all pass; the 21 tear-out tests
pass five runs at the default thread count.

---

## 2026-10-06 -- Combined coverage for shaped owners, and their reordering documented

New tests exercise shaped complex-script owners together with the other
presentation and model paths, which the per-script suites covered one at a
time. One row holds a Latin `->` ligature, the family emoji ZWJ cluster, and
a Devanagari conjunct: the runs never overlap, the Latin and emoji columns
draw exactly as they do without the owner, and the owner draws exactly as it
does alone. Another row holds `a->b`, Arabic beh lam alef, and the conjunct:
with Script shaping off the Latin run stays, with Programming ligatures off
the Arabic joining run and the owner stay, and each switch changes only the
pixels of its own scope. CJK wide and ambiguous cells beside owners keep
their columns with ambiguous width narrow and wide. Inside a right-to-left
isolate a conjunct owner moves as one unit, keeps its cells in order, and its
shaped run draws whole at its visual columns. Reflow at five widths moves
owners across generated wrap padding without splitting them and keeps their
shaped pixels, logical copy, logical search, and hyperlinks, never putting a
link on padding. Every cell of a linked owner carries the link, search spans
whole owners, a selection holding an owner's lead copies the whole owner, and
pointer columns under the display plan map back to the linked cells. Each
frame of a stream split into 1- to 4-byte reads holds exactly the complete
scalars received so far, shapes only whole owners, and ends on the one-shot
cells, runs, and pixels. Snapshot restore keeps shaped, emoji, and bidi rows,
their display plan, runs, and pixels; on Unix a session host reattach restores
such a row cell for cell. A Kitty image placed after an owner leaves its
cells, copy, and run unchanged. Toggling Programming ligatures in Settings
over shaped content re-keys the frame both ways, persists on Save, leaves
Script shaping on, and leaves cells and copy unchanged.

The feature reference and the shaping roadmap said that display reordering
leaves shaped owners on their own cells. That holds in plain left-to-right
text, but inside a right-to-left isolate the owner moves with the reordered
text. Both now state the tested behavior: the owner is never split, moves as
one unit, keeps its cells adjacent and in order, and its shaped run draws
whole at their visual columns. No product code changed.

Validation: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D
warnings` on Linux and for the Windows and macOS targets; `cargo test
--locked` with one test thread (6397 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored); the
production-file guard; the documentation guard and its tests; and the RustSec
audit. The new tests also passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-06 -- Separate script shaping from programming ligatures

A new default-on Script shaping setting (`script_shaping`,
`ODYTTY_SCRIPT_SHAPING`) controls Arabic contextual joining and harakat
positioning plus the font shaping of every enabled complex-script owner group.
Programming ligatures now controls only Latin/operator `calt` and `liga` and
its optional `ss01`/`ss02` settings. Both rows sit next to each other in
Settings > Rendering, reachable from the right-click Settings item, and apply
live. Alternate zero stays independent of both.

This deliberately changes behavior introduced in v0.11.0: turning programming
ligatures off no longer disables Arabic joining, and Arabic and complex-script
forms keep shaping unless Script shaping is off. Each switch, when off,
restores the previous per-cell output exactly for its own scope. Initial
frames, single panes, split panes, and reordered Arabic use the same
independent controls on Linux Wayland, Linux X11, macOS, and Windows.

Validation: an App-level test drives the real Settings input path and checks
frame re-keying and exact pixels for a Devanagari conjunct, Arabic with
harakat, and a programming arrow, with bidirectional reordering on and off.
Restoring the former combined gate in either the Arabic or the owner path makes
it fail. Settings tests cover defaults, environment and config overrides, live
publication, and saved values. Full local gate: formatting, three-target
clippy, serial tests (0 failed), production-file and documentation guards,
RustSec, and the locked fuzz manifest all pass; five default-thread runs of
the affected suites pass.

## 2026-10-05 -- Chakma, Javanese, Grantha, and Tai Tham owner shaping

Chakma, Javanese, Grantha, and Tai Tham width owners now use font-backed
shaping while programming ligatures are on. Stacked and conjunct forms,
pre-base and split vowels, cakra and medials draw inside their one or two
cells with bounded fitting. Shaping respects the retained ownership rules,
including separate Chakma owners when U+11134 is followed by ZWJ. Logical
cells, widths, cursor positions, selection, and copied text retain their
existing semantics; turning ligatures off restores scalar glyphs.

All 27 licensed reference rows match HarfBuzz 14.5 exactly. Twenty-two
single-owner samples draw the reference frame both directly and through the
fallback chain beneath a monospace primary face.

## 2026-10-05 -- Tai Tham stacks after a spacing sign

A Tai Tham medial or spacing vowel sign written before SAKOT (U+1A60) no
longer ends the stacked consonant's link back to its base: the reverse linker
scan now passes over a retained same-script spacing sign once it has seen the
stacker, so the stack stays in one two-cell owner instead of splitting into
three or four cells. The rule is limited to Tai Tham. A sign after SAKOT, a
ZWNJ, a foreign base, and the same shape in Devanagari, Khmer, and Myanmar
keep their prior split. Of the 72 sequences from the frozen `ucs-detect`
corpus that still differed (71 Tai Tham, 1 Malayalam), 70 now measure the
reference width in the terminal model; a fresh corpus run is separate. The
other two, a Tai Tham row that starts with the dependent vowel U+1A63 and a
Malayalam row that starts with the chillu U+0D7B, keep three cells by design
and are documented as differences from the wcwidth reference.

Validation: `cargo fmt --check`, Linux, Windows, and macOS clippy with `-D warnings`,
`cargo test --locked` at one test thread (6,382 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored),
the production-file and documentation guards, the RustSec audit, and five
default-thread runs of the touched width tests.

## 2026-10-05 -- Thai, Lao, and Tibetan owner shaping

Thai, Lao, and Tibetan width owners now take the owner-shaping path while
programming ligatures are on. The font's shaping tables do real work for
these scripts even though their widths do not change: SARA AM decomposes into
its nikhahit and aa, tone marks switch to their lowered or shifted alternates
and stack above upper vowels instead of colliding with them, Thai yo ying
drops its descender before a below-base vowel, and Tibetan subjoined letters
form the font's precomposed stacks. Each owner draws inside its own one or two
cells by the same fit rule as the other shaped groups; tone marks and
Tibetan subjoined letters stay in their one-cell owners. Every licensed Thai,
Lao, and Tibetan reference sample matches HarfBuzz 14.5 exactly and draws the
same frame as the reference glyph run, with the fixture face as the style
face and from the fallback chain under a monospace style face.

## 2026-10-05 -- Northern Indic owner shaping

Devanagari, Bengali, Gurmukhi, Gujarati, and Odia width owners are now shaped
with the font while programming ligatures are on. Each owner is shaped on its
own and drawn as one overlay over exactly its one or two cells, so conjuncts,
reph, below-base and pre-base forms, and ZWJ/ZWNJ requests draw as the font
intends. Cells, widths, cursor addressing, selection, copy, search, reflow,
snapshots, and export are unchanged: shaping is presentation only and never
crosses an owner. The run keeps its shaped pen positions and is centered in
the owner's span when it fits; a wider or taller run scales uniformly down to
a 0.6 floor and is clipped at the span edges below it. The shaping face is
the first that maps every scalar: a `symbol_map` override, the style face,
then the fallback chain and runtime resolver. Owners with no covering face, a
pending runtime answer, a `.notdef` result, or no atlas room keep the
per-cell path, and `ligatures = off` restores it everywhere. The cursor block
redraws per-cell glyphs, as it does under a Latin ligature.

Shaping uses `harfrust` 0.8.4 (MIT), the HarfBuzz project's Rust port,
because `swash` does not form reph and mishandles ZWJ/ZWNJ in these scripts.
It reads fonts through the `read-fonts` 0.39 parser that `skrifa` already
brings, so no second font parser enters the build; `swash` keeps the Latin,
Arabic, and emoji paths. A same-machine Linux x86_64 release build grows by 847,632 bytes (2.0
percent).

Every northern sample in the licensed fixtures matches its HarfBuzz 14.5.1
reference except Bengali ka, virama, ra, which is asserted as a known
difference. Pixel tests draw each single-owner sample through the production
path and compare it with the reference glyph run; reph, conjunct, and pre-base
samples differ from the per-cell path that drew them before. Southern
Indic, Sinhala, Khmer, Myanmar, Thai, Lao, Tibetan, Chakma, Javanese,
Grantha, and Tai Tham owners keep the per-cell path.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec on both lockfiles, and the fuzz lockfile
check passed. The full suite passed 6,366 tests with 30 ignored; the
complex-shaping, atlas, ligature, grid, text, and emoji tests passed five runs
at the default thread count.

Flag, modifier and ZWJ shaping inputs remain unchanged. This CPU path is shared

## 2026-10-05 -- SVG-in-OpenType color glyphs

Color glyphs now render from SVG-in-OpenType documents when no bitmap strike,
COLR v0 layer set, or COLR v1 Paint graph draws the glyph. Earlier sources keep
their established pixels; a face carrying both COLR and SVG data still draws
its COLR glyph. The document's glyph element is fitted into the existing one-
or two-cell color atlas slot the way COLR v1 glyphs are, so the grid, cursor,
selection, and copy are unchanged and no new GPU resource is created.

Documents are untrusted. The `SVG ` index is read with checked arithmetic and
ignored when records overlap or run past the table. A document is at most
1 MiB raw and after gzip decompression, XML is parsed with DTDs refused and at
most 20,000 nodes, nesting is at most 64 deep, and `use`, `href`, and
`url(#id)` references may expand to at most 80,000 nodes with cycles refused.
Documents with patterns, whose tile rasters resvg sizes from the transform,
and documents with stylesheet `url(` references are refused. Every image
resolver returns nothing, so no file, network URL, or embedded image is read;
scripts and animation have no effect, and SVG text is not drawn. A refused or
empty document keeps the monochrome fallback, and a color-glyph key that fails
to rasterize is remembered in a bounded set so it is not reparsed every frame.
Directory discovery accepts a face whose only color data is an `SVG ` table,
ranked after every named and COLR/CPAL face, so hosts that found a color face
before find the same one.

Rendering uses `resvg` and `usvg` 0.45.1 (Apache-2.0 OR MIT) with every default
feature off; this release is built on the `tiny-skia` 0.11.4 already used for
COLR v1, so no second rasterizer enters the build. New transitive crates are
`roxmltree`, `svgtypes`, `kurbo`, `simplecss`, `siphasher`, `data-url`, and
`base64` (MIT OR Apache-2.0), `float-cmp`, `imagesize`, `rgb`, `pico-args`, and
`xmlwriter` (MIT), and `png` 0.17 (MIT OR Apache-2.0), pulled by `tiny-skia`'s
default PNG support beside the existing `png` 0.18. Both lockfiles, including
the parser and graphics fuzz lockfile, carry them; `euclid` (MIT OR
Apache-2.0) is also recorded as an optional `kurbo` dependency and is not
built. Two
project-authored fixture fonts with a reproducible generator cover rendering,
selection by glyph id, gzip, inert external references, every limit, ancestor
transforms, COLR precedence, discovery order, and malformed tables; the tests
run on the CPU, so all three CI platforms execute them.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec on both lockfiles, the locked fuzz lockfile
check, and byte-identical fixture regeneration passed. The full suite passed
6,308 tests with 30 ignored. Removing SVG from the source order fails four of
the fourteen new tests, and each of twelve further deliberate breakages of a
limit, resolver, cache, ranking, or transform fails at least one.

## 2026-10-05 -- Monospace probe requires M; proportional Arabic joining limit stated

The monospace probe skipped probe glyphs a face lacks, so a script-only face
whose only probe hit was a period passed as monospace. A proportional Arabic
face given as a `font_family` path was then accepted, and its cells were sized
from `.notdef` because the atlas measures cell width from `M`. The probe now
requires `M`; such a face reports that it is not monospace. Faces with `M`
are judged exactly as before.

Arabic joining draws each joined glyph from its own cell's left edge, so
strokes connect only when the font's joining forms advance one cell. A
proportional face loaded through the `font` path setting, which is not
checked for monospace, draws gaps between joined letters; the features guide,
shaping roadmap, and runtime knobs now state this. A project-authored
Latin-less proportional fixture font covers the probe.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The full suite passed 6,295
tests with 30 ignored; the new probe test fails with the `M` requirement
removed.

faces under the SIL Open Font License, each with its license text beside it,

generator checks every source font's hash, renumbers subset glyphs, and
reproduces identical fonts and tables. Every reference row agrees between the
subset and the original face; one Bengali ra-phala row records a known

unchanged: these fixtures are test inputs for the shaping work that follows.

and documentation guards, and RustSec on both lockfiles passed.

## 2026-10-05 -- Color-emoji discovery probes table directories only

Directory discovery checks each font file not named after a known
color-emoji face for COLR and CPAL tables. That check read the whole file,
up to the 256 MiB font-file ceiling, and parsed it, so a host with no named
color-emoji face could read every installed font in full. The check now reads
the first face's header and table directory only: the same size ceiling and
regular-file rule, the same sfnt or collection magic, the same binary search
over the table records, and the same requirement that each table's byte range
lie inside the file. Answers are unchanged. A differential test compares the
directory probe with the parsed-font answer over bundled and fixture fonts and
malformed variants of each (truncated headers and directories, out-of-file
and overflowing table ranges, an unsorted directory, a wrong magic, an
inflated table count, and collection wrappers); a reader test pins that only
the magic, table count, and table records are read. Both tests pass against
the whole-file probe as well, because the answers did not change; deliberate
breakages of the range check, the search, collection support, and the read
bound each fail one of them. The body-family metadata reader still parses
whole files, because it needs the cmap, name, OS/2, and post tables.

The doc comment for the color-emoji candidate loader is moved from its
test-only wrapper onto the loader itself.

Arabic documentation now states plainly that, with bidirectional reordering
off, letters keep the contextual forms of their typed neighbors but sit left
to right, so their connecting strokes face away from each other and a word
draws as one connected shape only with reordering on. This is the existing
behavior, unchanged.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests (6,246
passed, 0 failed, 72 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec on both lockfiles pass; the emoji and font-file tests pass five runs
at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Font discovery falls through faces that fail to load

Several host font lookups chose one file and gave up when that file failed
to load, even though another installed face would have worked. Color emoji
took fontconfig's answer, or the first file named after a known color-emoji
face, and a load failure left the session without color emoji. The host
Nerd face took the best-ranked file only. Each Linux or Windows static
symbol-tail hint was dropped when its first matching file failed. A
configured `font_weight` face fell back to the regular face after its first
candidate failed. Each lookup now ranks its candidates and loads them in
order, at most eight attempts: color emoji tries fontconfig's answer, then
named color-emoji files, then other COLR/CPAL faces; the Nerd face keeps the
dedicated Symbols Nerd Font first; a tail hint still adds nothing when its
first match is a face an earlier hint loaded. When the first candidate loads,
every result is unchanged. The body family's regular and style faces are
exempt because they are chosen only among faces whose metadata was already
parsed, and the runtime per-codepoint query already tried its candidates in
order.

Five hermetic regressions, using temporary font roots that hold a broken file
beside a copy of a bundled face, fail with the previous single-candidate
behavior. A Windows counterpart for the static tail runs only on the Windows
CI leg, and one more test pins the unchanged de-duplication across hints. No
font path from the build machine appears in the tests or their output.

The `outline_glyph` doc comment in the font handle, which had been displaced
onto `px_per_unit` when that helper was added, is restored to its function.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests (6,217
passed, 0 failed, 72 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec on both lockfiles pass; the font discovery, emoji, and text tests
pass five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Sinhala terminal width ownership

Sinhala dependent spacing signs and virama-linked consonants now share bounded
source owners. Spacing signs or linked consonants promote a measured letter
to two cells; repeated stacks remain two cells. ZWJ preserves the virama link
and ZWNJ prevents joining. Terminal width ownership can cross a Unicode
grapheme boundary. Nonspacing marks, other script groups, the seventeen-scalar
source bound, and lossless overflow retain their prior policy. Font-backed
complex-script reordering remains unsupported.

The existing Unicode 17 generator includes Sinhala with the same bounded
runtime as northern and southern Indic. Licensed property fixtures cover all
111 assigned Sinhala Script scalars. Authored streams exercise byte splits,
snapshot continuation, wrap padding, insertion, edits, copy, search, history,
reflow, redraw, alternate screens, replay, and plain/HTML export. Nine
ownership regressions fail before the change and pass after it. No full
grapheme-conformance or new ucs-detect result is claimed.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec on both lockfiles, orchestration checks,
and byte-identical Unicode data regeneration pass.

## 2026-10-05 -- Arabic harakat inside joining runs

An Arabic letter carrying harakat now stays in its joining run instead of
breaking it. Before, a fatha or shadda on one letter split the word: the
marked letter drew in its isolated form and the letters after it joined as if
they started a new word. Harakat here are the Arabic and Arabic Extended-A
nonspacing marks, except U+08CA through U+08D2, which the shaping engine's
character data does not yet treat as transparent. The font's mark
composition and mark-to-base positioning apply: each shaped mark records its
offset from its base in font units, the glyph atlas rasterizes it at that
offset, and the renderer draws it inside the run's cells without also
drawing the monochrome mark. Within a run of two or more Arabic cells every
marked letter draws through the overlay, even where joining leaves its form
unchanged, so marks along a word take consistent positions. A marked cell
whose marks the text font does not map, and any non-harakat mark on an
Arabic letter, keep the monochrome combining path and split the run, so a
missing mark never draws as a box. Unmarked runs, Latin runs, and every
atlas key without a mark offset are unchanged. Copy, search, selection, and
cursor addressing still report the logical characters.

A new project-authored fixture face, `arabic-marks.ttf`, with its reproducible
generator, supplies joining forms, a composed shadda-fatha, a mark-to-base
anchor, a shadda with a full-cell font advance as many monospace faces give
their marks, an unattached kasra, and a deliberately unmapped damma. An
attached mark's own font advance no longer moves the pen of later marks on the
same letter. Eight of the ten new regressions, and the rewritten
run-membership test, fail with the previous eligibility rule; the other two
pin the harakat table and the monochrome fallback. Further mutations show the
offset, the mark-advance rule, atlas placement, the unmapped-mark split, and
the unchanged-form overlay each pinned by at least one test, including a
display-order test that keeps the mark on its base's visual cell with
`bidi_reorder` on. The ligature unit tests moved to their own file, which
leaves the shaping module well under the production-file limit.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests (6,196
passed, 0 failed, 72 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec on both lockfiles pass; the bidi, ligature, and atlas tests pass five
runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Bidi display shaping follows resolved levels

With `bidi_reorder` on, the frame's ligature shaping ignored the display
map: it shaped every row in logical order, so a programming ligature such as
`->` between two Hebrew letters still drew as one unmirrored arrow, and a
Latin run straddling a direction boundary was dropped to scalar glyphs as a
whole. The display-order pixel fixtures exercised a separate shaping path
that split runs by level, so they did not detect the gap. Frame shaping now
uses that level-aware path for every reordered row: compatible runs split
at each level change, Arabic joining runs at a right-to-left level shape
right to left, and Latin and operator runs at a right-to-left level stay
unshaped so their characters draw mirrored. Rows drawn in logical order, and
every frame with the setting off, shape and cache exactly as before. Cached
row plans for reordered rows are keyed by their level vector, so a row
shaped with the setting off is never reused once it is on.

A regression drives one long-lived shaper through the same row with the
setting off, on, and off again, and checks the reordered frame against the
hand-ordered visual string. It fails when reordered rows shape without
levels, and when the plan cache ignores levels. The frame wiring itself is
covered by inspection; no GPU frame capture is claimed.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests (6,186
passed, 0 failed, 72 ignored), production and documentation guards, and
RustSec on both lockfiles pass; the bidi and ligature tests pass five runs at
the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Southern Indic terminal width ownership

Tamil, Telugu, Kannada, and Malayalam retain same-script spacing signs and
virama-linked consonants with their source owner. Spacing signs or linked
consonants promote a measured base to two cells; repeated stacks remain two
cells. Tamil terminal width units can cross an extended-grapheme boundary.
ZWJ preserves the link and ZWNJ prevents joining. The seventeen-scalar bound,
lossless overflow, and logical copy semantics remain unchanged. Font-backed
complex-script reordering remains unsupported.

The existing Unicode 17 generator now emits the four southern scripts using
the same bounded policy as the northern scripts. Licensed property fixtures
cover all assigned scalars in the four scripts, and authored redraw, table,
byte-split, snapshot, edit/erase, wrapping, insertion, history, copy, search,
replay, and plain/HTML export regressions exercise their source owners.
Thirty-six ownership regressions fail before the change and pass after it.
The property-derived corpus is not a full grapheme-conformance result; no new
ucs-detect result is claimed.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec on both lockfiles, orchestration checks,
and byte-identical Unicode data regeneration pass.

## 2026-10-05 -- Opt-in bidirectional text display

Bidirectional display reordering is now a user setting, `bidi_reorder`, off
by default. It is reachable from Settings > Rendering > Bidirectional text,
from a **Reorder Right-to-Left Text** toggle in the right-click menu (shown
with a check mark while on, in the Settings group), and from
`ODYTTY_BIDI_REORDER`. No key chord is added. While it is on, the primary
screen draws right-to-left runs such as Hebrew and Arabic in display order with
a left-to-right paragraph level, through the display map the previous test-only
stages built: per-frame planning with paragraph context, per-pane maps in
split tabs, the pointer and mouse reports mapped to the logical cell drawn
under the pointer, the cursor and its effects at their drawn columns, and
overlay text rows in logical order. The alternate screen is never reordered.
Cells, cursor addressing, selection, copy, search, scrollback export, and
protocol values stay logical. The setting applies live, re-keys the frame
cache, and persists on Save; the menu toggle changes the running window
without writing the configuration file. With it off, no map is planned and
rendering, input, and reports are unchanged, as the existing pixel fixtures and
pointer tests pin.

The support tables in the features guide and shaping roadmap, the SPEC out of
scope list, and the README known gaps now describe exactly this boundary. The
remaining limits are stated plainly: no right-to-left paragraph levels, no
alternate-screen reordering, no complex-script shaping, unpaired mirrored
characters draw unmirrored, block-selection export is not reordered, and image
placements are not reordered. The content right-click menu gains one row, and
right-click menus are one column wider to fit the check mark.

Tests drive the Settings row and the menu toggle through the real overlay key
and pointer paths: each applies live, bumps the presentation epoch, starts or
stops display-order planning, and the Settings row persists
`bidi_reorder = on` on Save. Further tests cover the off-by-default identity
pointer, the pointer mapping under the setting, and the alternate screen
staying unplanned and unmapped. With the setting ignored, four of the five new
tests fail; with the alternate screen planned, the alternate-screen test fails.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The bidi and
settings tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Bidi lifecycle checks behind a test-only gate

The test-only bidi display map is planned for every frame from the rows on
screen and the paragraph above them. New tests confirm that this holds across
the terminal's lifecycle. After a width change to 12, 16, 31, and back to 20
columns, the presented map equals the plan of the same output printed at that
width. At every history scroll position, each visible row is placed as it is
when the whole paragraph is on screen. A session restored from an encoded
snapshot plans identically to the live one at every scroll position, and
output delivered in 1, 2, 3, 5, or 7 byte reads plans as one read.
Scrollback export returns the logical text. No production code changed, and
shipping builds plan no map. Image placements are not part of the plan, and
the plan is not cached between frames.

With the paragraph context above the viewport removed from planning, the
history scroll test fails; the other four tests pin behavior that per-frame
planning already provides.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The bidi tests
passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Northern Indic terminal width ownership

Devanagari, Bengali, Gurmukhi, Gujarati, and Odia retain spacing signs and
virama-linked consonants with their source owner. Spacing signs or linked
consonants promote a measured base to two cells; repeated stacks remain two
cells. ZWJ preserves a virama link while ZWNJ prevents consonant joining.
Gurmukhi terminal width units can cross a Unicode extended-grapheme boundary.
Other script groups, emoji widths, and complex-script glyph shaping keep their
prior behavior. The seventeen-scalar source bound and overflow policy remain.

Unicode 17.0.0 properties bound the script and consonant policy. Licensed
fixtures cover all five scripts' spacing and consonant properties and a
nineteen-row GraphemeBreakTest subset. Authored terminal redraw and table
streams exercise source ownership alongside byte splits, edit/erase,
right-edge expansion, insertion, snapshots, history, reflow, copy, and search.
Four frozen ownership fixtures fail before the change and pass after it.
This is local corpus evidence; no new ucs-detect result is claimed.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec on both lockfiles, orchestration checks,
and byte-identical Unicode data regeneration pass.

## 2026-10-05 -- Bidi cursor effects and cell overlays behind a test-only gate

Behind the test-only bidi gate, the cursor slide, trail, large-jump follower,
and aura now run in drawn columns: each starts and ends at the screen cell the
cursor block is drawn at, in the single pane and in the focused split pane. A
one-cell logical move that crosses a reversed run can therefore jump on
screen, and then starts the follower exactly as the same screen jump does on
an unreordered line. When an overlay writes text into the cursor's row (the
input method pre-edit, for example), that row draws in logical order and the
cursor effects snap rather than glide toward a column the block has left. The
open-modifier path and URL underline, link hover, and button chips address the
logical cell under the pointer. Underlines and chip fills only change cell
attributes, so they draw on the visual cells of their logical cells; a chip
pill cap or the click hint writes text, so its row draws in logical order and
the pointer maps through that identity row. Shipping builds plan no map, so
every cursor effect and overlay is unchanged.

The single-pane cell-paint sequence moved into one function shared by the
frame and its headless test path, and the pane layout unit tests moved to
their own file, bringing `src/native/app/panes.rs` from 1,915 to 1,430 lines.
Neither move changes behavior.

Tests compare a reordered move against the same screen move on an
unreordered line for the glide, trail, and follower in the single pane and a
split pane, pin the glide direction inside a reversed run, check the aura and
follower placement at the drawn cursor column, check the snap when the
pre-edit rewrites the cursor's row, and drive the armed underline, the click
hint, and chip activation through the real pointer path. With the drawn-column
mapping, the snap, or the renderer placement removed, six of the ten new tests
fail; the four overlay tests pin behavior the pointer mapping already
provides. Resize, replay, and export are not yet mapped.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The bidi tests
passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Bidi split panes, mouse reports, and input anchoring behind a test-only gate

Behind the same test-only gate, each content pane of a split tab now plans
its own bidi display map from its terminal, with the paragraph context above
its viewport, and draws its cells and cursor through it. Rows an overlay
draws text over stay in logical order, and chrome strips take no map. The
pointer over a split pane addresses the logical cell drawn under it, through
the focused pane's map. Mouse reports to applications therefore name the
logical cell in the normal, SGR, and URXVT encodings, which matches cursor
addressing. An SGR-pixel report moves by whole cells onto that logical cell
and keeps its offset inside the cell. Hyperlink hover follows the logical
cell under the pointer. The input method candidate window anchors at the
screen column the cursor cell is drawn at. The single-pane frame cache key
includes a hash of the placement, so a frame whose placement alone changes is
rebuilt. Shipping builds plan no map, so display, input, and reports are
unchanged.

Tests cover cell-encoded reports in three encodings against the report for
the same logical cell with the gate off, a pinned SGR report on the reversed
run, SGR-pixel offsets on every column, hyperlink hover on a partly reversed
link, per-pane maps and pointer mapping in a two-pane split, map teardown
when the gate turns off, the input method anchor, the placement hash, and
the cache decision for a placement-only change. With the new mapping
removed, four of the seven new App-level tests fail; the three report and
hover tests pin behavior the pointer mapping already provides. Cursor effects, click hints, button
chips, resize, replay, and export are not yet mapped.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The bidi tests
passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Bidi cursor, pointer, and selection behind a test-only gate

Behind a test-only gate that no setting, menu, or flag reaches, the live
single-pane frame now draws its content grid in bidi display order. Shipping
builds pass no display map, so on-screen output and behavior are unchanged.
The frame plans the map from the terminal's soft-wrap flags and places it
inside the decorated frame, so tab-bar and rail cells stay in logical order.
Any content row an overlay draws text over also stays in logical order. The
cursor stays on its logical cell and draws at that cell's visual column, with
a mirrored character's mirror glyph under a block cursor. The pointer over a
screen column addresses the logical cell drawn there, so a drag across a
direction boundary selects logical cells and copies logical text. Selection
and search highlights land on the visual cells of their logical cells. Copy,
search results, and cursor addressing stay logical.

A paragraph that opens above the visible rows is now resolved together with
the soft-wrapped rows above it. Those rows come from scrollback or from the
screen above a scrolled viewport, and the live alternate screen has none.
Lookback stops at the plan's row and owner caps, and a longer paragraph keeps
the identity layout, as any over-cap paragraph already does. The display map
gains an inverse for hit testing and a frame embedding for chrome offsets.

Tests cover a paragraph whose opening row is in scrollback, hard breaks and
the alternate screen, the context cap, the inverse map on every cell, chrome
embedding, rows reset under overlay text, the live entry point against the
earlier seam, cursor styles on reordered and mirrored cells, split selection
highlights, logical copy and search, and App-level pointer hit testing and
drag selection through the real pointer path. With the map ignored in the
cursor, pointer, and paragraph context, four of the new tests fail. Split
panes, mouse reports to applications, the input method editor, cursor
effects, hyperlink hover, the frame cache key, resize, replay, and export are
not yet mapped.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The bidi, grid, and
pointer tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-05 -- Save re-reads the config file it wrote

A settings Save, and a theme-builder save, applied the result by re-reading
the config through a path derived from the live process environment rather
than through the path the write targeted. A shipping process resolves both
from the same unchanged environment, so they named the same file and running
windows were unaffected. A test that points the App at a temp config file
read a different file instead: with no config at the environment path, the
re-read applied defaults over the saved edits, so the running settings and
the settings panel disagreed after Save. The alternate-zero panel test then
saw no change on its toggle back and failed on every CI leg, while passing on
hosts whose own config differed from the defaults. Save now re-reads the
written file through the settings reloader, with the environment snapshot
taken at startup, so the applied settings always describe the saved file. A
file that disappears between the write and the re-read keeps the current
settings instead of falling back to defaults; the background poll reports it
as before. A new test pins the environment-derived config path to an empty
directory and checks that a saved `font_zero = on` stays applied and that the
toggle back re-keys the frame; it and the existing panel test fail without
the change.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The full library
suite passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-04 -- Alternate zero font control

A new `font_zero` setting (Settings > Fonts > Alternate zero, config
`font_zero`, env `ODYTTY_FONT_ZERO`) draws `0` with the body font's OpenType
`zero` feature, a slashed or dotted zero as the font designs it. It is off by
default, reloads live, and works with ligatures on or off. Each body face
resolves its `zero` substitution for `0` once through the shaper when the
control is applied, and the atlas rasterizes that glyph for the regular,
bold, italic, and bold-italic faces. Contextual shaping runs set `zero`
identically in both passes, so the control never creates or removes a
ligature and a shaped `0` matches the scalar one. A toggle re-derives the
body faces and rebuilds the glyph atlas and shape-plan cache. Fonts without
the feature, including the default Victor Mono, render byte-identically, and
cell width, height, and baseline are unchanged. Only `zero` is exposed: no
further stylistic sets and no raw feature tags.

Atlas fixtures on the bundled JetBrains Mono (which has `zero`) show that
only the `0` slot changes, in grayscale and subpixel atlases, and that cell
metrics match at six sizes and two line heights. Victor Mono and two fixture
faces without the feature keep the cmap glyph. Shaper tests pin unchanged
overlays and the shaped `0` glyph. An App-level test toggles the Fonts row
through the real overlay key route, checks the live apply and the render
signature, and saves `font_zero = on`. With the face resolution disabled,
the five glyph, pixel, styled-face, and shaped-glyph tests fail.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, and RustSec passed. The atlas, shaper,
and settings tests passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-04 -- Emoji sequence width regression fixtures

`tests/emoji_width_g7.rs` specifies emoji sequence widths before any width
change: VS16 presentation, ZWJ sequences, skin-tone modifiers, keycaps with
VS16, and regional-indicator flag pairs each own two cells. Each case covers
cell ownership and cursor position, every UTF-8 byte split, right-edge pending
wrap, overwrite and erase, logical copy and search, and snapshot roundtrip.
These 42 tests are ignored and fail on the current scalar-width model.

Three active controls pin behavior that stays unchanged: VS15 does not demote
an emoji-presentation base, a standalone regional indicator keeps one cell,
and a keycap without VS16 keeps a one-cell base with U+20E3 retained. VS15
non-demotion and standalone regional-indicator width deliberately differ from
the frozen wcwidth 0.9.1 reference.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, and RustSec passed; the ignored set fails as
expected and the controls pass.

## 2026-10-04 -- Retain bounded streaming text owners

Text owners retain up to seventeen source scalars. Ordinary leading marks and overflow
extensions start new one-cell owners instead of disappearing. Controls, cursor
movement, editing, hard breaks, and resize terminate extension; SGR preserves
it. Thai/Lao consonant plus SARA AM occupies a two-cell owner, including when
an arriving spacing sign moves the complete owner across the right edge.
Tone marks, Tibetan subjoined letters, and pre-base-vowel positions keep their
prior widths. Indic conjunct and emoji sequence widths remain separate work.

Snapshot format v5 preserves the extension boundary and pending wrap, caps
extension counts before allocation, and retains decoding for versions 1 through
4. Logical scalars survive history, reflow, copy, search, replay frames, and
plain/HTML export. Live cells grow from 44 to 92 bytes; scrollback cells remain
28 bytes with bounded extension sidecars. The snapshot-safe visible-grid
budget follows the larger maximum encoded cell size.
Unattached width-zero format controls and selectors occupy zero columns and
are still not retained. They extend source text when an eligible owner exists.

Validation: failing-before ownership fixtures cover deeper clusters, ordinary leading
marks, control boundaries, and Thai/Lao spacing ownership. Ordinary leading
marks previously disappeared; they now occupy one cell without an invented base. Locked core and integration checks cover overflow, byte splits, edits, migration, and export.
The local gate passes format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, RustSec, and orchestration checks.

## 2026-10-04 -- Test-only bidi rendering seam

The cell renderer can now draw a snapshot in bidi display order, through a
seam that only the test suite reaches. No setting, menu, or flag enables it,
and every shipped render path passes no display map, so on-screen output is
unchanged. `BidiDisplayMap` plans each run of soft-wrapped rows as one
paragraph with the headless `src/core/bidi` plans and gives every cell its
visual column, resolved level, and presentation character. Backgrounds,
glyphs, combining marks, decorations, contextual runs, and color glyphs all
draw at visual columns. A wide cell keeps its lead and continuation together,
and each glyph on a reordered row is clipped to its own visual cells. Rows
left in identity layout take the unchanged path.

Mirrored characters in right-to-left text present their Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph
counterpart, generated from Unicode 17.0.0 BidiMirroring.txt into
`src/core/bidi/mirroring.rs` by `scripts/unicode-bidi-data.py`. Characters
with no mirroring counterpart (U+2211, for example) draw unmirrored.
Contextual shaping runs split where the resolved level changes. Arabic
joining runs at a right-to-left level shape right to left. Each shaped glyph
now records its cluster's source cells, so a lam-alef ligature lands on the
visual cells of its lam and alef. Left-to-right ligatures are not formed
inside right-to-left text. The live shaper still shapes left to right in
logical order and decides overlays from glyph ids and source columns only.
The color-glyph builder and coverage-glyph quad helpers moved into
`src/grid/glyph_quads.rs` without behavior change. The test-only counter of cell
color resolutions is now per thread, so concurrently running render tests
cannot disturb the once-per-cell assertion.

Pixel fixtures use a new project-authored, reproducibly generated font,
`tests/fixtures/fonts/bidi-mixed.ttf`. Each mixed-direction line must match
the unchanged renderer drawing the hand-ordered visual string. Fixtures cover
Hebrew with Latin and digits, a mirrored bracket pair, wide cells beside and
inside right-to-left text, a ligature across a direction boundary, Arabic
lam-alef joining, and color glyphs. Byte-identical vertices are pinned for
left-to-right text through the seam. Eight of the eleven fixtures fail with
the display map ignored. Paragraphs that begin in scrollback resolve from
their visible rows only. Cursor, selection, pointer, and search mapping are
not part of this seam.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests,
production and documentation guards, RustSec, and the fixture-font and
Unicode data regeneration checks passed. The grid, ligature, and bidi tests
passed five runs at the default thread count.

## 2026-10-04 -- Bound Miri jobs and isolate snapshot write timing

Miri retains all six required filters and 49 diagnostic probes across ten
serialized jobs, each with at most six filters. Separate artifacts preserve
per-shard results within the 150-minute job budget. Orchestration checks cover
filter ownership, shard bounds, and unchanged failure classification.

The slow snapshot reader test starts its PTY-latency clock after the snapshot
header arrives, excluding capture and encoding from the socket-write deadline.
The deadline and latency assertions remain unchanged; deterministic ASCII
output replaces the random fixture, with payload-size and truncation checks.
The test runs on Unix; Windows has no runtime surface for this fixture.

Validation: format, Linux/Windows/macOS clippy, full locked tests, production
and documentation guards, RustSec, four orchestration tests, and five
default-thread admission runs passed. AddressSanitizer and ThreadSanitizer
confirmation of the admission change comes from the next Dynamic analysis
workflow run; no sanitizer result is claimed here.

## 2026-10-04 -- Unicode 17.0.0 bidi data and width-0 format controls

The headless bidi display plan in `src/core/bidi` now resolves levels from
Unicode 17.0.0 data, the same version as the width tables. Two new tables are
generated by `scripts/unicode-bidi-data.py`: `classes.rs` holds every
Bidi_Class other than L from DerivedBidiClass.txt, with its `@missing`
default ranges (R, AL, ET, and BN for unassigned code points) applied before
the explicit data lines, and `brackets.rs` holds the BidiBrackets.txt pairs
keyed to their canonical opening bracket. `unicode-bidi` reads both through a
`BidiDataSource` and is now built without its default features, so its
bundled Unicode 16.0.0 tables are not compiled at all.

The two data versions differ on 4,101 code points: 146 assigned characters
and 3,955 unassigned code points whose block default the bundled tables did
not apply (noncharacters and default ignorables are BN, unassigned Arabic
ranges are AL rather than R). Bracket pairs are identical. New tests pin
each kind of class change and show visual order changing where it matters: a
Unicode 17 symbol, a nonspacing mark, or a noncharacter between two Hebrew
letters now reverses with them. Those tests fail against the bundled
Unicode 16.0.0 tables. The full BidiCharacterTest.txt and BidiTest.txt files
still pass in their entirety with the new data.

The plan also gains the control metadata its contract named. A width-0 owner
made only of bidi format controls (embeddings, overrides, isolates, LRM, RLM,
and ALM) keeps its exact logical position, including at the start of a
paragraph or row, takes part in level resolution, and covers no visual
column: its visual span is empty and no column maps back to it. A width-0
owner holding anything else is malformed input and gets the identity layout.

Generated Rust data tables are allowed under `src/` only with a header naming
the generator, each source file and its SHA-256, and "Do not edit by hand";
the production-file guard documents this and measures them like any other
file. No dependency version changes, and both lockfiles are unchanged.
Nothing user-visible changes; bidirectional layout remains unsupported.

Local gate: cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the full single-threaded test suite (6,033 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), the production-file and documentation guards, the RustSec audit, and the full Unicode 17.0.0 conformance files (91,707 of 91,707 and 770,241 of 770,241) pass.

## 2026-10-04 -- Antialiased polygon fills for legacy diagonal blocks

Geometric glyph rendering gains a general antialiased polygon filler, and the
Symbols for Legacy Computing diagonal-edged blocks (`U+1FB3C..U+1FB67`) and
negative diagonals (`U+1FBBD..U+1FBBF`) now render from cell geometry when
`geometric_boxdraw` is on, instead of falling back to a font or a missing-glyph
box. These were the last recorded holes in the geometric legacy-symbol set.

The filler computes the exact area of a polygon inside each pixel by clipping
it to the pixel square, so edges are antialiased without supersampling, any
simple polygon works in either winding, and overlapping polygons clamp to one
fully inked pixel. Each diagonal block is the cell cut by one line between
anchors named in its Unicode character name (left, centre, right; upper,
upper middle, lower middle, lower), keeping the named corner's side. Each
lower fill and the upper fill 22 codepoints later share the same line, so
they tile into one solid cell. The negative diagonals are a fully inked cell
with light strokes cut out, at the same thickness as the box-drawing
diagonals, so `box_thickness` applies to them.

New tests check all 47 codepoints for bounded, non-empty, non-solid coverage
at five cell sizes from 6x12 to 18x36. They also check that complementary
pairs sum to full coverage within one unit, that mirrored neighbours match on
their shared edge, and that four glyphs match independently computed rational-area fixtures within eight
units (measured maximum difference: one). FNV-1a hashes of every previously
covered geometric glyph at the same five sizes (3,800 records, frozen before
this change) are unchanged. The four diagonal tests fail on the previous
renderer. The glyph corpus moves both ranges from its deferred-holes section to
a covered section. With `geometric_boxdraw` off, these codepoints use the font
path as before. Performance is unmeasured.

Local gate: cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the full single-threaded test suite (6,032 passed, 0 failed, 30 ignored), the production-file and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit pass.

## 2026-10-03 -- Headless bidi display plan with pinned UAX #9 conformance

A new core module, `src/core/bidi`, computes the Unicode Bidirectional
Algorithm layout of one wrapped logical line without changing any display,
input, selection, copy, or stored cell. The paragraph is the wrapped logical
line with its level forced to left to right. Levels are resolved once per
paragraph, then the line rule and reordering run on each physical row, so a
cell never moves to another row. Owners (a base scalar with its attached
marks, a blank, or a wide pair) move as one unit, and a wide owner keeps its
two cells adjacent with the lead first. The result is an immutable plan with
per-owner levels, widths, and mirrored-glyph flags, plus reversible
owner-to-visual-span and visual-column-to-owner maps. Text that cannot
resolve to a non-zero level skips all bidi work. Paragraphs over 16,384
owners, 262,144 bytes of owner text, or 16,384 rows, and malformed input, get
the complete identity layout rather than a partly reordered one. Each cap is
tested at its exact boundary. Performance is unmeasured.

Conformance is pinned to Unicode 17.0.0. The full BidiCharacterTest.txt
(91,707 cases) and BidiTest.txt (770,241 cases across paragraph directions)
pass in their entirety. A strided subset of each (2,894 and 12,022 cases) is
committed under the Unicode License v3 and asserted case-for-case in the
regular suite. Left-to-right cases run through the public plan; right-to-left
and auto-direction cases exercise the same level resolution, line rule, and
reordering directly. Rules L3 and L4 are outside both files: marks never leave
their owner, and mirroring is reported as a flag. Bidi_Mirrored data is
generated from Unicode 17.0.0 by `scripts/unicode-bidi-data.py`.

New dependency: `unicode-bidi` 0.3.18 (MIT OR Apache-2.0, no transitive
dependencies), chosen for maintained UAX #9 level resolution rather than a
second hand-written implementation. It bundles Unicode 16.0.0 bidi classes
while OdyTTY's width tables are Unicode 17.0.0, so characters assigned or
reclassified in Unicode 17 resolve with their Unicode 16 class until a Unicode
17 class source is supplied. Both lockfiles are updated and the RustSec audit
passes. Nothing user-visible changes; bidirectional layout remains
unsupported.

Local gate: cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the full single-threaded test suite (0 failed), the production-file and documentation guards, and the RustSec audit pass.

## 2026-10-03 -- Wide-glyph padding preserves logical text

Wide-owner wrap padding now carries provenance in live cells, narrow history
storage, and snapshot format v4. Eager and lazy resize discard old generated
padding before wrapping again, so a wide character at the right edge no longer
injects a space into logical search, selection copy, or scrollback export.
Typed spaces at soft-wrap boundaries remain in user copy. Snapshot formats
v1 through v3 remain readable and restore their blanks as ordinary source
spaces. Format v4 uses the existing ownership byte and rejects malformed
padding before it enters the terminal model. Overwrite, erase, character
shifts, and rectangle copies retain explicit source-text behavior. Scalar
widths and the four-mark limit are unchanged. The core behavior is shared by
Linux Wayland, Linux X11, macOS, and Windows.

Failing-before tests cover the four-column resize/search defect and lost typed
spaces in selection copy. Further tests cover history projection, repeated
resize, snapshot encode/decode, both copy paths, plain and HTML export, and
edit boundaries. Local validation covers cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows
and macOS clippy, the single-threaded test suite, production-file and
documentation guards, and both-lockfile RustSec audit.

## 2026-10-03 -- Logical text ownership regression fixtures

Twelve headless Unicode fixtures in `tests/text_invariants.rs` pin the
logical-text contract that later grapheme-width and bidirectional work must
preserve: copy and cursor addressing stay in logical source order for mixed
Latin, Hebrew, Arabic, and digits; attached marks keep arrival order without
advancing the cursor; UTF-8 read boundaries do not change cells; wide cells
and their marks wrap together; pending-wrap marks attach before the next base
wraps; search crosses soft wraps but not hard breaks; reflow keeps marks and
logical search; rectangular copy keeps logical column bands; snapshot
round-trips keep cells and cursor; alternate-screen edits leave primary text
intact; and pixel hit testing keeps exact cell edges at several scales. The
fixtures are project-authored escape sequences and need no fonts, PTY, or GPU,
so they run identically on Linux, macOS, and Windows. No behavior changes.

Local gate: cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the full
single-threaded test suite (0 failed), the production-file and documentation
guards, and the RustSec audit pass.

## 2026-10-03 -- Argument launches keep the saved workspace layout

A launch with any command-line argument skipped workspace restore but still
owned the shape autosave, so the argument session replaced the saved layout on
its first structural change or on quit, and the next bare launch restored the
wrong layout. Ownership of the saved shape now requires both the instance lock
and a bare launch. A process launched with arguments writes no snapshot for
its whole lifetime: not the debounced autosave, the cwd checkpoint, a profile
binding edit, nor the clean-exit save, and no later window inherits ownership
through a close, merge, or move. It still holds the instance lock, so a
concurrent bare launch remains a secondary window, and it raises no
secondary-instance notice. Linux, macOS, and Windows share the rule and the
persistence path.

Local gate: cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the full single-threaded test suite (0 failed), and the production-file and documentation guards pass.

## 2026-10-03 -- Broadcast label for a set holding only the focused pane

A broadcast set whose only member is the focused pane showed `BROADCAST 1`,
which reads as one other pane receiving input when nothing fans out. That pane
now shows `BROADCAST this pane only` (compact `BC self` on narrow panes), and
the multi-line paste confirmation reads `Broadcast to this pane only.`; the
confirmation still opens exactly as before. A single receiver that is another
pane keeps `BROADCAST 1` and `RECV`. The self-only state joins the render
signature, so moving focus or changing membership repaints the label at once.
Platform-neutral: Linux (Wayland and X11), macOS, and Windows share the
change.

Local gate: cargo fmt --check, Linux, Windows and macOS clippy, the full single-threaded test suite (0 failed), and the production-file and documentation guards pass.
