## 2026-10-02 -- Release v0.16.0 -- Safer pane and window workflows

- Version 0.16.0 adds read-only panes, bounded text and sanitized HTML
  scrollback export, guarded broadcast input with explicit receivers and
  multiline confirmation, tab and pane movement through menus and a picker,
  stacked and floating layouts, an East Asian Ambiguous width preference,
  and explicit macOS secure keyboard input. Windows first-shell launch waits
  for the live ConPTY grid, with a five-second degraded backstop, and
  Ctrl+Shift+N no longer inserts a literal letter in the new window.
- The source version and both lockfiles are 0.16.0. Publication markers,
  the completed TODO milestone, shipped roadmap checkpoints, AppStream,
  the release guide, and final release notes agree. Feature guides no longer
  label these additions as development-only.
- Hands-on results retain their platform scope: read-only on owned headless
  Linux Wayland; text and HTML export on Linux with Firefox and macOS with
  Safari; secure-input toggle, single-pane label, and focus release on macOS;
  tab and pane movement on Linux Wayland, macOS, and Windows. Ten native
  Hyprland/NVIDIA sibling-window create/destroy cycles ran with the session
  locked. Multi-pane secure-input labels have automated test coverage.
- Designed limits remain explicit: no drag tear-out, no pointer drag of
  floating frames, focused-pane keyboard encoding for broadcast receivers,
  and a Windows backstop that can start before a later resize retry. Terminal
  text in an export is not redacted. Artifact, checksum, alias/pinned-pair,
  install/upgrade, and package-channel verification is recorded after
  publication; source validation does not establish those results.

## 2026-10-02 -- Right-click menu rows for window, layout, and export actions

- The v0.16 window and pane actions that were reachable only from the
  command palette now have right-click menu rows. The tab menu offers Move
  Tab to New Window and Move Tab to Window... for the right-clicked tab. The
  terminal menu offers Move Pane to New Window, Move Pane to Window..., Export
  Scrollback As Text and As HTML, Stack, Float, and Tile Panes, and Arrange
  Floating Pane. The empty tab-strip menu offers Merge This Window Into... and
  Pull Window Into This One....
- Each row runs the palette row of the same name through the one palette
  handler and is offered exactly when that palette row is: Move to New Window
  needs content left behind, the Window... and merge rows need another
  window, the pane rows need a split tab, the layout rows need two or more
  panes and hide the arrangement already in force, and Arrange Floating Pane
  shows only in a floating tab. The quick terminal offers no Window... or
  merge rows. A tab-menu move makes the right-clicked tab active first, as a
  click on it would; cancelling the window picker leaves it active. The
  window, layout, and move facts are part of the menu render signature.
- Tests drive the real right-click path for presence and absence per
  condition, dispatch parity with the palette, and the inactive-tab target;
  four of them fail without the change. Menu-geometry tests moved with the
  two export rows now in the single-pane reference layout. Full local gate
  passes, and the menu tests pass five times at default thread count.

## 2026-10-02 -- Tests for layout version skew and broadcast key encoding

- A layout saved before stacked and floating layouts existed (no
  arrangement field) restores every pane tiled with the original split
  geometry.
- Unknown fields in a saved layout are ignored, but they do not let a newer
  snapshot version past the version-skew rejection.
- Broadcast copies the focused pane's encoded key bytes to every receiver: a
  receiver that requested the Kitty keyboard protocol or Win32 input mode
  gets the same bytes as a legacy origin, and the Kitty encoding when the
  origin requests it. Test-only change; full local gate passes and the
  broadcast tests pass five times at default thread count.

## 2026-10-02 -- v0.16.0 notes describe shipped behavior and its limits

- The v0.16.0 release notes no longer carry a draft banner, a per-platform
  acceptance record, or lists of checks not yet run. They state what ships
  and its designed limits: no drag tear-out on any platform (the command
  palette and the merge picker move a tab or pane, and on Wayland the
  application cannot place the new window), the bottom-left click hint only
  in single-pane tabs, and the macOS secure-input crash-until-logout
  behavior as a property of the OS primitive.
- Corrections: profile and font discovery bounds are stated separately;
  export's exclusion of application metadata is distinguished from the
  terminal text itself, which is exported unredacted; the five-second
  held-launch backstop on Windows and its resize-retry consequence are
  described; the Windows Ctrl+Shift+N leftover-letter fix is recorded; and
  read-only panes, broadcast, and the in-window layouts are described as one
  implementation serving every platform rather than as verified everywhere.
  The roadmap describes tab and pane movement through the palette and picker,
  with drag as a possible later addition. TODO ticks the v0.16.0 code items
  whose commits passed three-OS CI and the device items that have hands-on
  results so far.
- Documentation guard and production-file guard pass. No code change.

## 2026-10-02 -- Open-modifier hover underline repaints on Cmd and on bare URLs

- The armed underline under a hovered path or bare URL only repainted when
  Ctrl changed over a hovered path. On macOS the open modifier is Cmd, so
  pressing Cmd over a path left the underline undrawn until some other output
  redrew the window, in split and single-pane tabs alike. On Linux and
  Windows, Ctrl over a hovered bare URL had the same delay.
- The modifier handler now compares the platform open modifier (Cmd on
  macOS, Ctrl on Linux and Windows) before and after each change and
  invalidates when an enabled path or URL is hovered, using the same hover
  predicate the painter uses. A modifier that is not the open modifier on
  that OS, a disabled feature, or no hover still changes nothing.
- Tests drive the handler for the Ctrl and Cmd choices on one host, over a
  path and a URL, in split and single-pane tabs, plus the wrong-platform,
  feature-off, and no-hover controls; four of them fail with the previous
  Ctrl-and-path-only gate. A forwarding test that assumed Ctrl now uses the
  host open modifier, so it also holds on the macOS runner. Full local gate
  passes (fmt, clippy on Linux and the Windows and macOS targets, tests with
  one thread, file and documentation guards, RustSec); the changed tests also
  pass five times at default thread count.

## 2026-10-02 -- AppImage smoke script passes shellcheck on the CI runner

- `dist/appimage/smoke-test.sh` used `a && b || die` for its usage check,
  which the runner's shellcheck reports as SC2015, so the shell-scripts CI
  job failed. The check is now an explicit `if`. shellcheck 0.9.0 and 0.11
  pass on the AppImage scripts and the install scripts, and the audit mode
  and usage error still behave the same. No Rust change.

## 2026-10-02 -- Secure-input label and path underline paint in multi-pane tabs

- On macOS, a split, stacked, or floating tab showed no `SECURE INPUT` label
  while the mode was held; only the single-pane frame path painted it. The
  protection itself was unaffected. The multi-pane rebuild now paints the
  label on the focused pane only, in the first free run of its top row so it
  sits beside the arrange, read-only, and broadcast labels instead of
  covering one. With no free run it moves to the second row; on a one-row
  pane it replaces the start of the top row. On an empty top row it lands
  exactly where the single-pane label does.
- The Ctrl+hover path underline is now drawn in multi-pane tabs on the
  focused pane, the only pane that maps the pointer. The bottom-left click
  hint still appears only in single-pane tabs: its expiry tick, like the open
  notice and the bell flash, runs only on the single-pane frame path, so the
  limitation is documented instead.
- New tests drive the move and merge pickers from the palette action on a
  split origin through the multi-window host: every visible pane of the
  source shows the banner, every pane of a split destination shows the
  numeral, Esc clears both windows, and a selection leaves no chrome. Other
  new tests cover the secure-input label in tiled, stacked, and floating
  tabs, focus changes, window focus loss, the label beside arrange and
  read-only, its absence on Linux and Windows, and the underline on the
  focused pane only. Eight of them fail with the three multi-pane paints
  removed.
- Full local gate passed (fmt, clippy for Linux, Windows, and macOS, 5741
  tests, file and doc guards, RustSec), and the multi-pane, secure-input,
  and multi-window tests passed five times at the default thread count.
- Open: a macOS hands-on check of the split-tab label.

## 2026-10-02 -- Move picker banner paints in split, stacked, and floating tabs

- When a pane or tab was moved out of a window whose active tab had more

- The multi-pane rebuild now paints the origin banner and the candidate

## 2026-10-02 -- AppImage bundles the X11 libraries the window layer loads

- The AppImage panicked at startup on an X11 host without
  `libxkbcommon-x11` ("Library libxkbcommon-x11.so could not be loaded").
  winit loads that library, Xcursor, and XInput with dlopen, and linuxdeploy
  follows only ELF NEEDED entries, so none of them were bundled.
- `build-appimage.sh` now deploys `libxkbcommon-x11.so.0`, `libXcursor.so.1`,
  and `libXi.so.6` explicitly and fails the build when one is missing.
  The core `libxkbcommon.so.0` is excluded and stays host-provided, so an
  older bundled copy never overrides a newer host copy.
- New `dist/appimage/smoke-test.sh`: `audit` classifies every library name
  in the shipped binary as bundled or host-provided and fails on an
  unclassified name; `run` starts the AppImage under Xvfb on a host that
  lacks every bundled library. The release workflow runs both, the second
  in a clean `ubuntu:22.04` container. CI shellchecks the scripts.
- The `.deb`, `.rpm`, and AUR packages declare `libxkbcommon-x11`, and
  `docs/install.md` lists the runtime libraries for the tarball.
- Verified locally in Ubuntu 22.04 containers: the v0.15.8 AppImage fails
  both checks and the rebuilt one passes them. An emulation of the AppImage
  catalog's window test (no Vulkan loader, Xvfb, firejail) fails v0.15.8 with
  the same panic and passes the rebuilt image on the OpenGL fallback. Full
  local gate passed (fmt, clippy for Linux, Windows, and macOS, 5726 tests,
  file and doc guards, RustSec). The release-workflow steps themselves have
  not yet run on GitHub.

## 2026-10-02 -- Secure keyboard input: hotkey apps stop while it is held

- macOS acceptance of secure keyboard input passed: the hold follows the
  toggle and window focus, a new window adopts the wish in force, a toggle
  from any window releases every hold, and quitting releases it.
- A remapped quit chord from an event-tap hotkey app stopped working while
  the hold was active. Terminal.app with Secure Keyboard Entry behaves the
  same way, and a real menu shortcut still worked there, so this is the
  intended effect of the macOS primitive rather than an OdyTTY defect. The
  feature notes and specification now name hotkey and key-remapping apps
  explicitly. Documentation only.

## 2026-10-02 -- Release v0.15.8 -- macOS idle CPU fix

Version 0.15.8 is a patch release from the `release/0.15.8` branch of
v0.15.7. The v0.16.0 development tree is not part of it.

On macOS, an untouched window's idle CPU returns to about zero. The cause
was a per-tick clone of the event-loop proxy inside `about_to_wait`, which
re-woke the macOS run loop. The clone has been present since v0.15.0. The
tick path no longer clones the proxy. On Linux and Windows, proxy clones
are cheap and showed no spin. The per-tick clone is removed there too. This
release makes no idle-CPU claim for those platforms, and no
rendering-throughput, startup, or memory claim. The v0.12.0 results remain
the applicable comparative evidence.

Publication checks for tag v0.15.8: SHA256SUMS verified for all 15 files,
and the seven always-latest aliases match their version-pinned copies byte
for byte. The minisign signature verifies with the key in `docs/install.md`.
Attestations verify for the AppImage, the Windows zip, and the macOS zip.
The Scoop manifest on master is v0.15.8. The Homebrew cask 0.15.8 sha256
`da0e88211b78f54eda1a5354f054e6c53e3f67cb00f38edc90c541cf711a71ea` matches
the macOS zip. The Homebrew formula sha256
`649f5b066d4c2e7880a1b2312db29cda2f6f71bacf283b415d91715f0bb1ed57` matches
the source tarball. AUR `odytty` is 0.15.8-1.

macOS idle-CPU check of the v0.15.8 source tag: a release build of tag
v0.15.8 on an Apple M4 Mac, left untouched after launch, settled from the
startup frames to 0.0% CPU within five seconds and stayed there for the
rest of the sampled minute.

## 2026-10-02 -- Closing the primary window keeps workspace autosave

- Closing the window that owns the saved workspace layout while other
  windows stayed open left no owner: autosave and the clean-exit save
  stopped for the rest of the process, so the next launch restored the
  layout from before the close.
- The oldest remaining ordinary window now takes ownership and arms one
  save of its own layout. The quick terminal never takes it, and closing a
  window that does not own the layout changes nothing. Merging windows and
  moving the last tab out of the owning window already transferred
  ownership; the ordinary close path was the missing sibling.
- Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded tests (library 5713 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored), guards,
  and RustSec passed; the multi-window host tests also passed five times
  under the default parallel test runner.

## 2026-10-02 -- Freeze watchdog: hidden windows on the slow retry are not stalls

- macOS logs showed the freeze watchdog reporting a stall for an unfocused
  window whose renderer had spent its fast skipped-frame retries: skipped
  frames rose about once a second, every retry delivered a redraw, and the
  windowing system presented nothing. That is a surface the system is not
  showing, not a frozen render path.
- An unfocused or occluded (not minimized) window on the slow retry whose
  delivered-redraw count keeps advancing now gets one rate-limited `info`
  record starting `window in skipped-frame slow retry` instead of the stall
  warning. If the redraw count stops advancing for six seconds, the retry
  timer itself is dead and the normal stall record applies. A focused,
  visible window on the slow retry still reports a stall.
- The float-arrange modal now has a name in watchdog records instead of
  `unknown`.
- The classification is platform-independent logic; it was observed on
  macOS only. Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded tests (library 5719 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored), guards,
  and RustSec passed; the watchdog tests also passed five times under the
  default parallel test runner.

## 2026-10-02 -- Cross-cutting tests for broadcast and floating layouts

- A broadcast set that mixes a local pane, a hidden pane, and a remote pane
  with bracketed paste on delivers a typed key to all three, asks for
  confirmation before any multiline paste is written, and then encodes the
  paste for each receiver's own paste mode.
- A receiver hidden by a stacked layout still receives input, and a
  receiver made read-only while a paste confirmation is open receives
  nothing when the paste is confirmed; it stays in the broadcast set.
- A saved layout with floating geometry under a foreign schema version is
  rejected before restore instead of being applied best-effort.
- Tests only; no behavior change. Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS
  cross-target clippy, single-threaded tests (library 5701 passed, 0
  failed, 16 ignored), and guards passed; the new tests also passed five
  times under the default parallel test runner.

## 2026-10-02 -- Secure keyboard input on macOS, revised

- Secure keyboard input returns with the defects that withdrew it fixed.
  The first window establishes the process-wide wish from its settings; a
  window created later adopts the wish already in force instead of writing
  its file configuration over it, so toggling in a new window turns secure
  input off for every window. Config reload and the palette toggle still
  set the wish for all windows.
- A hold released while a window is unfocused bumps that window's frame
  signature and requests a presenting frame, so the `SECURE INPUT` label
  clears instead of staying on the last frame.
- Tests that touch the wish, the hold counter, or the test primitive share
  one re-entrant lock, and window construction takes it as well, so the
  default parallel test runner cannot interleave them. The palette ranking
  test uses a query that the macOS-only action does not match.
- Behavior on Windows and Linux is unchanged from the withdrawn version:
  the row and palette action are absent and a config value is kept but not
  applied. macOS hands-on for the new-window path is pending.
- Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded tests (library 5710 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored), file
  and doc guards, and RustSec passed; the secure-input, palette, and
  multi-window wish tests also passed five times under 32 test threads.

## 2026-10-02 -- Secure keyboard input withdrawn for rework

- The secure keyboard input change is reverted. Continuous integration
  failed on all three platforms: its tests share process-wide state (the
  wish and the hold counter) and failed under the default parallel test
  runner on Linux and Windows, and on macOS the new palette action changed
  an existing palette ranking expectation. The local gate runs tests on one
  thread and did not see the first failure.
- macOS device testing also found a defect: opening a second window reset
  the process-wide wish from that window's file configuration, so toggling
  in the new window turned secure input on instead of off, and the first
  window kept its label.
- The feature returns as one revision with isolated test state, the palette
  expectation updated, a new window adopting the current wish, and the label
  clearing on a release while unfocused. Master returns to the previous
  behavior: no secure-input setting on any platform.

## 2026-10-02 -- Secure keyboard input on macOS

- **Toggle Secure Keyboard Input** (palette) and the **Secure keyboard
  input** settings row (`secure_keyboard_input`, off by default) set one
  process-wide wish on macOS. `EnableSecureEventInput` runs only while the
  wish is on and an OdyTTY window has keyboard focus; a process counter
  pairs it with `DisableSecureEventInput` when focus leaves every window,
  the last window closes, the wish is turned off from any window, or the
  process exits. There is no default chord and no password-prompt
  detection.
- Chosen: hold the primitive only while focused, as Terminal.app does,
  because while it is enabled macOS withholds keystrokes from every
  keyboard-intercept tool (event taps such as text expanders, and some
  accessibility and automation tools), even when OdyTTY is in the
  background. Other apps still receive keys. A crash while it is enabled
  can leave secure input reported as active until logout.
- Each holding window shows a `SECURE INPUT` label, part of the frame
  cache signature; the label blanks the spacer of a wide glyph it cuts.
  Windows and Linux have no equivalent primitive: the row and the palette
  action are absent and a config value is kept but not applied.
- Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded tests (library 5707 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored), file
  and doc guards, and RustSec all passed on the parent commit; the change
  applies to the current tree with only line offsets moved. macOS hands-on
  is pending.

## 2026-10-02 -- macOS idle busy loop in the event loop

- Device testing on an Apple M4 Mac found an untouched OdyTTY window using
  a full CPU core from launch, and the installed v0.15.7 app showed the same.
  A stack sample put the main thread in the event loop's idle hook, inside
  `EventLoopProxy::clone`. In winit 0.30 on macOS, cloning a proxy adds a
  run-loop source and wakes the run loop. The local automation service
  cloned its proxy on every idle tick, even with automation disabled (the
  default), so each tick scheduled the next one. The code has been present
  since v0.15.0.
- The automation reconcile now takes a factory for its wake callback and
  builds it, with its proxy clone, only when an endpoint actually starts.
  Every host proxy clone goes through one helper; the remaining clones are
  one-shot (shortcut registration, Wayland file-drop listener) or
  event-driven. A test runs 32 idle ticks and requires zero proxy clones; it
  fails before the change (32 clones). Linux and Windows proxy clones are
  cheap and showed no spin, but the per-tick clone is removed there too.
- No performance claim beyond the macOS idle check. Gate: fmt, Linux plus
  Windows and macOS cross-target clippy, single-threaded tests (library 5700
  passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored), file and doc guards, and RustSec all
  passed. macOS before/after idle check on the same machine is pending.

## 2026-10-02 -- Broadcast fan-out diagnostics

- Device testing found a pane created by a split inside a broadcast group
  showing `BROADCAST 2` while a typed character reached only that pane. The
  same sequence driven through the App key path delivers to every receiver,
  so the difference is not yet attributed.
- Under the existing `ODYTTY_KEY_EVENT_DIAGNOSTICS` gate, every input,
  IME commit, or paste that reaches broadcast fan-out now records one
  `broadcast-fanout` line: payload kind and byte count, the focused pane's
  numeric token, and each receiver's token with its outcome (`delivered`,
  `skipped-focused`, `skipped-read-only`, `skipped-unresolved`,
  `queued-other-window`, `write-failed`, `refused-too-large`, or `pruned`).
  An empty set logs `receivers=0`; no line at all means the input never
  reached fan-out. Typed bytes, text, titles, and paths are never recorded,
  and nothing is logged while the gate is off. Broadcast behavior is
  unchanged.
- Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded tests (library 5698 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored), file
  and doc guards, and RustSec all passed.

## 2026-10-02 -- Stacked and floating pane layouts

- A tab keeps its split tree as the single record of which panes exist and
  adds an arrangement beside it: tiled (the default, and the only mode older
  layouts know), stacked, or floating. **Stack Panes**, **Float Panes**, and
  **Tile Panes** are palette actions; no default chord was added. Switching
  mode never restarts a pane, and Tile returns the exact tiled geometry
  because the tree is never discarded.
- Stacked shows the focused pane over the whole content area while the other
  panes keep running at their tiled size. Floating panes are whole-cell
  rectangles with a z-order, a frame, an 8 x 2 cell minimum, and a clamp at
  resolution time, so a smaller window never deletes a pane. The focused pane
  is always painted on top; focus cycling stays in stable tree order so every
  pane is reachable, and **Focus Pane k of n** palette rows list them.
- **Arrange Floating Pane** moves (arrows) and resizes (Shift+arrows) the
  focused floating pane from the keyboard. Overlapping panes are cut around
  the panes painted above them for backgrounds, glyphs, and overlays. Known
  limits: pointer drag of a floating frame is not implemented, and a covered
  floating pane draws no inline images until it is uncovered.
- Persistence writes an `arrangement` object only for non-tiled tabs, so
  tiled layout files are byte-identical. Malformed or partial entries load
  tiled or as an unplaced pane, never as a partial rectangle; restore is
  deterministic. A pane moved from another window joins the destination tab
  as a floating pane only when that tab is floating.
- New feature, no performance claim. Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS
  cross-target clippy, single-threaded tests (library 5695 passed, 0 failed,
  16 ignored), file and doc guards, and RustSec all passed. Device acceptance
  on Linux, macOS, and Windows is pending.

## 2026-10-01 -- New Window chord letter and sibling hold release

- Windows device testing at 49244543 still showed the `n` of
  `Ctrl+Shift+N` in the new window's prompt, removed about five seconds
  later. Two causes. First, the new window already has focus when the
  key-up arrives, so the bare press that follows it was encoded as
  ordinary typing; a modifier press and a one-character IME commit after a
  same-letter pre-edit also escaped the latch. A window that inherits the
  latch now drops its first bare press of that letter whether it arrives
  before or after the key-up, keeps the latch across modifier presses, and
  swallows that IME commit. The window that handled the chord still treats
  a press after its key-up as typing, and a different character ends the
  latch.
- Second, the new window's surface was created before the window was
  registered with the host, so the creation resize was dropped and the held
  ConPTY shell waited for the five-second fallback. The window is now
  registered first, and surface creation applies the window's current
  non-zero size, so the held shell starts on the window's grid. A 0x0
  (minimized) window still waits for the fallback, which remains.
- Bound: each new window drops at most one press of the chord letter. Linux
  and macOS have no ConPTY hold. Gate: fmt, Linux plus Windows and macOS
  cross-target clippy, single-threaded tests (library 5637 passed, 0
  failed, 16 ignored), file and doc guards, and RustSec all passed. Windows
  hands-on retest is pending.

## 2026-10-01 -- Cross-cutting tests for landed v0.16.0 surfaces

- Eight tests pin behavior across features that landed separately: a
  released surface keeps device loss sticky while a sibling window's timers
  stay independent; a three-window merge preserves the survivors with one
  snapshot owner; the close policy exits only after the last live window; a
  legacy nested layout restores writable and a foreign schema is not
  restored; a mixed image-and-link export document stays inert; multibyte
  text and image markup are bounded by encoded byte limits; a 32 MiB export
  refusal leaves the existing export file unchanged; and a hidden tab keeps
  its read-only policy after writable input and export elsewhere.
- Deferred until their features land: broadcast and reparenting cross-cuts,
  ordinary primary-close handoff, and floating/stacked layout restoration.
- Verification: fmt, Linux plus Windows/macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded `cargo test` (5611 library tests passed, 16 ignored), the
  guards, and both RustSec audits pass.

## 2026-10-01 -- Ambiguous character width preference

- `ambiguous_width` (`ODYTTY_AMBIGUOUS_WIDTH`, profile `appearance.ambiguous_width`)
  chooses how East Asian Ambiguous characters are measured: `narrow` (the
  default, today's width table, one column) or `wide` (two columns, using
  the width crate's East Asian context table). A pane follows the profile
  that launched it; a missing profile value uses the global setting.
- Changing the value reflows that pane's primary grid and scrollback in
  place through the existing resize path. The PTY is not resized and the
  shell is not told, so a prompt drawn under the old width stays as drawn
  until the shell repaints it; a selection or search hit can land on
  different cells after the remeasure. An active alternate screen is left
  for the application to repaint. Width policy is part of the projection
  cache key, so a same-width policy change never reuses old row counts.
- Overlay chrome, the tab bar, the search status line, and the glyph atlas
  stay on the narrow table: a shared atlas cannot cache two slot widths for
  one codepoint while two panes disagree, and the grid's continuation cell
  reserves the second column. IME pre-edit follows the pane's policy.
- Verification: fmt, Linux plus Windows/macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded `cargo test` (5610 library tests passed, 16 ignored), the
  guards, and both RustSec audits pass; width conformance and the new
  ambiguous-width tests cover both tables and the reflow. Hands-on Linux
  acceptance is pending.

## 2026-10-01 -- Move tabs and panes between windows

- A tab or a pane can move to another window of the same process, or into a
  window of its own, from the command palette: Move Tab to New Window, Move
  Tab to Window..., Move Pane to New Window, Move Pane to Window.... The "to
  Window..." rows reuse the merge picker: each other window shows a numeral
  and "Press N to move here". Rows appear only when they can work (a sibling
  window exists, something stays behind, the tab is split).
- The moved shell keeps running. Its PTY or attached session, writer, pump,
  terminal model, scrollback, images, selection, search, recorder, profile
  binding, and `--hold` state move as owned values between the two workspace
  arenas; nothing is respawned, detached, or replayed. A moved tab keeps its
  panes and layout; a moved pane becomes the only pane of a new tab whose
  identity is minted from the destination's token range, and the pane it
  leaves behind takes over its split.
- The move is a transaction: a preflight checks token collisions and
  capacity without mutating either window; a refused destination or a new
  window that cannot open restores the tab index, workspace, pane tree,
  focus, and zoom exactly, with a notice. Before a pane leaves, the source
  settles the state that names it (focus-out report, pending paste and image
  confirmations, IME composition, context-menu target, open overlay, drags)
  and afterwards prunes OSC 52 consents and reflows. The destination drops
  its presentation caches, parks timers, applies its presentation policy,
  activates the tab, and reflows.
- Moving a window's last tab retires that window, and the primary autosave
  role moves to the destination. The quick terminal neither sends nor
  receives. Attached host-session ids are shared across windows, so a session
  attached in another window is refused with a notice rather than attached
  twice.
- Drag tear-out is not supported: on Wayland the compositor owns placement
  and winit reports no cross-window drag, so the palette is the route there
  and on every other platform.
- Chosen, and reversible: palette rows only (no context-menu rows yet); a
  window's only tab does not move to a new window; a moved pane keeps its own
  recorder state; an open command-export save dialog stays with the window
  that opened it while the export follows the pane.
- Verification: fmt, Linux plus Windows/macOS cross-target clippy,
  single-threaded `cargo test` (5617 library tests passed, 0 failed), the
  guards, and both RustSec audits pass; 5 model tests and 9 host/App tests
  cover the picker move, last-tab retire with primary handoff, pane to a new
  window with hold, IME, and focus-out, exact restore when the new window
  cannot open, only-tab and quick-terminal refusals, cross-window attach
  dedup, and the palette rows. Hands-on acceptance on Linux (including NVIDIA
  window create and destroy), macOS, and Windows is pending.
